- Congress reunion (MOD-3006).
- Congress–League scheme (MOD-3007).
- Concession: separate electorates and weightage (✅ owner 18).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** December 1916 (✅ owner 18; ✅ Wikipedia: Lucknow Pact).
**Links:** MOD-3006, MOD-3007, MOD-3008.
**Trap:** "Lucknow abolished communal politics" is wrong; it accepted communal electorates.
**Mains hook:** Critically examine Lucknow as a high point of unity and a source of communal complications (owner 18 Mains).
**Back:** *"Lucknow: two unities, one communal price."*

### Flashcard MOD-3018 — Verdict scaffolds for WWI, Home Rule and Lucknow
**Front:** What are the owner's verdict lines for war, Home Rule, Lucknow and the 1919 Act?
**Definition (learn):** Ready-made qualified verdict sentences for 10/15/20-mark answers.
**Breakdown:**
- War: "The First World War did not create Indian nationalism; it removed the arguments with which Britain had been postponing it." (✅ owner 18).
- Home Rule: "The Leagues built the roads on which Gandhian mass politics travelled" (✅ owner 18).
- Lucknow: "The Pact solved nationalist division by institutionalising the problem of communal representation" (✅ owner 18).
- 1919: "Dyarchy failed ... because they were given the departments that could not succeed" (✅ owner 18).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner verdict lines (✅ owner 18).
**Links:** MOD-3001, MOD-3012, MOD-3017.
**Trap:** Do not present the verdicts as settled facts; they are argument scaffolds.
**Mains hook:** Use one thesis per answer; the four options are contradiction, organisational bridge, designed-to-fail and ambivalent pact.
**Back:** *"War removes arguments; Leagues lay roads; Lucknow trades division for communalism; dyarchy starves its ministers."*

### Flashcard MOD-3019 — PYQ routes: WWI, Home Rule and 1919 reforms
**Front:** Which verified PYQs route to the material in owner 18, and what is the key status?
**Definition (learn):** Prelims and Mains routes connected to this owner, with key status as recorded in the owner.
**Breakdown:**
- 2018 Prelims GS-I Q79: "All India Home Rule League renamed Swarajya Sabha 1920" (key unavailable locally, ✅ owner 18). Route: MOD-3015.
- 2021 Prelims GS-I Q42: women political rights, Montagu-Chelmsford, GoI Act 1935 (key unavailable locally, ✅ owner 18). Route: MOD-3013.
- 2022 Prelims GS-I Q51: GoI Act 1919 reserved and transferred subjects (key unavailable locally, ✅ owner 18). Route: MOD-3011.
- 2026 Prelims GS-I Q18: Montagu-Chelmsford, separate electorates, community alliances (provisional 2026 Set-A key present; no answer letter recorded, ✅ owner 18). Route: MOD-3008.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PYQ demand status as stated; no answer letters inferred.
**Links:** MOD-3011, MOD-3013, MOD-3015, MOD-3008.
**Trap:** Do not assume an option letter; the owner does not record one for these routes.
**Mains hook:** Use the 1919 and 1935 reform pair to answer "continuity or advance" demands.
**Back:** *"2018 renamed, 2021 women, 2022 subjects, 2026 electorates: four routes, no keys assumed."*

### GROUP — 19 Gandhi's Rise, Rowlatt Act and Jallianwala Bagh

### Flashcard MOD-3020 — Gandhi's rise: method first, then campaigns, then the 1919 rupture
**Front:** How did Gandhi move from a South African method to a national leader by 1919?
**Definition (learn):** A sequence: satyagraha tested in South Africa (1893–1914) → return and mentorship under Gokhale (1915) → three local campaigns (Champaran 1917, Ahmedabad and Kheda 1918) → all-India agitation against the Rowlatt Act and the Jallianwala Bagh shock (1919).
**Breakdown:**
- Method: satyagraha in South Africa (✅ owner 19).
- Mentor: Gokhale (✅ owner 19).
- Credibility: three limited, winnable local campaigns (✅ owner 19, analytical).
- Turning point: Rowlatt and Jallianwala (✅ owner 19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1893–1914; 1915; 1917; 1918; 1919 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** MOD-3021, MOD-3022, MOD-3035 (chronology ladder), MOD-3037 (leadership verdict).
**Trap:** The early campaigns were local and narrow; they are not an all-India mass movement.
**Mains hook:** Gandhi became indispensable by 1920 because he had shown, in three settings, that his method produced results and that he would pay its price.
**Back:** *"South Africa forged it, Gokhale launched it, three local wins proved it, 1919 made it national."*

### Flashcard MOD-3021 — Satyagraha: origin in South Africa
**Front:** Where and when was satyagraha developed, and what did it mean?
**Definition (learn):** Satyagraha is a method of non-violent resistance based on truth-force and willingness to suffer, developed by Gandhi in South Africa between 1893 and 1914.
**Breakdown:**
- Organisation: Natal Indian Congress (✅ owner 19).
- Communities: Phoenix Settlement and Tolstoy Farm (✅ owner 19).
- Target: racial laws in a settler colony (✅ owner 19, limit).
- Analytical point: indentured and ex-indentured labourers gave early experience of mobilising the poor (⚠️ owner 19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1893–1914 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** MOD-3020, MOD-3036 (trap: not passive submission).
**Trap:** Satyagraha was developed in South Africa, not "invented in India" (owner's factual caution).
**Mains hook:** Do not present South Africa as a rehearsal in scale; the struggle was over specific statutes.
**Back:** *"Eighteen-ninety-three to nineteen-fourteen: truth-force forged in South Africa."*

### Flashcard MOD-3022 — Gokhale and Gandhi's return (1915)
**Front:** Who guided Gandhi on his return to India, and what did he do first?
**Definition (learn):** Gandhi returned to India in 1915; Gopal Krishna Gokhale was his political mentor and advised him to study Indian conditions before launching campaigns.
**Breakdown:**
- Mentor: Gokhale (✅ owner 19).
- Period: touring India before major campaigns (✅ owner 19).
- Method: observation before action (✅ owner 19, Advanced 19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1915 return (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** MOD-3020, MOD-3023.
**Trap:** Gokhale was a mentor, not the leader of the Champaran campaign.
**Mains hook:** The mentorship shows how Gandhi's knowledge of Indian conditions was built before he acted.
**Back:** *"Fifteen: Gokhale's advice: learn India first."*

### Flashcard MOD-3023 — Champaran Satyagraha (1917)
**Front:** What was the Champaran Satyagraha, who brought Gandhi there, and what did it secure?
**Definition (learn):** A 1917 peasant campaign in Bihar against the indigo planters' tinkathia/teenkathia system, in which Gandhi conducted an inquiry and an official inquiry resulted.
**Breakdown:**
- Issue: indigo cultivation bound by the teenkathia system; planters demanded enhanced rents and illegal dues (✅ owner 19; ✅ Wikipedia: Champaran Satyagraha).
- Invitation: Raj Kumar Shukla, a local peasant (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 19).
- Method: Gandhi continued the inquiry in defiance of an order to leave (✅ owner 19).
- Outcome: official inquiry on which Gandhi served; teenkathia ended; planters refunded 25 per cent of unlawful collections (✅ Wikipedia: Champaran Satyagraha).
- Date: April–May 1917 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1917; Raj Kumar Shukla; teenkathia; 25 per cent refund (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3024 (trap), MOD-3027 (comparison), MOD-3020.
**Trap:** Champaran was not an anti-colonial campaign; it addressed a specific rent and contract grievance.
**Mains hook:** Champaran converted a private planter grievance into a state matter and proved satyagraha in an agrarian setting.
**Back:** *"Seventeen, Shukla's invitation, teenkathia ended: the first proof."*

### Flashcard MOD-3024 — Champaran trap: first civil disobedience, not first hunger strike
**Front:** Was Champaran Gandhi's first hunger strike, and what was it significant for?
**Definition (learn):** No. Champaran was his first civil-disobedience success in India; the first hunger strike in India was at Ahmedabad in 1918.
**Breakdown:**
- Champaran: first civil-disobedience success (✅ owner 19).
- Ahmedabad: first hunger strike in India (✅ owner 19).
- PYQ route: 2018 Prelims GS-I Q69 on Champaran significance (key unavailable locally, ✅ owner 19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1917 Champaran; 1918 Ahmedabad (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** MOD-3023, MOD-3025, MOD-3039.
**Trap:** The "hunger strike at Champaran" distractor is the owner's own warning.
**Mains hook:** Champaran's significance is threefold: satyagraha worked in agrarian conditions, a leader accepted arrest, and state machinery took over.
**Back:** *"Champaran: civil disobedience. Ahmedabad: first hunger strike."*

### Flashcard MOD-3025 — Ahmedabad Mill Strike (1918)
**Front:** What was the 1918 Ahmedabad mill dispute, and what distinctive method did Gandhi use?
**Definition (learn):** A cotton-mill wage dispute in Ahmedabad in which Gandhi used arbitration, worker discipline and a fast, his first hunger strike in India.
**Breakdown:**
- Issue: mill workers' wages (✅ owner 19).
- Method: arbitration and hunger strike as moral pressure (✅ owner 19, Advanced).
- Emphasis: labour–capital conflict resolved through arbitration (✅ owner 19 Advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1918 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** MOD-3024, MOD-3026, MOD-3027.
**Trap:** Do not place Ahmedabad in the 1917 Champaran sequence.
**Mains hook:** Ahmedabad extended satyagraha to industrial labour, Kheda to the state's revenue demand.
**Back:** *"Eighteen mills, wages, first hunger strike."*

### Flashcard MOD-3026 — Kheda Satyagraha (1918)
**Front:** What did the Kheda Satyagraha demand, who emerged there, and why is it more radical than Ahmedabad?
**Definition (learn):** A 1918 Gujarat campaign demanding revenue remission after crop failure; it gave Vallabhbhai Patel and Indulal Yagnik prominence.
**Breakdown:**
- Issue: revenue remission after crop failure (✅ owner 19).
- Leaders: Vallabhbhai Patel and Indulal Yagnik (✅ owner 19).
- Radicalism: withheld payment from the government, not an employer; the ancestor of 1928 and 1930 no-tax campaigns (✅ owner 19, analytical).
- Limit: remission under existing rules, not a repudiation of revenue (✅ owner 19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1918 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** MOD-3027, MOD-3119 (Bardoli 1928).
**Trap:** Kheda is not indigo (that is Champaran) and not 1930-type civil disobedience.
**Mains hook:** Kheda taught the no-tax logic later used at Bardoli.
**Back:** *"Kheda: revenue, not indigo; remission, not repudiation."*

### Flashcard MOD-3027 — Three early satyagrahas compared
**Front:** How do Champaran, Ahmedabad and Kheda differ in issue, opponent, organisation, scale and outcome?
**Definition (learn):** A comparison table of Gandhi's first three local campaigns.
**Breakdown:**
- Champaran (1917): indigo and tinkathia; planters; ad hoc volunteers; district scale; concrete redress (✅ owner 19).
- Ahmedabad (1918): mill wages; mill-owners; arbitration; hunger strike (✅ owner 19).
- Kheda (1918): revenue remission; the state; peasant solidarity; withheld revenue (✅ owner 19).
- Contrast: Non-Cooperation and CD (1920s–30s) were national, symbolic and constitutional (✅ owner 19, 8.5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1917, 1918, 1918 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** MOD-3023, MOD-3025, MOD-3026, MOD-3040 (NCM).
**Trap:** The early three were laboratories, not all-India movements.
**Mains hook:** "Champaran, Ahmedabad and Kheda were laboratories, not isolated local agitations" (owner Mains line).
**Back:** *"Indigo, wages, revenue: three labs, one method."*

### Flashcard MOD-3028 — Rowlatt Act (1919): provisions
**Front:** What did the Rowlatt Act of 1919 provide, and when was it passed?
**Definition (learn):** A wartime-emergency extension law that allowed detention without trial and special political courts.
**Breakdown:**
- Passed: 18 March 1919; took effect 21 March 1919 (✅ Wikipedia: Rowlatt Act).
- Detention without trial for up to two years, arrest without warrant (✅ Wikipedia: Rowlatt Act).
- Special courts: three judges, in camera, no jury, no appeal (✅ Wikipedia: Rowlatt Act; ✅ owner 19).
- Habeas corpus suspended (✅ Wikipedia: Rowlatt Act).
- Popular label: "Black Act" (✅ owner 19; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 18 March 1919; 21 March 1919; two years (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3029 (hartal), MOD-3030 (Jallianwala), MOD-3031 (Tagore), MOD-3033 (trap).
**Trap:** The Rowlatt Act did not create separate electorates (1909 is the date of separate electorates).
**Mains hook:** Reform and repression arriving together was the argument on which non-cooperation later rested (owner 19 analytical).
**Back:** *"Rowlatt: eighteen March, two years without trial, no jury, no appeal."*

### Flashcard MOD-3029 — Rowlatt Satyagraha hartal (6 April 1919)
**Front:** What was the Rowlatt Satyagraha, and what did the 6 April 1919 hartal involve?
**Definition (learn):** A nationwide non-violent protest and hartal on 6 April 1919 against the Rowlatt Act, which Gandhi called as the first all-India agitation.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 6 April 1919 (✅ owner 19; ✅ Wikipedia: Rowlatt Act).
- Form: suspension of business, fasting, prayers, public meetings (✅ Wikipedia).
- Violence: rioting in Punjab, Delhi and Gujarat; Gandhi suspended the resistance (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 19).
- Lesson: non-violence had to be trained and controlled (✅ owner 19, Advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 6 April 1919 (✅ owner 19; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3028, MOD-3030, MOD-3036.
**Trap:** The hartal was not a complete organisational success; the Advanced owner notes its violence.
**Mains hook:** Rowlatt revealed Gandhi's popularity and the danger of insufficient discipline.
**Back:** *"Six April: hartal, fast, prayer; violence taught discipline."*

### Flashcard MOD-3030 — Jallianwala Bagh massacre (13 April 1919)
**Front:** When and where did the Jallianwala Bagh massacre occur, who ordered the firing, and what was the aftermath?
**Definition (learn):** On 13 April 1919 at Amritsar, Brigadier-General Reginald Dyer ordered firing on an unarmed gathering; the massacre transformed Indian opinion.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 13 April 1919, a Sunday during Baisakhi (✅ Wikipedia: Jallianwala Bagh).
- Commander: Brigadier-General Reginald Dyer (✅ Wikipedia).
- Inquiry: Hunter Commission announced 14 October 1919 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Casualties: disputed; do not quote figures (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 19).
- Punjab martial law (✅ owner 19 Advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13 April 1919; Hunter Commission announced 14 October 1919 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3031 (Tagore), MOD-3032 (Kaiser-i-Hind), MOD-3038 (rupture thesis).
**Trap:** Jallianwala occurred in April 1919, before Non-Cooperation was launched; it is a bridge, not the sole cause (owner 19, 20).
**Mains hook:** Jallianwala destroyed remaining faith in British justice and made non-cooperation credible.
**Back:** *"Thirteen April, Amritsar, Dyer: firing on the unarmed; Hunter followed in October."*

### Flashcard MOD-3031 — Tagore renounces his knighthood (1919)
**Front:** When and how did Rabindranath Tagore protest the Jallianwala Bagh massacre?
**Definition (learn):** Tagore repudiated his knighthood in a letter to the Viceroy, a mass signal of withdrawn elite consent.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 31 May 1919, letter to the Viceroy (✅ Wikipedia: Jallianwala Bagh).
- Meaning: withdrawal of elite consent (✅ owner 19 Advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 31 May 1919 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3030, MOD-3032.
**Trap:** Tagore's protest (1919) is not Gandhi's medal return (1920).
**Mains hook:** The mass renunciation of honours registered the legitimacy break that made non-cooperation credible.
**Back:** *"May nineteen: Tagore's knighthood, a letter to the Viceroy."*

### Flashcard MOD-3032 — Kaiser-i-Hind medal returned (1920)
**Front:** When did Gandhi return the Kaiser-i-Hind medal, and why?
**Definition (learn):** Gandhi returned his Kaiser-i-Hind medal in 1920, over the Punjab wrongs and the Khilafat issue.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 1920, not 1919 (✅ owner 19).
- Reasons: Punjab wrongs and Khilafat (✅ owner 19).
- Contrast: Tagore's renunciation came in 1919 (MOD-3031).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1920 (✅ owner 19; ✅ owner 19 factual caution).
**Links:** MOD-3031, MOD-3041 (Khilafat).
**Trap:** Gandhi returned the medal in 1920, not 1919; Tagore renounced in 1919.
**Mains hook:** The honours protests bridged 1919 repression and 1920 non-cooperation.
**Back:** *"Tagore nineteen, Gandhi twenty: two medals, two years."*

### Flashcard MOD-3033 — Rowlatt and separate electorates: a trap pair
**Front:** Did the Rowlatt Act introduce separate electorates?
**Definition (learn):** No. Separate electorates came with the Morley-Minto reforms of 1909; the Rowlatt Act was about detention without trial.
**Breakdown:**
- 1909: separate electorates (✅ owner 19; ✅ Wikipedia: Lucknow Pact).
- 1919: Rowlatt Act (MOD-3028).
- Use the year and subject to separate the two.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1909; 1919 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** MOD-3008 (ladder), MOD-3028.
**Trap:** Rowlatt ≠ separate electorates.
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to answer communal-representation questions chronologically.
**Back:** *"Nine: separate electorates. Nineteen: Rowlatt detention."*

### Flashcard MOD-3034 — Gandhism and Marxism: common ground and divergence
**Front:** On what points do Gandhian social thought and Marxism agree, and where do they part?
**Definition (learn):** Shared diagnosis of exploitation with opposed means and theory of the person.
**Breakdown:**
- Shared (analytical ⚠️): rejection of exploitation; criticism of unregulated capitalism; aim of a condition without coercive state; test of the poorest (✅ owner 19 analytical).
- Divergence: class struggle and revolutionary force (Marxism) vs non-violence where means determine ends (Gandhi) (⚠️ owner 19).
- Property: abolition (Marx) vs trusteeship (Gandhi) (⚠️ owner 19).
- Consciousness: socially determined (Marx) vs individual moral transformation (Gandhi) (⚠️ owner 19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No quotations; analytical characterisations only (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** MOD-3036, MOD-3039 (PYQ 2020 Q14).
**Trap:** Do not claim either endorsed the other's programme or attribute formulations verbatim.
**Mains hook:** Gandhian social thought is best assessed as a political technique with costs and strengths.
**Back:** *"Same diagnosis, different means: force vs moral trusteeship."*

### Flashcard MOD-3035 — Chronology ladder: Gandhi 1893 to 1919
**Front:** Give the Gandhi chronology from South Africa to the 1919 Jallianwala.
**Definition (learn):** A single ladder of Gandhi's formative dates.
**Breakdown:**
- 1893–1914: South Africa, satyagraha (✅ owner 19).
- 1915: return; Gokhale mentor (✅ owner 19).
- 1917: Champaran (April–May) (✅ Wikipedia).
- 1918: Ahmedabad (first hunger strike); Kheda (✅ owner 19).
- 6 April 1919: Rowlatt hartal (✅ Wikipedia).
- 13 April 1919: Jallianwala Bagh (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** MOD-3020, MOD-3023, MOD-3025, MOD-3026, MOD-3029, MOD-3030.
**Trap:** Keep the sequence fixed: Champaran 1917 → Ahmedabad/Kheda 1918 → Rowlatt/Jallianwala 1919 (owner 19 trap).
**Mains hook:** The order shows how credibility compounded before the 1919 rupture.
**Back:** *"Ninety-three, fifteen, seventeen, eighteen, nineteen: the ladder of Gandhi."*

### Flashcard MOD-3036 — Satyagraha is active resistance, not passive submission
**Front:** Does satyagraha mean passive submission?
**Definition (learn):** No. Satyagraha is active non-violent resistance based on truth and self-suffering, which makes repression politically costly.
**Breakdown:**
- Active: resistance through accepting punishment (✅ owner 19).
- Method thesis: repression made costly for a regime claiming to rule by law (✅ owner 19).
- Limit: non-violence had to be organised and controlled (✅ owner 19 Advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner statement (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** MOD-3021, MOD-3029, MOD-3034.
**Trap:** Passive submission is the wrong definition for satyagraha.
**Mains hook:** Satyagraha worked against a state that needed legitimacy; its power was proportional to that need.
**Back:** *"Satyagraha: not submission; resistance that suffers."*

### Flashcard MOD-3037 — Gandhi's leadership verdict and credibility-first thesis
**Front:** How should an answer argue Gandhi became the national leader?
**Definition (learn):** Gandhi built authority on three limited, local, winnable campaigns and applied that credibility to an all-India cause.
**Breakdown:**
- Credibility-first thesis (✅ owner 19 qualified thesis).
- Verdict: indispensable by 1920 (✅ owner 19).
- Limit: Home Rule Leagues had already built branch networks (✅ owner 19 counter-evidence).
- Local agency: Raj Kumar Shukla, Kheda and Ahmedabad leaders (✅ owner 19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1917–1919 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** MOD-3020, MOD-3018 (Home Rule bridge), MOD-3040 (NCM).
**Trap:** Do not present Gandhi as sole originator; local grievance and earlier organisation mattered.
**Mains hook:** Gandhi supplied method and visibility, not the grievance.
**Back:** *"Three local wins, one national cause, one price paid."*

### Flashcard MOD-3038 — Rowlatt and Jallianwala as a rupture
**Front:** Why did Rowlatt and Jallianwala make non-cooperation thinkable?
**Definition (learn):** The 1919 events destroyed the working assumption of Moderate politics: that British rule was lawful and reformable.
**Breakdown:**
- Rupture thesis (✅ owner 19 qualified thesis).
- Measurable shift: renunciation of honours, mass hartal, call for non-cooperation (✅ owner 19).
- Verdict: "Rowlatt made non-cooperation arguable and Jallianwala made it necessary" (✅ owner 19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1919 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** MOD-3028, MOD-3030, MOD-3040.
**Trap:** Jallianwala alone did not cause NCM; Khilafat and post-war hardship combined (✅ owner 20).
**Mains hook:** Rowlatt and Jallianwala converted a constitutional movement into a mass one.
**Back:** *"Rowlatt raised the question; Jallianwala forced the answer."*

### Flashcard MOD-3039 — PYQ routes: Gandhi, 1917–19 and ideology
**Front:** Which PYQs route to owner 19 and what is their key status?
**Definition (learn):** Verified PYQ routes into this owner; no answer letter is recorded or inferred.
**Breakdown:**
- 2018 Prelims GS-I Q69: Champaran significance (key unavailable, ✅ owner 19). Route: MOD-3024.
- 2019 Prelims GS-I Q14: Gandhi, indenture and CDM events (key unavailable, ✅ owner 19). Route: MOD-3027 / MOD-3034.
- 2020 Prelims GS-I Q14: Gandhism and Marxism common ground (key unavailable, ✅ owner 19). Route: MOD-3034.
- Mains 2018 GS-I Q3 (10 marks): significance of Gandhi's thought today (✅ owner 19). Route: MOD-3036.
- Mains 2023 GS-I Q2 (10 marks): Gandhi and Tagore on education and nationalism (✅ owner 19). Route: MOD-3031.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed.
**Links:** MOD-3024, MOD-3034, MOD-3036.
**Trap:** Do not invent option letters.
**Mains hook:** Gandhi's thought as a bridge between politics and social reform.
**Back:** *"Champaran in 2018, Gandhism in 2020, thought in Mains: routes, not keys."*

### GROUP — 20 Non-Cooperation and the Khilafat Movement

### Flashcard MOD-3040 — Non-Cooperation Movement: the core chain
**Front:** What was the Non-Cooperation Movement (1920–22), and how did Khilafat and Congress reorganisation fit into it?
**Definition (learn):** The first all-India mass movement, in which Indians withdrew cooperation from the colonial state (titles, schools, courts, councils, foreign cloth), linked to the Khilafat issue, and withdrawn by Gandhi after Chauri Chaura.
**Breakdown:**
- Khilafat gave a Hindu–Muslim unity platform (✅ owner 20).
- Calcutta (September 1920) adopted it; Nagpur (December 1920) ratified it and reorganised Congress (✅ owner 20).
- Programme: boycott plus constructive work (✅ owner 20).
- Withdrawal 12 February 1922 after Chauri Chaura (✅ owner 20; ✅ Wikipedia: Chauri Chaura incident).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1920–22; Calcutta September 1920; Nagpur December 1920; withdrawal 12 February 1922 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3041, MOD-3044, MOD-3045, MOD-3046, MOD-3049, MOD-3050.
**Trap:** NCM was not begun by tax refusal; the early programme was boycott and construction (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** Evaluate NCM as the first real mass movement; its durable gain was organisational.
**Back:** *"Twenty to twenty-two: boycott, construction, Nagpur, then withdrawal."*

### Flashcard MOD-3041 — Khilafat: grievance and leaders
**Front:** What was the Khilafat grievance and who were its leaders?
**Definition (learn):** Indian Muslim anxiety over the treatment of the Ottoman Sultan-Caliph after WWI (including the Treaty of Sèvres), organised as an anti-British political mobilisation on a religious question.
**Breakdown:**
- Grievance: harsh treatment of the Caliph after WWI and the Treaty of Sèvres (✅ owner 20).
- Leaders: Maulana Mohammad Ali and Shaukat Ali (the Ali Brothers), Maulana Azad, Hakim Ajmal Khan; Hasrat Mohani (✅ owner 20, Advanced).
- Gandhi linked Khilafat to Swaraj (✅ owner 20).
- Khilafat movement period: 1919–22 (✅ Wikipedia: Khilafat Movement).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1919–22; Ali Brothers (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3040, MOD-3042, MOD-3043, MOD-3060.
**Trap:** Khilafat was not a Hindu religious movement; it was a Muslim political mobilisation (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** The Khilafat–NCM alliance was powerful but rested on an issue decided outside India.
**Back:** *"Caliph's cause, Ali Brothers' voice, Gandhi's bridge to Swaraj."*

### Flashcard MOD-3042 — Khilafat Conference (November 1919) and the Committee dating trap
**Front:** When was the All-India Khilafat Conference held, and was the Khilafat Committee formed then?
**Definition (learn):** The securely dated event is the All-India Khilafat Conference at Delhi in November 1919, which threatened withdrawal of cooperation if demands were rejected; the Committee already existed before that conference.
**Breakdown:**
- Conference: Delhi, November 1919 (✅ owner 20).
- Committee: formed before the November 1919 conference (✅ owner 20 Must-Know).
- Do not misdate the committee to the conference (✅ owner 20 Advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** November 1919 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3041, MOD-3040.
**Trap:** The conference is not the committee's founding.
**Mains hook:** The Khilafat issue turned religious sentiment into political threat.
**Back:** *"Committee first, conference November 1919, threat of non-cooperation."*

### Flashcard MOD-3043 — Caliphate abolished (1924)
**Front:** When and by whom was the Caliphate abolished, and why does it matter for Khilafat?
**Definition (learn):** The Turkish Republic abolished the Caliphate in 1924 under Atatürk's reforms, ending the Khilafat issue's purpose.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 1924 (✅ Wikipedia: Khilafat Movement; ✅ owner 20).
- Actor: Turkey under Mustafa Kemal (Atatürk) (✅ owner 20; Wikipedia says Atatürk's reforms).
- Exact day and the Turkish assembly's name not confirmed by fetch (⚠️).
- Effect: exposed the fragility of an alliance built on a contingent issue (✅ owner 20 Advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1924 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3041, MOD-3060.
**Trap:** The 1924 date is for abolition; the Khilafat agitation ended in 1922 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Mains hook:** Khilafat unity was deepest on the narrowest foundation; history removed the issue within two years.
**Back:** *"Twenty-four: Caliphate gone, Khilafat's reason gone."*

### Flashcard MOD-3044 — Calcutta Special Session (September 1920)
**Front:** Where and when did the Congress adopt Non-Cooperation, and what is the trap?
**Definition (learn):** The Calcutta Special Session of September 1920 adopted the Non-Cooperation programme; Nagpur in December 1920 ratified it.
**Breakdown:**
- Adoption: Calcutta Special Congress, September 1920 (✅ owner 20).
- Ratification: Nagpur, December 1920 (MOD-3045).
- Programme: surrender of titles, boycotts, khadi, national education (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** September 1920 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3040, MOD-3045.
**Trap:** Calcutta adopted it; Nagpur ratified and reorganised. Do not swap them.
**Mains hook:** Calcutta gave the decision; Nagpur gave the machinery.
**Back:** *"Calcutta adopts, Nagpur builds."*

### Flashcard MOD-3045 — Nagpur Congress (December 1920): reorganisation
**Front:** What did the Nagpur Congress of December 1920 change in the Congress organisation?
**Definition (learn):** Nagpur ratified Non-Cooperation and reorganised Congress into a mass-party structure.
**Breakdown:**
- Linguistic provincial committees (✅ owner 20).
- An AICC and a stronger Working Committee (✅ owner 20).
- A low membership fee (✅ owner 20).
- Transformation from annual notables' conference to permanent organisation (✅ owner 20 Advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** December 1920 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3040, MOD-3044, MOD-3061 (trap).
**Trap:** Nagpur was not only a political resolution; it reorganised Congress (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** The Nagpur reorganisation is the movement's most durable achievement.
**Back:** *"Nagpur: linguistic committees, a Working Committee, a cheap ticket."*

### Flashcard MOD-3046 — NCM programme: five-stage escalation ladder
**Front:** What were the five stages of the Non-Cooperation programme, and which was never reached?
**Definition (learn):** An escalation ladder from renunciation to mass civil disobedience and tax refusal; the fifth stage was never launched.
**Breakdown:**
- Stage 1: renunciation of titles and honorary offices (implemented) (✅ owner 20).
- Stage 2: boycott of schools, courts, legislatures, official functions (implemented unevenly) (✅ owner 20).
- Stage 3: economic boycott of foreign cloth and goods, liquor picketing (most effective) (✅ owner 20).
- Stage 4: constructive programme (khadi, charkha, swadeshi, national education, panchayats, Hindu–Muslim unity, temperance) (✅ owner 20).
- Stage 5: mass civil disobedience and tax refusal; announced for Bardoli taluqa; never launched (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Five stages; stage five never launched (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3047, MOD-3048, MOD-3052.
**Trap:** The ladder answers both "what were the stages" and "why did it fail": withdrawal came before stage five.
**Mains hook:** Movement withdrawn exactly where it would have moved from symbolic to fiscal confrontation.
**Back:** *"Titles, boycott, cloth, construction, then taxes never reached."*

### Flashcard MOD-3047 — NCM constructive institutions
**Front:** Which national institutions were founded in the boycott phase?
**Definition (learn):** Permanent educational institutions born of the constructive programme.
**Breakdown:**
- Jamia Millia Islamia (✅ owner 20).
- Kashi Vidyapith (✅ owner 20).
- Gujarat Vidyapith (✅ owner 20).
- Bihar Vidyapith (✅ owner 20).
- Also: khadi and charkha networks; full-time village workers (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Founded in the 1920s boycott phase (✅ owner 20; exact founding dates not in owner).
**Links:** MOD-3046, MOD-3048.
**Trap:** Do not invent founding dates for these four institutions from memory.
**Mains hook:** Constructive work produced the most durable products of NCM.
**Back:** *"Jamia, Kashi, Gujarat, Bihar: schools that survived the boycott."*

### Flashcard MOD-3048 — Boycott versus construction: durability comparison
**Front:** Which mattered more in Non-Cooperation: the boycott or the constructive programme?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's verdict: boycott was spectacular and reversible; the constructive programme was less visible but durable.
**Breakdown:**
- Boycott: visible, mass, reversible; Swarajists reversed the council boycott in 1923 (✅ owner 20; ✅ owner 21).
- Constructive: slower, durable; institutions and cadre survived to 1930 (✅ owner 20).
- Weakness: boycott costly to the poor; construction quietist (✅ owner 20).
- Caution: do not quantify schools or khadi output (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1923 council boycott reversal (✅ owner 20/21).
**Links:** MOD-3046, MOD-3047, MOD-3065.
**Trap:** The "boycott was the main story" answer misses the durable constructive assets.
**Mains hook:** Boycott is by nature temporary; construction created a trained cadre and a rural presence.
**Back:** *"Boycott burns bright; construction builds the house."*

### Flashcard MOD-3049 — Chauri Chaura (4 February 1922)
**Front:** What happened at Chauri Chaura on 4 February 1922, and how many police died?
**Definition (learn):** A procession of Congress and Khilafat volunteers at Chauri Chaura in Gorakhpur district, United Provinces, attacked police, and the crowd burnt a police station after police fired.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 4 February 1922 (✅ Wikipedia: Chauri Chaura incident; ✅ owner 20).
- Place: Gorakhpur district, United Provinces (✅ owner 20; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Deaths: 22 policemen (Wikipedia notes some accounts count 23) (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 20).
- Result: Gandhi's decision to withdraw (MOD-3050).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 4 February 1922; 22 police (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3050, MOD-3051, MOD-3053, MOD-3056.
**Trap:** Not Punjab, not Bihar; Gorakhpur district, UP (✅ owner 20). Note: owner's Bipin Chandra book prints a date one day earlier, overridden by owner (⚠️ source note).
**Mains hook:** Chauri Chaura defined Gandhi's insistence that non-violence is a condition of mass politics.
**Back:** *"Four February, Gorakhpur, twenty-two police: the violence that ended a movement."*

### Flashcard MOD-3050 — Withdrawal (12 February 1922) and the Bardoli resolution
**Front:** When was the Non-Cooperation Movement withdrawn, and why is the resolution called the Bardoli resolution?
**Definition (learn):** Gandhi withdrew the movement on 12 February 1922; the Congress Working Committee ratified it, and the decision is known as the Bardoli resolution because mass civil disobedience was to have begun at Bardoli.
**Breakdown:**
- Withdrawal: 12 February 1922 (✅ Wikipedia: Chauri Chaura; ✅ owner 20).
- Name "Bardoli resolution": ✅ owner 20 claims; the fetched Chauri Chaura page does not name it (⚠️ not independently confirmed).
- Irony: the meeting meant to authorise escalation ordered retreat (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12 February 1922 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3049, MOD-3052.
**Trap:** Withdrawal was because of violence, not because the movement was weak (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** The same meeting that was to authorise escalation ordered retreat; judge by criteria.
**Back:** *"Twelve February: withdrawn at Bardoli, which was meant to begin."*

### Flashcard MOD-3051 — Gandhi's arrest and sentence (March 1922)
**Front:** When was Gandhi arrested and sentenced, and to how much imprisonment?
**Definition (learn):** Gandhi was arrested in March 1922 and sentenced to six years' imprisonment.
**Breakdown:**
- Arrest: March 1922 (✅ owner 20).
- Sentence: six years (✅ owner 20).
- Context: after the withdrawal (MOD-3050).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** March 1922; six years (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3050, MOD-3049.
**Trap:** Owner says the 1922 trial; avoid attaching the "Sedition has become my religion" statement to it (MOD-3057).
**Mains hook:** Gandhi's arrest followed withdrawal, leaving the Khilafat and Congress debate to continue without him.
**Back:** *"March twenty-two: six years."*

### Flashcard MOD-3052 — Bardoli: announced in 1922, launched in 1928
**Front:** What was announced for Bardoli in 1922, and when did Bardoli actually launch its no-tax satyagraha?
**Definition (learn):** Gandhi announced mass civil disobedience to begin in the Bardoli taluqa of Surat district; withdrawal prevented it, and Bardoli launched its no-tax satyagraha in 1928.
**Breakdown:**
- 1922: announced for Bardoli; never launched (✅ owner 20).
- 1928: Bardoli Satyagraha under Vallabhbhai Patel (✅ Wikipedia: Bardoli Satyagraha).
- The taluqa "was destined to wait another six years" (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1922 announced; 1928 launched (✅ owner 20; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3046, MOD-3119 (Bardoli 1928 organisation), MOD-3050.
**Trap:** Bardoli 1922 was announced, not started. Do not treat 1928 as a continuation of 1922.
**Mains hook:** The stage-five ladder was passed on to the Congress campaign of 1928.
**Back:** *"Twenty-two announced, twenty-eight delivered."*

### Flashcard MOD-3053 — The withdrawal debate: Dutt, Bipin Chandra and contemporaries
**Front:** How do historians differ on Gandhi's withdrawal after Chauri Chaura?
**Definition (learn):** A historiographical contest on whether withdrawal was a strategic necessity or a lost opportunity.
**Breakdown:**
- R. Palme Dutt (India Today): withdrawal shielded propertied classes from a radicalising peasantry (✅ owner 20).
- Bipin Chandra rebuttal: crowd showed no anti-property intent; agrarian unrest was ebbing by late 1921; withdrawal pre-empted repression (✅ owner 20).
- C.R. Das and Motilal Nehru favoured council entry and continued pressure (✅ owner 20 historiography note).
- Shahid Amin on local interpretation of nationalist language (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Attributions as stated in owner (⚠️ not independently fetched).
**Links:** MOD-3050, MOD-3062 (verdicts), MOD-3021.
**Trap:** Do not attribute quotations; present the criteria of judgement.
**Mains hook:** "Necessity or lost opportunity?" needs criteria: Gandhi's premises, momentum, and what followed.
**Back:** *"Dutt: class fear. Bipin: repression pre-empted. Gandhi: violence violates method."*

### Flashcard MOD-3054 — Eka movement (United Provinces, 1921–22)
**Front:** What was the Eka (Unity) movement, what were its grievances, and how did it end?
**Definition (learn):** A cross-class peasant movement in the United Provinces that grew under the Congress–Khilafat umbrella but escaped Congress discipline.
**Breakdown:**
- Grievances: rent generally 50 per cent above recorded rent; oppression by thekedars; share-rents (✅ owner 20).
- Ritual: Ganges-water rite; pledges to pay only recorded rent (on time), not leave when ejected, refuse forced labour, give no help to criminals, abide by panchayats (✅ owner 20).
- Leadership: Madari Pasi and low-caste grassroots leaders; resisted non-violence discipline (✅ owner 20).
- Composition: tenants plus many small zamindars (✅ owner 20).
- End: severe repression, March 1922 (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1921–22; 50 per cent; March 1922 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3055, MOD-3059, MOD-3134 (left/peasant trap cross-ref).
**Trap:** Do not describe Eka as a Congress movement; autonomy was its defining feature.
**Mains hook:** Eka shows peasants adopting the national moral form while rejecting its leadership.
**Back:** *"Eka: fifty per cent above record, pledges, Madari Pasi, March twenty-two."*

### Flashcard MOD-3055 — Eka versus Bardoli: autonomy versus organisation
**Front:** How do the Eka movement and the 1928 Bardoli Satyagraha contrast?
**Definition (learn):** The contrast is not peasant militancy versus moderation but autonomy versus organisation: Eka escaped national direction; Bardoli was harnessed to it.
**Breakdown:**
- Eka: autonomous, low-caste leadership under Madari Pasi, no central machinery (✅ owner 20).
- Bardoli: Congress-organised, directed by Vallabhbhai Patel; women of Bardoli gave the title Sardar (✅ owner 20; ✅ Wikipedia: origin of title, uncited).
- Social composition: Eka tenants and small zamindars; Bardoli peasant proprietors (✅ owner 20).
- Outcome: Eka repressed March 1922; Bardoli became the model of organised satyagraha (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1921–22 vs 1928 (✅ owner 20; ✅ Wikipedia: Bardoli).
**Links:** MOD-3054, MOD-3052, MOD-3119.
**Trap:** Do not treat Eka as the same kind of mobilisation as Bardoli.
**Mains hook:** Autonomy vs organisation is the analytical point; PYQ 2026 Q9 routes here.
**Back:** *"Eka escaped; Bardoli was harnessed."*

### Flashcard MOD-3056 — Mass civil disobedience never launched in 1922
**Front:** Was mass civil disobedience launched in 1922, and what is the trap about the NCM stages?
**Definition (learn):** No. The fifth stage (mass civil disobedience and tax refusal) was announced for Bardoli and never launched; NCM did not begin with tax refusal.
**Breakdown:**
- Stage five: announced, not launched (✅ owner 20).
- Early programme: boycott and construction, not tax refusal (✅ owner 20 trap).
- Consequence: withdrawal before fiscal confrontation (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1922 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3046, MOD-3049, MOD-3052.
**Trap:** "Non-Cooperation began with tax refusal" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** The untested part of the programme was the fiscal stage.
**Back:** *"Tax refusal: planned, never started."*

### Flashcard MOD-3057 — "Sedition has become my religion": not an NCM statement
**Front:** Where does Gandhi's statement "Sedition has become my religion" belong, and why is the 1922 placement a trap?
**Definition (learn):** The statement belongs to the Second Civil Disobedience Movement launched with the Dandi March (12 March 1930), not to Non-Cooperation or the 1922 trial.
**Breakdown:**
- Placement: Dandi March, 1930 (✅ owner 20 correction; ✅ owner 22; ✅ Wikipedia: Salt Satyagraha for the march date).
- Content: British rule as moral, material, cultural and spiritual ruin; "Ours is a non-violent battle" (✅ owner 22).
- PYQ routes: 2025 Prelims GS-I Q71 (key locally available, answer not recorded here, ✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12 March 1930 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3099 (content card), MOD-3064 (PYQ routes), MOD-3051.
**Trap:** Do not place it in 1920–22.
**Mains hook:** The phrase signals the 1930 shift from withdrawal of cooperation to breaking the law.
**Back:** *"Thirty, not twenty-two: sedition at Dandi."*

### Flashcard MOD-3058 — Legal defence of Chauri Chaura accused (2025 key as recorded in owner)
**Front:** Who led the legal defence of those arrested after Chauri Chaura, according to the owner's record?
**Definition (learn):** The owner records the official 2025 Prelims GS-I Series-A key for Q73 as option (b): Madan Mohan Malaviya and Krishna Kant. No other source in the repository confirms this as history.
**Breakdown:**
- Key citation: Ans-2025-GS1 Series A, page 1, Q73 = (b) (✅ owner 20 as recorded).
- Independent corroboration: none in the repository (⚠️ owner 20).
- What is verified: the incident, 22 police deaths, withdrawal and the Bardoli resolution date (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Q73 key as recorded in owner (✅ owner 20, key not independently checked).
**Links:** MOD-3049, MOD-3064.
**Trap:** Do not extend the key into an independent historical narrative.
**Mains hook:** Use the verified incident facts, not the defence attribution, in a Mains answer.
**Back:** *"Seventy-three: the key says Malaviya and Krishna Kant; the history needs more."*

### Flashcard MOD-3059 — UP Kisan Sabha and Baba Ramchandra
**Front:** What was the UP Kisan Sabha movement and who was its grassroots figure?
**Definition (learn):** A peasant movement in the United Provinces during the NCM years, associated at grassroots with Baba Ramchandra.
**Breakdown:**
- Related currents to name alongside Eka (✅ owner 20).
- Grassroots figure: Baba Ramchandra (✅ owner 20).
- Relation: autonomous peasant initiative (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1920s (⚠️ no date in owner).
**Links:** MOD-3054, MOD-3023 (Champaran agrarian work).
**Trap:** Not the 1936 All India Kisan Sabha (MOD-3118).
**Mains hook:** Agrarian mobilisation during NCM escaped national direction.
**Back:** *"Baba Ramchandra: UP peasants, not the 1936 AIKS."*

### Flashcard MOD-3060 — Khilafat–NCM alliance: temporary and fragile
**Front:** Did Khilafat guarantee permanent Hindu–Muslim unity, and was it a Hindu movement?
**Definition (learn):** No. Khilafat produced temporary unity on a religious-symbolic issue outside India; it was a political mobilisation of Muslims on a religious question.
**Breakdown:**
- Temporary: unity collapsed after the Caliphate's abolition and local communal violence (✅ owner 20).
- Not Hindu: a Muslim issue linked by Gandhi to Swaraj (✅ owner 20).
- Malabar (MOD-3133): local mobilisation damaged the alliance (⚠️ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1919–24 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3041, MOD-3043, MOD-3133.
**Trap:** Khilafat was not a Hindu religious movement and not permanent unity.
**Mains hook:** Critically evaluate the Khilafat alliance in the making and unmaking of unity.
**Back:** *"Khilafat: a brief bridge, not a permanent one."*

### Flashcard MOD-3061 — NCM trap: not only an elite council boycott
**Front:** Was Non-Cooperation only an elite boycott of councils?
**Definition (learn):** No. It drew students, lawyers, peasants, workers, traders, women and local leaders into national politics.
**Breakdown:**
- Social base widened (✅ owner 20 Advanced).
- Boycott of foreign cloth and khadi converted economic critique into practice (✅ owner 20).
- Flexible local translation: schools, khadi, peasant protest, anti-liquor picketing (⚠️ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1920–22 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3045, MOD-3066.
**Trap:** Do not reduce NCM to elite councils or pure spontaneous revolt.
**Mains hook:** NCM was both national and local (owner's trap line).
**Back:** *"Students, peasants, workers, women: not just councils."*

### Flashcard MOD-3062 — Withdrawal and NCM verdict scaffolds
**Front:** What verdict lines does the owner provide for NCM's evaluation, unity and withdrawal?
**Definition (learn):** Three qualified verdict scaffolds for 10/15/20-mark answers.
**Breakdown:**
- Evaluation: Non-Cooperation failed to win swaraj in a year and made it a demand millions had acted on (✅ owner 20).
- Unity: deepest Hindu–Muslim cooperation on the narrowest foundation (✅ owner 20).
- Withdrawal: obligatory on Gandhi's premises; costly on momentum; preserved an organisation (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner verdicts (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3053, MOD-3060, MOD-3048.
**Trap:** The verdicts are arguments; state criteria.
**Mains hook:** Avoid saying withdrawal was obviously right or wrong.
**Back:** *"Failed in a year, lasting in a demand; obligatory, costly, preserving."*

### Flashcard MOD-3063 — Swarajya Sabha (1920) is not the Swaraj Party (1923)
**Front:** Is the renamed Home Rule organisation (Swarajya Sabha) the same as the Swaraj Party?
**Definition (learn):** No. Swarajya Sabha was the renamed All-India Home Rule League (1920); the Swaraj Party was founded on 1 January 1923.
**Breakdown:**
- Swarajya Sabha: 1920 (✅ Wikipedia: Indian Home Rule Movement; ✅ owner 20).
- Swaraj Party: 1 January 1923 (✅ Wikipedia: Swaraj Party; MOD-3069).
- Owner mechanism control: "not the 1923 Swaraj Party" (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1920; 1923 (✅ as above).
**Links:** MOD-3015, MOD-3069.
**Trap:** Similar names, different organisations.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish the Home Rule organisation from the council-entry party.
**Back:** *"Twenty's Sabha is not twenty-three's Party."*

### Flashcard MOD-3064 — PYQ routes: Non-Cooperation and Chauri Chaura
**Front:** Which PYQs route to owner 20 and what is their key status?
**Definition (learn):** Verified PYQ routes into the NCM owner, with key status as recorded.
**Breakdown:**
- 2025 Prelims GS-I Q12: NCM programme components (key present locally, answer not recorded, ✅ owner 20). Route: MOD-3046.
- 2025 Prelims GS-I Q20: NCM Swaraj resolution and its stages (key present, answer not recorded, ✅ owner 20). Route: MOD-3046.
- 2025 Prelims GS-I Q71: "Sedition has become my religion" (key present, answer not recorded). Route: MOD-3057.
- 2025 Prelims GS-I Q73: Chauri Chaura legal defence (key present, answer not recorded). Route: MOD-3058.
- 2026 Prelims GS-I Q9: Eka and Bardoli (provisional 2026 key, answer not inferred). Route: MOD-3055.
- 2021 GS-I Q12 Mains (15 marks): constructive programmes of Gandhi (✅ owner 20). Route: MOD-3047.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed; no answer letter inferred (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3046, MOD-3057, MOD-3058, MOD-3055, MOD-3047.
**Trap:** Keys are not recorded in the owner; do not invent letters.
**Mains hook:** Constructive programme answers should weigh durability against visibility.
**Back:** *"Twenty-five's four, twenty-six's Eka, twenty-one's constructive Mains."*

### Flashcard MOD-3065 — Constructive work was central, not decoration
**Front:** Was the constructive programme merely decoration beside the boycott?
**Definition (learn):** No. For Gandhi, khadi and social reconstruction were central to Swaraj.
**Breakdown:**
- Khadi, charkha, national schools and panchayats created an alternative moral economy (✅ owner 20 Advanced).
- Durable products: institutions and cadre (MOD-3047).
- Congress capacity in 1930 relied on this work (✅ owner 22; ✅ owner 21).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1920s (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3047, MOD-3048, MOD-3083 (1920s four streams).
**Trap:** Constructive work is not decorative (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** The constructive programme is the foundation of the 1930 mobilisation.
**Back:** *"Khadi was not decoration; it was Swaraj's scaffold."*

### Flashcard MOD-3066 — Local translation of NCM: subaltern readings
**Front:** How do subaltern and local studies complicate the Gandhian account of NCM?
**Definition (learn):** Local peasants and workers interpreted nationalist idioms through their own grievances against police, landlords and officials.
**Breakdown:**
- Local grievance: peasants read NCM through their own disputes (✅ owner 20 Advanced; ⚠️ interpretive).
- Eka and Chauri Chaura as evidence of autonomy (MOD-3054, MOD-3049).
- Historians' balance: Gandhi's agency and accumulated discontent (✅ owner 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1921–22 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** MOD-3054, MOD-3049, MOD-3053.
**Trap:** Avoid making Congress the only actor.
**Mains hook:** Balance Gandhi's agency with war, agrarian distress and repression.
**Back:** *"Local anger spoke nationalist: Eka, Chauri Chaura, police."*

### GROUP — 21 Swarajists and Revolutionaries in the 1920s

### Flashcard MOD-3067 — The 1920s: four parallel streams
**Front:** What were the four streams of the 1920s, and why is the decade not a gap between two Gandhian movements?
**Definition (learn):** After the 1922 withdrawal, four streams ran in parallel: Swarajist council obstruction, No-changer constructive work, revolutionary (HRA/HSRA) action, and an emerging Left and labour current.
**Breakdown:**
- Swarajist: discredit reforms from inside legislatures (✅ owner 21).
- No-changer: build society before confronting the state (✅ owner 21).
- Revolutionary: sacrifice and propaganda by deed (✅ owner 21).
- Emerging Left/labour: class organisation (✅ owner 21).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1922–29 (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** MOD-3068, MOD-3069, MOD-3072, MOD-3082.
**Trap:** The 1920s are not a trough; they produced the Congress organisation, the Purna Swaraj case and the martyr tradition (✅ owner 21).
**Mains hook:** Continuity thesis: the 1920s built the base of 1929–30.
**Back:** *"Four streams: council, village, bomb, class."*

### Flashcard MOD-3068 — No-changers versus Pro-changers (1922–23)
**Front:** What was the post-1922 split in Congress between No-changers and Pro-changers?
**Definition (learn):** A strategic debate over how to use the defeat of 1922: No-changers emphasised constructive work; Pro-changers, led by C.R. Das and Motilal Nehru, wanted council entry.
**Breakdown:**
- No-changers: khadi, national education, Hindu–Muslim unity, removal of untouchability (✅ owner 21).
- Pro-changers: C.R. Das and Motilal Nehru (✅ owner 21).
- Both wings remained inside Congress (✅ owner 21).
- Gaya (December 1922) preceded the Swaraj Party (✅ Wikipedia: Swaraj Party; ✅ owner 21 date control).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gaya, December 1922 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3069, MOD-3071, MOD-3067.
**Trap:** The split was tactical, not a permanent rupture (✅ owner 21).
**Mains hook:** Both wings reassembled for 1930; the split was a division of labour (qualified thesis).
**Back:** *"No-changers built; Pro-changers entered."*

### Flashcard MOD-3069 — Swaraj Party (1 January 1923)
**Front:** When was the Swaraj Party founded, by whom, and who held which office?
**Definition (learn):** The Swaraj Party was founded on 1 January 1923 to contest and obstruct councils from within.
**Breakdown:**
- Founders: C.R. Das and Motilal Nehru; Narasimha Chintaman Kelkar helped (✅ Wikipedia: Swaraj Party).
- Office: Das president; Nehru one of the secretaries (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 21).
- Das died in 1925, and the party was weakened (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 21).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1 January 1923 (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 21).
**Links:** MOD-3070, MOD-3063 (trap), MOD-3015.
**Trap:** Not the 1920 Swarajya Sabha (MOD-3015).
**Mains hook:** The Swarajist experiment exposed the limits of dyarchy from inside.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three, Das president, Nehru secretary, obstruct from within."*

### Flashcard MOD-3070 — Swarajist obstruction and the 1923 elections
**Front:** What did the Swarajists achieve in the 1923 elections and how did they use council entry?
**Definition (learn):** Council entry for obstruction: defeat government motions, refuse supply where possible, and expose dyarchy.
**Breakdown:**
- 1923: Swarajists won 45 of 145 seats in the councils (✅ Wikipedia: Swaraj Party).
- Method: obstruction, exposure of dyarchy's emptiness (✅ owner 21).
- Limit: could obstruct, not govern; erosion as patronage drew members (✅ owner 21).
- Council boycott reversed in 1923 (✅ owner 20/21).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1923; 45 of 145 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3069, MOD-3048, MOD-3082.
**Trap:** Owner warned earlier against seat numbers; the fetched page verifies 45 of 145 for 1923 only.
**Mains hook:** "An elected Indian majority could obstruct but not govern" (owner's analytical point).
**Back:** *"Forty-five of one-forty-five: obstruct, expose, never govern."*

### Flashcard MOD-3071 — No-changers' constructive programme
**Front:** What did the No-changers do between 1922 and 1930?
**Definition (learn):** A constructive programme of khadi, national education, temperance, communal harmony and anti-untouchability work that kept a cadre rooted in villages.
**Breakdown:**
- Khadi and charkha work (✅ owner 21).
- National schools, temperance, communal-harmony work, anti-untouchability (✅ owner 21).
- Politically quietist by design (✅ owner 21).
- Social base used by 1930 (✅ owner 21).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mid-1920s (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** MOD-3068, MOD-3065, MOD-3047.
**Trap:** Constructive work was not inactivity (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** The 1920s were not a lull; the cadre sustained rural contact.
**Back:** *"Spinning, schools, harmony: the cadre for 1930."*

### Flashcard MOD-3072 — Hindustan Republican Association (1924)
**Front:** When was the HRA founded, by whom, and what was its programme?
**Definition (learn):** The Hindustan Republican Association was an armed revolutionary organisation aiming at armed overthrow of colonial rule and a republican India.
**Breakdown:**
- Founded 1924; owner says October 1924 date control (✅ owner 21).
- Founders named: Sachindranath Sanyal, Ram Prasad Bismil, Jogesh Chatterjee (✅ owner 21; ✅ Wikipedia: HSRA infobox lists Yogendra Shukla, Bismil, Ashfaqulla Khan, Sachindra Nath Bakshi, Jogesh Chandra Chatterjee).
- Programme: republican, armed (✅ owner 21 Advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1924; October 1924 (✅ owner 21; ⚠️ month not fetched).
**Links:** MOD-3073, MOD-3075.
**Trap:** HRA (1924–28) and HSRA (1928 on) are one lineage with a changed ideology, not rival bodies (owner).
**Mains hook:** Armed revolutionary politics revived after 1922 discredited non-violence among some young people.
**Back:** *"HRA, twenty-four: republic by arms, then socialism."*

### Flashcard MOD-3073 — Kakori train action (9 August 1925)
**Front:** What was the Kakori train robbery and what were its consequences?
**Definition (learn):** A 1925 train robbery by HRA revolutionaries intended to raise money for weapons and publicise revolutionary courage.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 9 August 1925 (✅ Wikipedia: Kakori conspiracy).
- Purpose: funds for HRA; protest against tax burdens; publicity (✅ Wikipedia).
- Four condemned: Ram Prasad Bismil, Roshan Singh, Rajendra Lahiri, Ashfaqulla Khan (✅ Wikipedia).
- Executed by 19 December 1927 (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 21 as 1927).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 9 August 1925; by 19 December 1927 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3072, MOD-3074, MOD-3081.
**Trap:** Kakori was not led by Bhagat Singh (MOD-3074).
**Mains hook:** Kakori's trial and executions created the first interwar martyr cult (owner 21).
**Back:** *"Kakori, nine August: four condemned, all hanged by December twenty-seven."*

### Flashcard MOD-3074 — Kakori trap: Bhagat Singh was not in Kakori
**Front:** Was Bhagat Singh involved in the Kakori train robbery?
**Definition (learn):** No. Kakori was associated with Bismil, Ashfaqulla Khan and HRA colleagues; Bhagat Singh's major cases were Saunders, the Assembly bomb and the Lahore Conspiracy.
**Breakdown:**
- Kakori: HRA, 1925 (MOD-3073).
- Bhagat Singh: Saunders (1928), Assembly bomb (1929), Lahore Conspiracy (MOD-3079) (✅ owner 21).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** MOD-3073, MOD-3077, MOD-3078.
**Trap:** Do not place Bhagat Singh in Kakori (owner's 21 trap).
**Mains hook:** Distinguish HRA republicanism from HSRA socialism.
**Back:** *"Kakori: Bismil's trial, not Bhagat's."*

### Flashcard MOD-3075 — HRA becomes HSRA (8–9 September 1928)
**Front:** When and where was the HRA reorganised as the Hindustan Socialist Republican Association?
**Definition (learn):** The reorganisation took place at Feroz Shah Kotla, Delhi, when revolutionary groups from Bengal, Bihar and Punjab met with the HRA.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 8–9 September 1928 (✅ Wikipedia: HSRA).
- Place: Feroz Shah Kotla, Delhi (✅ Wikipedia).
- Owner's date: 9–10 September 1928 (⚠️ conflict with Wikipedia).
- Leaders: Chandra Shekhar Azad, Sachindra Nath Sanyal, Bhagat Singh (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 8–9 September 1928 (✅ Wikipedia; ⚠️ owner says 9–10 September).
**Links:** MOD-3076, MOD-3067, MOD-3081.
**Trap:** The new name marks a socialist turn, not a mere renaming (owner's trap).
**Mains hook:** HSRA made socialism a mainstream vocabulary of Indian anti-colonialism.
**Back:** *"Twenty-eight, Feroz Shah Kotla: republican became socialist."*

### Flashcard MOD-3076 — HSRA's socialist turn
**Front:** What changed in revolutionary thought when the HRA became HSRA?
**Definition (learn):** HSRA added a socialist and anti-imperialist vocabulary to the republican programme; Bhagat Singh's influence is cited for the name change.
**Breakdown:**
- Socialist republican orientation (✅ owner 21).
- Prominent figures: Bhagat Singh, Chandra Shekhar Azad, Sukhdev, Bhagwati Charan Vohra (✅ owner 21).
- Name change "probably being largely due to the influence of" Bhagat Singh (✅ Wikipedia: HSRA; ⚠️ hedged).
- Advanced: from individual heroism to rationalist socialist politics (⚠️ owner 21).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1928 (✅ owner 21; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3075, MOD-3079, MOD-3082.
**Trap:** Do not quote Bhagat Singh's writings verbatim; describe the position (owner's rule).
**Mains hook:** Evaluate Bhagat Singh as a political thinker, not only a martyr.
**Back:** *"HSRA: republic, socialism, and anti-imperial courtroom politics."*

### Flashcard MOD-3077 — Saunders killing (December 1928)
**Front:** Why was Saunders killed in December 1928?
**Definition (learn):** Saunders was killed in December 1928 to avenge Lala Lajpat Rai's death after the Lahore lathi-charge.
**Breakdown:**
- Lajpat Rai died 17 November 1928 (✅ Wikipedia: Simon Commission).
- Saunders killed December 1928 (✅ owner 21).
- Lahore Conspiracy case followed (✅ owner 21).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 17 November 1928; December 1928 (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 21).
**Links:** MOD-3088, MOD-3074, MOD-3079.
**Trap:** The Saunders case is distinct from the Assembly bomb case (owner).
**Mains hook:** The Saunders killing shows how the Simon boycott radicalised youth.
**Back:** *"Lajpat Rai's death, Saunders' death: December twenty-eight."*

### Flashcard MOD-3078 — Central Legislative Assembly bomb (8 April 1929)
**Front:** Who threw the bombs in the Central Legislative Assembly on 8 April 1929, and why?
**Definition (learn):** Bhagat Singh and Batukeshwar Dutt threw bombs at empty benches to make the deaf hear and use the courtroom as a platform.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 8 April 1929, Delhi (✅ Wikipedia: HSRA).
- Actors: Bhagat Singh and Batukeshwar Dutt (✅ owner 21).
- Designed non-lethal, symbolic (✅ owner 21).
- Quotation "to make the deaf hear" attributed in owner (✅ owner 21; ⚠️ wording not fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 8 April 1929 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3079, MOD-3076, MOD-3074.
**Trap:** The bomb was not aimed at mass killing (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** The Assembly action moved revolutionary method from insurrection to propaganda through the court.
**Back:** *"Eight April twenty-nine: empty benches, loud message."*

### Flashcard MOD-3079 — Bhagat Singh, Sukhdev and Rajguru: execution (23 March 1931)
**Front:** When were Bhagat Singh, Sukhdev and Rajguru executed, and which case were they tried in?
**Definition (learn):** They were hanged on 23 March 1931 after the Lahore Conspiracy Case, separate from the Assembly bomb case.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 23 March 1931 (✅ Wikipedia: HSRA; ✅ owner 21).
- Case: Lahore Conspiracy (⚠️ owner 21 separate proceedings).
- Pact effect: Gandhi–Irwin Pact did not prevent these executions (MOD-3100).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 23 March 1931 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3078, MOD-3100, MOD-3081.
**Trap:** Separate trials: the Assembly Bomb and Lahore Conspiracy are different proceedings (owner).
**Mains hook:** The martyr tradition in the 1920s fed into 1930–31 mass politics.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three March thirty-one: three hanged, one pact unable to save them."*

### Flashcard MOD-3080 — Chittagong Armoury Raid (1930), Bengal
**Front:** What was the Chittagong Armoury Raid and who led it?
**Definition (learn):** A 1930 attempt to seize a locality in Chittagong, Bengal, led by Surya Sen, in which women revolutionaries participated.
**Breakdown:**
- Leader: Surya Sen (✅ owner 21).
- Women: Kalpana Datta and Pritilata Waddedar (✅ owner 21 Advanced).
- Locality seizure, most ambitious operation of the period (✅ owner 21).
- Overlaps Civil Disobedience (MOD-3096).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1930; Bengal (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** MOD-3081, MOD-3096.
**Trap:** Chittagong is in Bengal, not Punjab (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** Revolutionary activity was regionally plural and included women.
**Back:** *"Chittagong, thirty, Surya Sen, and women in the armoury."*

### Flashcard MOD-3081 — Revolutionary sequence chronology ladder
**Front:** Give the revolutionary chronology from HRA to the Assembly bomb.
**Definition (learn):** A fixed ladder for 1920s revolutionary events.
**Breakdown:**
- Swaraj Party 1 January 1923 (MOD-3069).
- HRA 1924 (MOD-3072).
- Kakori 9 August 1925 (MOD-3073).
- Executions 1927 (MOD-3073).
- HSRA 8–9 September 1928 (MOD-3075).
- Saunders December 1928 (MOD-3077).
- Assembly bomb 8 April 1929 (MOD-3078).
- Chittagong 1930 (MOD-3080).
- Executions 23 March 1931 (MOD-3079).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed (✅ owner 21; ✅ Wikipedia where noted).
**Links:** MOD-3072–MOD-3080.
**Trap:** Keep the sequence HRA 1924 → Kakori 1925 → HSRA 1928 → Assembly bomb 1929 (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** The sequence shows ideological change without a change in the revolutionary goal of ending colonial rule.
**Back:** *"Twenty-four, twenty-five, twenty-eight, twenty-nine, thirty-one: the revolutionary ladder."*

### Flashcard MOD-3082 — Four streams comparison table
**Front:** How do the Swarajist, No-changer, revolutionary and emerging Left streams differ in theory, arena, achievement and limit?
**Definition (learn):** A comparison matrix for the 1920s.
**Breakdown:**
- Swarajist: theory = discredit reforms from inside; arena = legislatures; achievement = exposed dyarchy; limit = could obstruct, not govern (✅ owner 21).
- No-changer: theory = build society first; arena = villages, schools, khadi; achievement = cadre for 1930; limit = non-confrontational (✅ owner 21).
- Revolutionary: theory = sacrifice and propaganda; arena = courts; achievement = martyr tradition; limit = executions removed leadership (✅ owner 21).
- Emerging Left: theory = class organisation; arena = unions and peasant bodies; achievement = fed 1930s; limit = weak organisation (✅ owner 21).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** MOD-3067, MOD-3070, MOD-3071, MOD-3078, MOD-3110.
**Trap:** Do not merge the four streams into one.
**Mains hook:** Use the matrix to answer "was the 1920s a period of decline?"
**Back:** *"Obstruct, build, bomb, organise: four ways to the same goal."*

### Flashcard MOD-3083 — Mains verdicts and PYQ routes for the 1920s
**Front:** What are the verdicts and PYQ routes for the 1920s streams?
**Definition (learn):** Verdict scaffolds plus two verified routes.
**Breakdown:**
- Comparative verdict: council entry proved reforms worthless; constructive work proved the movement permanent (✅ owner 21).
- Ideological verdict: HSRA made socialism a mainstream vocabulary (✅ owner 21).
- Continuity verdict: 1920s ended with a stronger Congress (✅ owner 21).
- PYQ 2020 GS-I Q13 (15 marks): ideological strands of the national movement since the 1920s (✅ owner 21). Route: MOD-3082, MOD-3110.
- PYQ 2018 Q79 is Swarajya Sabha (Topic 18), kept as name-confusion control (MOD-3015).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** MOD-3067, MOD-3082, MOD-3015.
**Trap:** Do not promote an unkeyed route to a solved answer.
**Mains hook:** The 1920s as a continuity thesis, not a trough.
**Back:** *"Continuity, not a lull; ideas, not just martyrs."*

### Flashcard MOD-3084 — Chandra Shekhar Azad's death (February 1931)
**Front:** When and where did Chandra Shekhar Azad die?
**Definition (learn):** Azad died at Alfred Park in February 1931 (owner date; ⚠️ city Allahabad not in owner text).
**Breakdown:**
- Death: February 1931, Alfred Park (✅ owner 21 Advanced; ⚠️ not fetched).
- Leader of HSRA prominence (✅ owner 21).
- Executions of Bhagat Singh and others followed on 23 March 1931 (MOD-3079).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** February 1931 (✅ owner 21; ⚠️ not independently verified).
**Links:** MOD-3076, MOD-3079.
**Trap:** Azad was not executed; he died at Alfred Park (owner).
**Mains hook:** The loss of HSRA leadership in 1931 weakened the revolutionary organisation.
**Back:** *"Azad, Alfred Park, February thirty-one."*

### GROUP — 22 Simon Commission, Nehru Report, Civil Disobedience and Round Table Conferences

### Flashcard MOD-3085 — From constitutional deadlock to Purna Swaraj and CDM
**Front:** How did the Simon Commission, the Nehru Report and the Round Table Conferences move the Congress from dominion status to mass civil disobedience?
**Definition (learn):** Each British move produced a nationalist counter-move that narrowed compromise: the all-white Simon Commission (1927–28) → the Nehru Report (1928) → Lahore Purna Swaraj (December 1929) → Dandi and CDM (1930) → Gandhi–Irwin Pact (1931) → RTCs → CDM withdrawn (1934).
**Breakdown:**
- Provocation: Simon Commission, all-white (✅ owner 22).
- Counter-draft: Nehru Report, dominion status (✅ owner 22).
- Goal shift: Purna Swaraj at Lahore (✅ owner 22).
- Mass action: Dandi March and CDM (✅ owner 22).
- Negotiation: Gandhi–Irwin and RTCs (✅ owner 22).
- Settlement: Communal Award and Poona Pact (✅ owner 22).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1927–1934 (✅ owner 22).
**Links:** MOD-3086, MOD-3089, MOD-3092, MOD-3094, MOD-3100, MOD-3101, MOD-3104.
**Trap:** Keep goal (Purna Swaraj), method (CDM) and negotiation (RTCs) distinct.
**Mains hook:** "Simon and the Nehru Report destroyed the dominion-status compromise from opposite directions" (owner verdict).
**Back:** *"Simon, Nehru, Lahore, Dandi, Irwin, RTCs, Poona: one chain of closing doors."*

### Flashcard MOD-3086 — Simon Commission: appointment, arrival and composition
**Front:** When was the Simon Commission appointed and when did it reach India, and who chaired it?
**Definition (learn):** The British statutory commission on constitutional reform, which excluded Indian members and triggered an all-party boycott.
**Breakdown:**
- Appointed: November 1927 (✅ Wikipedia: Simon Commission).
- Arrived: 3 February 1928, Bombay (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 22).
- Chairman: Sir John Simon (✅ Wikipedia).
- Composition: seven British members, all white (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 22).
- Slogan: "Simon Go Back" (✅ owner 22).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** November 1927; 3 February 1928 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3087, MOD-3088.
**Trap:** The Simon Commission had no Indian member; this is the owner's standard trap.
**Mains hook:** The commission delegitimised British reform from above and turned it into mass protest.
**Back:** *"Simon, twenty-seven appointed, twenty-eight arrived, all-white."*

### Flashcard MOD-3087 — Simon trap: all-white and boycott
**Front:** Did the Simon Commission include Indian representatives?
**Definition (learn):** No. It was an all-white commission; the boycott was all-party and nationwide.
**Breakdown:**
- All-white composition (✅ owner 22).
- Boycott, hartals, "Simon Go Back" (✅ owner 22).
- Owner's note: the commission was exclusively British (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1928 (✅ owner 22).
**Links:** MOD-3086, MOD-3088.
**Trap:** A commission with Indian members is the wrong answer.
**Mains hook:** Exclusion made the constitutional question a nationalist question.
**Back:** *"No Indian in Simon, so Simon go back."*

### Flashcard MOD-3088 — Lala Lajpat Rai's death (17 November 1928)
**Front:** When and how did Lala Lajpat Rai die, and how did it connect to the Simon protests?
**Definition (learn):** Lajpat Rai, leading a Lahore anti-Simon protest, was injured in a lathi-charge and died on 17 November 1928.
**Breakdown:**
- Lathi-charge: 30 October 1928 (✅ owner 22 date control).
- Death: 17 November 1928 (✅ Wikipedia: Simon Commission).
- Consequence: the Saunders killing, December 1928 (MOD-3077).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 30 October 1928; 17 November 1928 (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 22).
**Links:** MOD-3086, MOD-3077.
**Trap:** Lajpat Rai died in November 1928; do not date his death to 1929.
**Mains hook:** The death fed the radical turn in youth politics.
**Back:** *"Lathi in October, death in November, twenty-eight."*

### Flashcard MOD-3089 — Nehru Report (1928)
**Front:** What did the Nehru Report propose and who prepared it?
**Definition (learn):** The Congress-era constitutional draft prepared by a committee chaired by Motilal Nehru, proposing dominion status and fundamental rights.
**Breakdown:**
- Chairman: Motilal Nehru (✅ Wikipedia: Nehru Report).
- Dominion status with a federal set-up and residuary powers at the centre (✅ Wikipedia).
- Fundamental rights including "no state religion" and equal citizen rights (✅ Wikipedia).
- Joint electorates; reserved seats for minorities where they were at least 10 per cent (✅ Wikipedia).
- Owner: rejected separate electorates (✅ owner 22; consistent with joint electorates).
- Adoption month not fetched (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1928 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3090, MOD-3091, MOD-3092.
**Trap:** The Nehru Report proposed dominion status, not Purna Swaraj (MOD-3090).
**Mains hook:** A milestone in Indian constitutional thinking and a failure of inter-communal agreement.
**Back:** *"Nehru, twenty-eight: dominion, rights, joint seats."*

### Flashcard MOD-3090 — Nehru Report trap: dominion status versus Purna Swaraj
**Front:** Did the Nehru Report demand complete independence?
**Definition (learn):** No. It proposed dominion status; Purna Swaraj (complete independence) was adopted at Lahore in December 1929.
**Breakdown:**
- Nehru Report: dominion status (✅ owner 22).
- Lahore: Purna Swaraj (MOD-3092).
- Difference: goal vs compromise (✅ owner 22).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1928 vs 1929 (✅ owner 22).
**Links:** MOD-3089, MOD-3092, MOD-3005.
**Trap:** Nehru Report ≠ Purna Swaraj.
**Mains hook:** The Report's failure pushed Congress to a more radical goal.
**Back:** *"Report: dominion. Lahore: complete."*

### Flashcard MOD-3091 — Jinnah's Fourteen Points (1929)
**Front:** What was Jinnah's response to the Nehru Report?
**Definition (learn):** Jinnah's Fourteen Points (1929) rejected the Nehru Report's minority formula and demanded minority safeguards.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 1929 (✅ owner 22; Wikipedia: drafted 1929 in reaction to the report).
- Demanded minority safeguards and separate electorates (✅ owner 22).
- Jawaharlal Nehru and Subhas Bose demanded full independence (✅ owner 22).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1929 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3089, MOD-3092.
**Trap:** Do not date the Fourteen Points to 1928.
**Mains hook:** The communal and radical challenges arrived together.
**Back:** *"Twenty-nine: fourteen points, safeguards first."*

### Flashcard MOD-3092 — Lahore Congress (December 1929): Purna Swaraj
**Front:** What did the Lahore Congress of December 1929 adopt, who presided, and what happened on the Ravi?
**Definition (learn):** The session adopted Purna Swaraj (complete independence) with Jawaharlal Nehru presiding.
**Breakdown:**
- Purna Swaraj adopted (✅ owner 22; ✅ Wikipedia: Purna Swaraj).
- Presiding: Jawaharlal Nehru (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 22).
- Tricolour hoisted on the Ravi at midnight, 31 December 1929 (✅ Wikipedia: Purna Swaraj).
- 26 January 1930 declared Independence Day (MOD-3093).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** December 1929; 31 December 1929 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3093, MOD-3089, MOD-3005.
**Trap:** Purna Swaraj was adopted in 1929; the pledge day was 1930.
**Mains hook:** Lahore was not a sudden rhetorical leap but the end of dominion politics.
**Back:** *"Twenty-nine: complete independence, flag on the Ravi."*

### Flashcard MOD-3093 — 26 January 1930: Independence Day pledge
**Front:** Why is 26 January significant in the nationalist calendar?
**Definition (learn):** The Congress asked people to observe 26 January as Independence Day, from 1930, the date of its declaration.
**Breakdown:**
- Declaration: 26 January 1930 (✅ Wikipedia: Purna Swaraj).
- Link to 26 January 1950 Republic (⚠️ owner 22 current link).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 26 January 1930 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3092, MOD-3094.
**Trap:** Purna Swaraj (1929) and 26 January (1930) are different dates.
**Mains hook:** Constitutional-history hook for the Republic's date.
**Back:** *"Twenty-six January, thirty: pledge day, later Republic Day."*

### Flashcard MOD-3094 — Dandi Salt March (12 March–6 April 1930)
**Front:** When and where was the Dandi March, how many marched at the start, and when was the salt law broken?
**Definition (learn):** Gandhi's march from Sabarmati Ashram to Dandi, which opened CDM and broke the salt law on the coast.
**Breakdown:**
- Start: 12 March 1930 from Sabarmati (✅ Wikipedia: Salt Satyagraha; ✅ owner 22).
- Marchers: Gandhi and 78 chosen followers (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 22).
- Salt law broken: 6 April 1930, at Dandi (✅ Wikipedia).
- Route: about 24 days (✅ Wikipedia; owner: nearly 200 miles).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12 March–6 April 1930 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-3095, MOD-3096, MOD-3057 (Sedition statement).
**Trap:** The 78 figure is for the start; do not extend it to the march's growing crowds (owner).
**Mains hook:** Salt made a constitutional demand something every Indian could personally enact.
**Back:** *"Twelve March to six April: seventy-eight with Gandhi, salt at Dandi."*

### Flashcard MOD-3095 — Why salt? The symbol thesis
**Front:** Why did Gandhi choose salt as the symbol for civil disobedience?
**Definition (learn):** Salt was a universal colonial exaction, legally clean and economically trivial, yet symbolically total.
**Breakdown:**
- Universality: touched every Indian regardless of class, region, caste or gender (✅ owner 22, thesis).
- Legality: an offence anyone could commit (✅ owner 22).
- Symbol: economic effect small, symbolic meaning large (✅ owner 22).
- Owner caution: salt manufacture was not economically significant (✅ owner 22).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1930 (✅ owner 22).
**Links:** MOD-3094, MOD-3096.
**Trap:** Do not present salt as an economic issue.
**Mains hook:** Discuss why salt broadened mass participation.
**Back:** *"Salt: universal tax, trivial to make, total in meaning."*

### Flashcard MOD-3096 — CDM forms, geography and participants
**Front:** Which forms of civil disobedience appeared, where were they strongest, and who participated?
**Definition (learn):** CDM was a family of movements: salt satyagraha, boycott of cloth and liquor, no-tax, forest-law defiance, Dharasana, Peshawar, Sholapur and NWFP mobilisation.
**Breakdown:**
- Salt: coastal Gujarat, Bombay, Bengal, Madras coast (✅ owner 22).
- Boycott and picketing of foreign cloth and liquor: urban centres (✅ owner 22).
- No-tax and no-rent: Gujarat, United Provinces (✅ owner 22).
- Forest-law defiance: Central Provinces, Maharashtra, tribal tracts (✅ owner 22).
- Dharasana, Peshawar, Sholapur (✅ owner 22).
- Women's wide participation (✅ owner 22).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1930–34 (✅ owner 22).
**Links:** MOD-3095, MOD-3097, MOD-3106, MOD-3107.
**Trap:** CDM was not uniform; five movements under one name (owner).
**Mains hook:** "Civil Disobedience was more than the Salt March" (owner Mains line).
**Back:** *"Salt, cloth, tax, forest, frontier: one name, many movements."*

### Flashcard MOD-3097 — NWFP: Khudai Khidmatgars under Ghaffar Khan
**Front:** What was the Khudai Khidmatgars movement and how did it reach the NWFP?
**Definition (learn):** The Khudai Khidmatgars ("Servants of God", the Red Shirts) pledged to non-violence under Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan in the NWFP.
**Breakdown:**
- Name: Khudai Khidmatgars, Red Shirts (✅ owner 22).
- Leader: Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan, "the Frontier Gandhi" (✅ owner 22).
- Penetration: deepest in a Muslim-majority region (✅ owner 22).
- Prior organisation: active for several years before 1930 (✅ owner 22; Bipan Chandra quoted in owner).
- Agrarian content: peasants against revenue extraction (✅ owner 22).
- Ghaffar Khan arrested 24 December 1931 (✅ owner 22).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1930; 24 December 1931 (✅ owner 22).
**Links:** MOD-3098, MOD-3096, MOD-3065.
**Trap:** Do not attribute frontier participation to "Pathan martial character" (owner).
**Mains hook:** Mass movements penetrate where a local organisation and grievance exist to receive the national call.
**Back:** *"Red Shirts, Servants of God, Ghaffar Khan: organised before, not converted in a day."*

### Flashcard MOD-3098 — Peshawar 1930 and the Garhwali refusal
**Front:** What happened at Peshawar in April 1930 and why does it matter?
**Definition (learn):** After Congress leaders were arrested in the NWFP on 23 April 1930, a mass demonstration held Peshawar for more than a week, and Garhwali soldiers refused to fire.
**Breakdown:**
- Tipping event: 23 April 1930 arrests (✅ owner 22).
- City "virtually in the hands of the crowd" for over a week (✅ owner 22).
- Two platoons of Garhwali soldiers refused to fire (✅ owner 22).
- Consequence: court martial and long imprisonment (✅ owner 22).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 23 April 1930 (✅ owner 22).
**Links:** MOD-3097, MOD-3096.
**Trap:** Refusal was by Garhwali soldiers, not Pathan troops.
