The Muslim League stayed aloof or opposed the movement; the Communists opposed it after the Nazi attack on the USSR, adopting the "People's War" line; the princes and much of the propertied class stayed loyal or neutral; the Hindu Mahasabha stayed out.
**Breakdown:**
- Muslim League: aloof or opposed; Jinnah backed the British war effort (✅ owner 25 adv; ✅ Wikipedia: Quit India Movement).
- Communists: after June 1941 (Nazi attack on the USSR) adopted the People's War line; opposed the movement (✅ owner 25 §5; ✅ Wikipedia: Quit India Movement).
- Princes and large landholders: loyal or neutral (⚠️ owner basic 25 §8.6).
- Hindu Mahasabha: stayed out (✅ owner 25 adv §2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Communist position after June 1941 (✅ owner 25 §8.6; the June 1941 date is owner-stated).
**Links:** MOD-4009, MOD-4017, MOD-4039 (League's 1945–46 sweep).
**Trap:** Do not say the Communists led or joined Quit India; the People's War line makes their opposition strategic, not collaboration (✅ owner 25 §8.6).
**Mains hook:** Argue that the isolation of Congress in 1942 shaped the balance of forces in 1945–46 (owner's "isolation thesis").
**Back:** *"League aloof, Communists at war-front, princes loyal, Mahasabha outside."*

### Flashcard MOD-4017 — Comparison: NCM 1920–22, CDM 1930–34 and Quit India 1942
**Front:** How do the three Gandhian mass movements compare on demand, method, leadership, state response and outcome?
**Definition (learn):** The three movements form a sequence of mass mobilisation: Non-Cooperation (1920–22), Civil Disobedience (1930–34) and Quit India (1942).
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: NCM = Swaraj and Khilafat redress; CDM = Purna Swaraj; Quit India = immediate British withdrawal.
- Method: NCM = withdrawal of cooperation; CDM = breaking specific laws; Quit India = open rebellion in effect after leadership removal.
- Leadership: NCM continuous; CDM continuous with negotiated pauses; Quit India removed within 24 hours.
- Muslim participation: NCM high; CDM low outside the NWFP; Quit India very low.
- State response: NCM restrained then repressive; CDM mass arrests and ordinances; Quit India immediate, comprehensive and military.
- Ending: NCM unilateral withdrawal; CDM negotiated suspension; Quit India suppression then negotiation from 1945.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25 basic §8.8 comparison engine).
**Links:** MOD-4005, MOD-4009, MOD-4018, MOD-4019.
**Trap:** Quit India was not a Gandhi-directed continuous campaign like NCM or CDM; its leadership was removed in a day.
**Mains hook:** Use the comparison to argue that each movement widened the social base while narrowing cohesion (owner's reach-coherence trade-off).
**Back:** *"NCM withdrew, CDM broke laws, Quit India was broken by arrests."*

### Flashcard MOD-4018 — Quit India results: immediate, political, long-term
**Front:** What were the immediate, political and long-term results of Quit India?
**Definition (learn):** Quit India's results must be stated in three registers: immediate suppression, political change in the conditions of British rule, and long-term effects on British calculation and on the Left.
**Breakdown:**
- Immediate: mass arrests, military deployment, firing, collective fines; the open campaign broken within months (✅ owner 25 §8.7).
- Political: after 1942 the Raj rested on coercion; Congress removed from legal politics until mid-June 1945, leaving the field to the League (✅ owner 25 §8.7).
- Political: demonstration of mass willingness to act, including districts with no Congress organisation (✅ owner 25 §8.9).
- Long-term: the British calculation shifted, with wartime exhaustion and the Labour government (⚠️ owner); radicalisation of the rank and file fed post-war Left politics (✅ owner 25 §8.7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Leadership imprisoned until mid-June 1945 (✅ owner 25 §8.7). No numbers of arrests or deaths in owners; do not state them (✅ owner 25 §8.11).
**Links:** MOD-4017, MOD-4016, MOD-4039 (elections), MOD-4053 (chain).
**Trap:** Do not claim Quit India won independence or forced an immediate concession in 1942 or 1943 (✅ owner 25 §8.9).
**Mains hook:** The movement's importance is best argued through changes in the conditions of British rule, not through its immediate success.
**Back:** *"1942 broke the movement and sealed coercion; 1945 released the leaders into a changed field."*

### Flashcard MOD-4019 — Trap: official non-violence vs actual sabotage in Quit India
**Front:** Was Quit India a non-violent movement, and how should a Mains answer state its violence question?
**Definition (learn):** Congress ideology and official method remained non-violent, but the upsurge included sabotage, attacks on railways, telegraph lines and police stations, and clashes with police. A balanced answer keeps both facts.
**Breakdown:**
- Official line: Congress remained non-violent in ideology (✅ owner 25 §8.5 limit/caution).
- Actual events: strikes, sabotage, attacks on communications and police posts (✅ owner 25 §5).
- Repression: severe, including firing and collective fines (✅ owner 25 §8.7).
- Method for answer: name the distinction between official method and events; do not choose one.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No violence counts or casualty figures are stated in the owners; do not cite them (✅ owner 25 §8.11).
**Links:** MOD-4011, MOD-4017, MOD-4018.
**Trap:** "Only peaceful protest" is wrong; "Congress planned violence" is also wrong.
**Mains hook:** Discuss the violence question "without losing sight of colonial repression" (✅ owner 25 adv §7).
**Back:** *"Non-violent in creed, not in every street: state both."*

### Flashcard MOD-4020 — PYQ route: the Quit India events question (2024 GS-I Q3; 2021 Prelims Q43)
**Front:** Which verified PYQs route to the Quit India cards, and what are their directives?
**Definition (learn):** The 2024 GS-I Q3 Mains question is verbatim in the owner: "What were the events that led to the Quit India Movement? Point out its results. (Answer in 150 words)". The 2021 Prelims Q43 is a routed objective demand on the 8 August 1942 AICC resolution, with the key unavailable locally.
**Breakdown:**
- 2024 GS-I Q3: Explain · 10 marks · 150 words; events leading to Quit India, plus results (✅ owner 25 adv §2 table).
- Answer chain: 1939 war → ministries resign → August Offer fails → Individual Satyagraha → Japanese advance → Cripps → AICC 8 August 1942 (✅ owner 25 adv "Exact PYQ application").
- 2021 Prelims Q43: Quit India resolution adopted August 1942 AICC (✅ owner 25 PYQ block; official key unavailable locally).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Verbatim 2024 wording (✅ owner 25 adv). No answer letter for 2021 Q43 is supplied (⚠️ not invented).
**Links:** MOD-4009, MOD-4018, MOD-4017.
**Trap:** Prelims: do not guess the key for 2021 Q43; the owner withholds the answer.
**Mains hook:** 150-word compression: name four causal steps in one clause each, then immediate and political results.
**Back:** *"Quit India question: four causes in a breath, then three results."*

### Flashcard MOD-4021 — PYQ route: the Cripps proposals (2022 Prelims Q54)
**Front:** What does the 2022 Prelims Q54 route cover, and what must the card cover for it?
**Definition (learn):** The 2022 GS-I Prelims Q54 demand is "Cripps Mission proposals for Indian constitution and provinces" (routed, neutral rendering; key unavailable locally).
**Breakdown:**
- Coverage: the constitution-making body and the provincial non-accession clause (✅ owner 25 PYQ block).
- Statement-level distinctions: post-war dominion status vs immediate freedom; provincial option vs princely states via rulers (✅ owner 25 §2 and §5).
- No option letter is inferred (⚠️ owner rule).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Prelims GS-I Q54 (✅ owner 25 PYQ block).
**Links:** MOD-4006, MOD-4007, MOD-4024.
**Trap:** Cripps did not offer immediate independence; the constitution was to be framed after the war by an elected body.
**Mains hook:** Use it to explain why the Congress rejected the offer and why the League read the province clause as a route to Pakistan.
**Back:** *"Cripps route: body after war, provinces may opt out."*

### Flashcard MOD-4022 — Chronology ladder: 1939 to 1942
**Front:** Give the chronological ladder from the war declaration to the Quit India arrests.
**Definition (learn):** A dated ladder that orders the chain of the 1939–42 crisis.
**Breakdown:**
- 3 Sep 1939 → India declared belligerent (MOD-4002).
- Oct–Nov 1939 → Congress ministries resign (MOD-4003).
- 22 Dec 1939 → League's Deliverance Day (MOD-4003).
- Aug 1940 → August Offer rejected (MOD-4004).
- Oct 1940 → Individual Satyagraha; Vinoba Bhave, then Nehru (MOD-4005).
- Mar–Apr 1942 → Cripps Mission; rejected (MOD-4006, MOD-4007).
- 8 Aug 1942 → Quit India resolution, Bombay; "Do or Die" (MOD-4009, MOD-4010).
- 9 Aug 1942 → leadership arrested; upsurge (MOD-4010).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25 mini-timeline and §9 date control).
**Links:** MOD-4001, MOD-4002, MOD-4003, MOD-4004, MOD-4005, MOD-4006, MOD-4009, MOD-4010.
**Trap:** Keep 8 August (resolution) and 9 August (arrests) distinct; keep Cripps (March–April 1942) before both.
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to frame "events that led to Quit India" in 150 words.
**Back:** *"Blockade of consent, offers refused, Cripps, then eight-nine August."*

### Flashcard MOD-4023 — Concept (advanced): spontaneity vs organisation in Quit India
**Front:** Was Quit India spontaneous or organised, according to the advanced owner's historiography?
**Definition (learn):** The advanced owner frames a debate: the nationalist reading stresses Congress's moral call and underground continuity, while historians such as Gyanendra Pandey stress local initiative and popular agency.
**Breakdown:**
- Nationalist reading: centrally proclaimed movement with underground continuity (✅ owner 25 adv historiography, as stated).
- Local-agency reading: locally improvised revolt against colonial authority (⚠️ owner historiography note, attributed to Pandey).
- Balanced answer: both centrally proclaimed and locally improvised (⚠️ owner's recommended synthesis).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner attribution to Gyanendra Pandey (⚠️ not verified by fetch; attribute as owner's citation).
**Links:** MOD-4011, MOD-4012, MOD-4019.
**Trap:** "Quit India was spontaneous" is a half-truth; "Quit India was planned from Bombay" is also a half-truth.
**Mains hook:** "Quit India was spontaneous, but not unorganised." Use the parallel governments and underground networks.
**Back:** *"Called from the top, lived from the bottom."*

### Flashcard MOD-4024 — Comparison pair: August Offer 1940 vs Cripps Mission 1942
**Front:** How does the August Offer of 1940 differ from the Cripps proposals of 1942?
**Definition (learn):** Both were wartime constitutional offers that promised future dominion status without immediate responsible government. They differ in author, date, content and in the minority or province mechanism.
**Breakdown:**
- Author: Linlithgow (August Offer) vs Stafford Cripps (Cripps Mission).
- Date: August 1940 vs March–April 1942.
- Content: August Offer = expansion of Viceroy's Executive Council, minority safeguard; Cripps = constituent body and province opt-out.
- Reaction: Congress rejected both; Gandhi's "post-dated cheque" is linked to Cripps.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 25 §2, §8.11).
**Links:** MOD-4004, MOD-4006, MOD-4007.
**Trap:** Not the same event: the August Offer does not contain the Cripps province clause; the "post-dated cheque" remark does not belong to the August Offer.
**Mains hook:** Show the progression from minority veto (1940) to provincial opt-out (1942) as the British working through communal mechanisms.
**Back:** *"August gave a veto; Cripps gave an exit."*

### GROUP — Post-War Upsurge: INA, RIN Mutiny & the Cabinet Mission

### Flashcard MOD-4025 — Concept: the post-war upsurge and the loyalty of the coercive base
**Front:** Why did the INA trials, the RIN revolt and the 1945–46 elections matter more for British calculation than their military size?
**Definition (learn):** The post-war upsurge (1945–46) tested whether the Raj could still rely on Indian soldiers, sailors, police and officials. The INA trials and the RIN revolt showed that the coercive base was unreliable, even though neither event defeated the British militarily.
**Breakdown:**
- Coercive base: the Raj had always governed through Indian personnel in the army and police (⚠️ owner's collaboration-base argument, basic 26 §8.9).
- Symbolic shock: INA trials turned defeated soldiers into cross-communal national heroes (✅ owner 26 adv §1).
- Armed-force unrest: RIN revolt in February 1946 (✅ owner 26 basic §1).
- Owner's weighting: loyalty collapse ranks highest; INA and RIN are moderate but sharply timed (⚠️ owner 26 §8.9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Post-war sequence 1945–46: Simla (1945), INA trials (Nov 1945–May 1946), elections (winter 1945–46), RIN revolt (Feb 1946), Cabinet Mission (1946) (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** MOD-4031, MOD-4034, MOD-4035, MOD-4051.
**Trap:** INA and RIN did not force British withdrawal alone; they intensified a crisis already shaped by mass nationalism and British exhaustion (✅ owner 26 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** Use the "collaboration base" thesis to argue that the decisive change between 1942 and 1946 was the reliability of the instruments of rule.
**Back:** *"The guns stayed loaded but the hands stopped being sure."*

### Flashcard MOD-4026 — The first INA (1942): Mohan Singh and Rash Behari Bose
**Front:** What was the first Indian National Army, who founded it, and how did it end?
**Definition (learn):** The first INA emerged in 1942 from Indian prisoners of war, with Japanese support, under Mohan Singh and Rash Behari Bose. It was an early experiment that collapsed before Bose's reorganisation.
**Breakdown:**
- Founders: Mohan Singh and Rash Behari Bose (✅ owner 26 basic §2).
- Support: Japanese (✅ owner 26).
- Dissolution: Mohan Singh disbanded it in December 1942 after disputes with the Japanese; troops were sent back to POW camps (✅ Wikipedia: Indian National Army).
- Aftermath: M.Z. Kiani took command on 15 February 1943 (✅ Wikipedia: Indian National Army).
- Owner's version: the first experiment collapsed by early 1943 when Mohan Singh pressed for autonomy and was removed and arrested (✅ owner 26 adv §8.4); the disbanding date is Dec 1942 per Wikipedia, so both statements agree on the collapse.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1942 (founding), December 1942 (disbanded) (✅ Wikipedia), 15 February 1943 Kiani (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-4027, MOD-4028, MOD-4033.
**Trap:** Do not credit Bose with the 1942 INA; Bose revived and reorganised it in 1943.
**Mains hook:** The first INA shows that a Japanese-backed Indian army could be formed but not sustained without a leader able to command Indian trust.
**Back:** *"1942 Mohan Singh's army, 1943 Bose's army: different founders, same name."*

### Flashcard MOD-4027 — Subhas Chandra Bose's journey to the INA (1941–43)
**Front:** What route did Subhas Chandra Bose take from his 1941 escape to leadership of the INA?
**Definition (learn):** Bose left Calcutta in disguise in January 1941, travelled via Kabul to Berlin, reached East Asia in 1943, and took over the INA and the Indian Independence League.
**Breakdown:**
- Background: resigned the Congress presidency after Tripuri (1939), founded the Forward Bloc (✅ owner 26 adv §8.4).
- Escape: left Calcutta in disguise on the night of 16–17 January 1941, via Kabul to Berlin (✅ owner 26 adv §8.4).
- Germany to Asia: left Germany 8 February 1943; reached Tokyo 11 May 1943 (✅ Wikipedia: Indian National Army).
- Singapore: arrived July 1943; on 4 July 1943 took leadership of the Indian Independence League and the Second INA at the Cathay Building (✅ Wikipedia: Indian National Army).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 16–17 January 1941 (owner); 8 February 1943 (Wikipedia); 11 May 1943 Tokyo (Wikipedia); July 1943 Singapore (Wikipedia); 4 July 1943 (Wikipedia). The owner says he "reached Southeast Asia in May 1943" ⚠️ — Wikipedia puts Tokyo (East Asia) in May and Singapore in July.
**Links:** MOD-4026, MOD-4028.
**Trap:** Do not confuse Bose's Berlin phase (1941–43) with his Singapore phase (1943–45); the "May 1943" date is Tokyo arrival, not Singapore.
**Mains hook:** Bose converted prisoners into a claimant government; argue this with the Azad Hind structure (next card).
**Back:** *"Calcutta 1941, Berlin, Tokyo May 1943, Singapore July 1943."*

### Flashcard MOD-4028 — Provisional Government of Azad Hind (1943)
**Front:** What was the Provisional Government of Azad Hind, when and where was it proclaimed, and what did it do?
**Definition (learn):** Bose proclaimed the Provisional Government of Azad Hind (Free India) at Singapore in October 1943. It had an army, a cabinet and a territorial ambition, and it declared war on Britain and the United States.
**Breakdown:**
- Proclamation: October 1943, Singapore (✅ owner 26 §2, §8.4; Wikipedia gives only the month).
- Declaration of war on Britain and the United States: 23 October 1943 (✅ Wikipedia: Indian National Army).
- Structure: army, cabinet, claimant-government character (✅ owner 26 adv §8.4).
- Recognition: owner says recognised by Japan and later by eight other governments (⚠️ not verified by the fetched text).
- Slogans: "Dilli Chalo" and "Give me blood and I will give you freedom" (✅ owner 26 basic §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** October 1943 (✅ owner, Wikipedia); 23 October 1943 declaration of war (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-4027, MOD-4029, MOD-4030.
**Trap:** The Provisional Government was a claimant government set up under Japanese-backed arrangements in Singapore; it was not India's recognised government in the Allied sense, and the "eight governments" recognition detail is ⚠️ unverified.
**Mains hook:** Bose's contribution was to make the INA a political claimant, which is why the trials could not be treated as ordinary courts-martial.
**Back:** *"October 1943 at Singapore: a government with an army, and a war declared on London and Washington."*

### Flashcard MOD-4029 — INA symbols: slogans, the Rani of Jhansi Regiment and the Andaman names
**Front:** Which symbols of the INA are the most examinable, and what does each stand for?
**Definition (learn):** The INA's symbols were its slogans, its women's regiment and the renaming of the Andaman and Nicobar islands under the Azad Hind administration.
**Breakdown:**
- "Dilli Chalo": march to Delhi (✅ owner 26 §2, §8.4).
- "Give me blood and I will give you freedom": Bose's slogan (✅ owner 26 §1).
- Rani of Jhansi Regiment: all-female unit under Lakshmi Sahgal; named after Queen Lakshmibai of 1857; drew women volunteers from Malaya and Burma; withdrew from Rangoon to Singapore in 1945 (✅ Wikipedia: Indian National Army).
- Andaman and Nicobar renamed "Shaheed" and "Swaraj" under the Azad Hind setup (✅ owner 26 §4; ⚠️ not confirmed by fetched text).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rani of Jhansi Regiment under Lakshmi Sahgal (✅ owner, Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-4028, MOD-4031.
**Trap:** Lakshmi Sahgal leads the women's regiment; do not confuse her with the Red Fort accused Prem Sahgal (MOD-4031).
**Mains hook:** The women's regiment represented women's participation in militant nationalism; use it as a point on gender in the freedom struggle.
**Back:** *"Dilli Chalo, Rani's regiment, Shaheed and Swaraj islands."*

### Flashcard MOD-4030 — Imphal–Kohima campaign (1944) and the INA's military failure
**Front:** When was the Imphal campaign launched, what was its outcome, and why does it matter for the INA's political meaning?
**Definition (learn):** The INA's military campaign toward Imphal–Kohima in 1944 was launched with Japanese forces and failed. The defeat made the INA's later significance political rather than military.
**Breakdown:**
- Start: Imphal campaign launched 8 March 1944 (✅ owner 26 adv §8.4); Wikipedia confirms 1944 and the Japanese U-Go offensive toward Manipur (✅ Wikipedia: Indian National Army).
- Early success: INA units took Moirang in early April 1944 (✅ Wikipedia: Indian National Army).
- Failure: Imphal–Kohima failed (✅ owner 26).
- Meaning: the defeat did not end the INA's political impact; it was turned into trial material (MOD-4031).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 8 March 1944 (owner-only date; ✅ owner); April 1944 Moirang (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-4028, MOD-4031, MOD-4033.
**Trap:** The INA did not defeat British forces; "INA won on the battlefield" is wrong (✅ owner 26 §5). Do not state casualty figures (✅ owner 26 §8.12).
**Mains hook:** "The INA lost every battle it fought and won the only trial that mattered" (owner's scaffold, ✅ owner 26 §8.11).
**Back:** *"Imphal lost on the map, won in the courtroom."*

### Flashcard MOD-4031 — The Red Fort trials (1945–46)
**Front:** When were the INA trials held at the Red Fort, who were the first joint accused, and what was the sentence?
**Definition (learn):** The Red Fort courts-martial of INA officers were held publicly between November 1945 and May 1946. The first joint trial involved Shah Nawaz Khan, Prem Sahgal and Gurbaksh Singh Dhillon, a Muslim, a Hindu and a Sikh.
**Breakdown:**
- Period: roughly ten courts-martial, November 1945 to May 1946 (✅ Wikipedia: Indian National Army).
- Accused: Shah Nawaz Khan, Prem Sahgal, Gurbaksh Singh Dhillon (✅ owner 26; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Sentence: deportation for life; never carried out (✅ Wikipedia: Indian National Army).
- Effect: cross-communal sympathy; government retreat from prosecutions (⚠️ owner's interpretation; the Commander-in-Chief's and NWFP Governor's remarks are owner-cited, ✅ owner 26 adv §8.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Nov 1945–May 1946 (✅ Wikipedia); accused names (✅ owner, Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-4029, MOD-4032, MOD-4025.
**Trap:** Prem Sahgal (Red Fort accused) ≠ Lakshmi Sahgal (Rani of Jhansi Regiment). Do not state the trials were ordinary courts-martial in the owner's view.
**Mains hook:** The trials created a national symbol because the accused's composition made the prosecution look like punishment of patriotism.
**Back:** *"Three names, three faiths, one courtroom: Shah Nawaz, Prem, Dhillon."*

### Flashcard MOD-4032 — INA defence team and the INA Relief and Enquiry Committee
**Front:** Who defended the INA officers, and what organisation supported them?
**Definition (learn):** The INA defence was led by a team of Congress-associated lawyers and politicians, and the Congress organised an INA Relief and Enquiry Committee.
**Breakdown:**
- Defence: Bhulabhai Desai, Tej Bahadur Sapru, K.N. Katju, Jawaharlal Nehru and Asaf Ali (✅ owner 26 adv §8.4).
- Owner's basic list: Bhulabhai Desai, Sapru, Nehru, Asaf Ali and Katju associated with defence (✅ owner 26 §2).
- Organisation: INA Relief and Enquiry Committee (✅ owner 26 adv §8.4).
- Owner's note: the Congress took up the INA cause after the fact and made it an election plank; S. Gopal called it "a touch of escapism" (⚠️ attributed to S. Gopal by owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Defence names (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4031, MOD-4034, MOD-4041.
**Trap:** Do not present the defence team as the INA's founders; they defended the officers.
**Mains hook:** Use the defence team to show the Congress's mobilisation of the INA cause in the 1945–46 campaign.
**Back:** *"Desai, Sapru, Katju, Nehru, Asaf Ali: the defence bench."*

### Flashcard MOD-4033 — Trap: INA's military failure vs political success
**Front:** How should a candidate state the INA's significance without overstating it?
**Definition (learn):** The INA's significance is political: it failed militarily and succeeded symbolically through the trials and cross-communal sympathy.
**Breakdown:**
- Military: campaign failed at Imphal–Kohima (✅ owner 26 §1).
- Political: trials created public sympathy (✅ owner 26 §1).
- Limit: historians differ on whether INA agitation changed imperial policy; P.S. Gupta suggested a contribution to the Cabinet Mission decision (⚠️ owner attribution, not verified).
- Caution: do not state INA strength or casualty figures (✅ owner 26 adv §8.12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ "around forty thousand men by 1945" recruitment is attested in Bandyopadhyay per the owner; attribute only, do not use casualty figures (✅ owner 26 adv §8.4).
**Links:** MOD-4030, MOD-4031, MOD-4025.
**Trap:** "INA won India's independence on the battlefield" is wrong (✅ owner 26 §5).
**Mains hook:** The INA was defeated in battle but turned its defeat into political energy; qualify whether that energy changed British policy.
**Back:** *"Battles lost, trials won: the INA's arithmetic."*

### Flashcard MOD-4034 — RIN mutiny (February 1946): HMIS Talwar and the naval revolt
**Front:** What was the Royal Indian Navy revolt of February 1946, where did it begin, and how far did it spread?
**Definition (learn):** The Royal Indian Navy ratings' revolt began in February 1946 at HMIS Talwar in Bombay and spread to ships and shore establishments. It was serious but short-lived and urban-centred.
**Breakdown:**
- Start: HMIS Talwar, Bombay (✅ owner 26 §1, §8.5).
- Spread: ships and shore establishments (✅ owner 26).
- Character: not a coordinated national military revolution; national parties did not fully own it (✅ owner 26 §8.5).
- Significance: for a regime built to prevent Indian solidarity in the ranks, a naval revolt backed by urban crowds signalled failure of the security doctrine (✅ owner 26 §8.5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** February 1946 (✅ owner 26); HMIS Talwar (✅ owner 26); do not state the number of ships (✅ owner 26 §8.12).
**Links:** MOD-4025, MOD-4035, MOD-4041.
**Trap:** RIN Mutiny was not a Congress-led movement and did not directly cause the Mission's dispatch (✅ owner 26 §5; §9).
**Mains hook:** Use the RIN revolt to illustrate the armed-force loyalty question and then qualify that it was not a national revolution.
**Back:** *"February 1946, HMIS Talwar: the navy stopped obeying, briefly."*

### Flashcard MOD-4035 — Comparison: 1857 and 1946 armed disaffection
**Front:** How do the 1857 revolt and the 1946 armed-forces unrest differ in composition, politics, civilian link and outcome?
**Definition (learn):** Both were armed-force disaffection episodes against the Raj, but 1857 was restorationist and regionally concentrated while 1946 was nationalist, naval and urban.
**Breakdown:**
- Composition: 1857 Bengal army sepoys, regionally concentrated; 1946 naval ratings across establishments, urban support.
- Politics: 1857 restorationist (Mughal, Peshwa, taluqdari); 1946 nationalist (INA symbols, tricolour, freedom slogans).
- Civilian link: 1857 deep in Awadh and the Doab; 1946 urban strikes and crowds, no rural base.
- Duration/outcome: 1857 prolonged, suppressed by force; 1946 days, settled by negotiation.
- Effect: 1857 restructured the empire to continue; 1946 convinced government continuation was unaffordable (✅ owner 26 basic §8.5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 26 basic §8.5 comparison table).
**Links:** MOD-4034, MOD-4011, MOD-4025.
**Trap:** Don't equate the RIN revolt with 1857; 1857 is a restorationist revolt while 1946 is shorter, urban and nationalist.
**Mains hook:** Compare the two as "loyalty tests" of the same coercive base, and note the outcome shift.
**Back:** *"1857 restored old masters; 1946 refused new orders."*

### Flashcard MOD-4036 — C.R. Formula (April 1944)
**Front:** What did the C.R. Formula of April 1944 propose, and why did it matter?
**Definition (learn):** The C.R. Formula, proposed by C. Rajagopalachari in April 1944, offered a post-war plebiscite in contiguous Muslim-majority districts on Pakistan, with mutual arrangements for essential services and implementation after the transfer of power.
**Breakdown:**
- Proposer: C. Rajagopalachari (April 1944).
- Mechanism: a commission to demarcate contiguous Muslim-majority districts; a plebiscite of the adult population; border districts could choose either state.
- Joint arrangements: essential services such as defence and communications.
- Timing: implementation deferred until after full transfer of power (✅ owner 26 basic §2, §8.6).
- Status: not an official Congress offer (✅ owner 26 adv §8.6; ⚠️ not verified by fetch).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** April 1944 (✅ owner); July 1944 Gandhi proposed talks on the "Rajaji formula" (✅ owner 26 basic §8.6).
**Links:** MOD-4037, MOD-4038, MOD-4027.
**Trap:** The formula was not an official Congress offer; the plebiscite was only in contiguous Muslim-majority districts and after transfer of power (✅ owner 26 §8.12).
**Mains hook:** Argue that the formula showed the Congress leadership's willingness to contemplate partition in 1944, so 1947 was not a sudden capitulation (owner's argument).
**Back:** *"Rajaji 1944: a district plebiscite after the British go."*

### Flashcard MOD-4037 — Gandhi–Jinnah talks (July–September 1944)
**Front:** Why did the Gandhi–Jinnah talks of 1944 break down?
**Definition (learn):** Gandhi and Jinnah negotiated on the basis of the C.R. Formula from July 1944; the talks broke down in September 1944 because they disagreed about what separation meant.
**Breakdown:**
- Start: Gandhi opened talks with Jinnah in July 1944 on the Rajaji basis (✅ owner 26 basic §1).
- Breakdown: September 1944 (✅ owner 26).
- Gandhi's view: separation as division within the family, retaining elements of partnership (✅ owner 26).
- Jinnah's view: complete dissolution with sovereignty (✅ owner 26).
- Owner's three objections: truncated territory; implementation deferred; district-by-district consent instead of recognition of Muslims as a nation (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** July–September 1944 (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** MOD-4036, MOD-4038, MOD-4049.
**Trap:** The breakdown was about the mechanism and scale of separation, not the principle of separation (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Mains hook:** Use the 1944 failure to argue that the obstacle to unity was the mechanism of minority security, not only the leaders' personality.
**Back:** *"Family divorce versus full sovereignty: the talks ended in 1944."*

### Flashcard MOD-4038 — Wavell Plan and the Simla Conference (25 June–14 July 1945)
**Front:** What was the Wavell Plan and why did the Simla Conference collapse?
**Definition (learn):** Viceroy Wavell convened the Simla Conference (25 June–14 July 1945) to form an entirely Indian Executive Council, with only the Viceroy and Commander-in-Chief as British members, equal caste Hindu and Muslim representation and separate SC representation. It collapsed over the League's claim to nominate all Muslim members.
**Breakdown:**
- Convener: Lord Wavell, who replaced Linlithgow in 1943 (✅ owner 26 basic §4).
- Design: entirely Indian Executive Council; equal caste Hindu and Muslim seats; separate SC representation (✅ owner 26 basic §1).
- Collapse: Jinnah demanded parity and the League's exclusive right to nominate all Muslim members; Congress refused (✅ owner 26).
- Congress president at that time: Maulana Abul Kalam Azad (✅ owner 26).
- Result: Wavell called off the conference (✅ owner 26 adv §8.6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 25 June–14 July 1945 (✅ owner 26). Wavell's March 1945 London visit and Churchill's view are owner-only (✅ owner 26 adv §8.6).
**Links:** MOD-4036, MOD-4039, MOD-4041, MOD-4049.
**Trap:** Simla failed on representation, not on constitutional substance; do not present Wavell as a partitionist (✅ owner 26 adv §8.6).
**Mains hook:** Simla shows the veto had passed to the League: the British were willing to Indianise the executive, and one party's claim to a monopoly of Muslim representation was enough to stop it.
**Back:** *"Simla 1945: the British offered an Indian cabinet, Jinnah demanded the Muslim seats."*

### Flashcard MOD-4039 — Elections of 1945–46: vote shares and seat results
**Front:** What were the key results of the 1945–46 elections, and what did they change in bargaining power?
**Definition (learn):** The winter 1945–46 elections gave Congress a sweep of the general constituencies and the League a sweep of the Muslim constituencies. The result certified two mandates in separate electorates, without settling the territory of Pakistan.
**Breakdown:**
- Congress: over 90 per cent of general seats provincially, including 23 of 36 labour seats; 80.9 per cent of general-constituency votes (all-India) (✅ owner 26 basic §1, §8.7).
- League: 74.7 per cent of votes in Muslim constituencies (all-India) (✅ owner 26 §8.7).
- Franchise: heavily restricted, about 10 per cent of the population (✅ owner; ✅ Wikipedia: 1946 Indian provincial elections gives "less than 10%" eligible).
- Other parties: Communist Party 8 seats, Hindu Mahasabha 3, SCF 2 of 151 reserved (✅ owner 26 §8.7).
- Effect: Congress lost its argument that the League did not represent Muslims; the League became the only constitutionally certified Muslim representative (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Winter 1945–46 (✅ owner); 80.9% and 74.7% (✅ owner). Owner's Muslim seats remark "13% of Muslims allowed to vote" is Wikipedia's (✅ Wikipedia: 1946 Indian provincial elections).
**Links:** MOD-4040, MOD-4041, MOD-4038, MOD-4042.
**Trap:** Do not convert vote share into seat share; do not state central-legislature seat totals (⚠️ not supported by the owners).
**Mains hook:** Mandate, not inevitability: the elections changed bargaining power without deciding the territory.
**Back:** *"Congress swept the general seats; the League swept the Muslim seats; ten per cent voted."*

### Flashcard MOD-4040 — Bengal and Punjab seat figures: owner vs source conflict
**Front:** What are the Bengal and Punjab seat totals in the 1946 elections, and which figure should a candidate use?
**Definition (learn):** The owners give Bengal's League total as 119 of 250 and Punjab's League total as 75 of 175; a fetched table gives Bengal's League total as 113 and Punjab's League as 73, with the Unionist total differing.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner (basic 26 §8.7): Bengal League 93 per cent of Muslim votes and 119 of 250 seats; Punjab League 75, Congress 51, Akalis 22, Unionist 18 (✅ owner).
- Fetched table (✅ Wikipedia: 1946 Indian provincial elections): Bengal League 113, Congress 86, others 51; Punjab League 73, Congress 51, Akalis 22, Unionist 20, Ahrar 2, independents 7. The same article's text also says 75 League and 43 Congress for Punjab (internal inconsistency).
- Verdict: Bengal figure conflicts (⚠️ 119 vs 113); Punjab Unionist count differs (⚠️ 18 vs 20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ Do not state 119 or 113 without checking a primary source; use "a plurality of 250" if needed (⚠️ advice).
**Links:** MOD-4039, MOD-4041.
**Trap:** Owner-vs-source conflict on seat counts is a common MCQ option trap; prefer the vote shares given in §8.7 unless verified.
**Mains hook:** Use the Punjab exclusion (League kept out of office despite the most seats) as the qualification rather than exact seat numbers.
**Back:** *"Bengal 113 or 119? Check before you quote."*

### Flashcard MOD-4041 — Trap: the 1945–46 elections were not a plebiscite for Partition
**Front:** Why is it wrong to call the 1945–46 elections a referendum or plebiscite on Partition?
**Definition (learn):** The poll used separate electorates on a restricted franchise and did not place any territorial question on the ballot. The League's plebiscite framing was a claim about its campaign, not the poll's constitutional character.
**Breakdown:**
- Franchise: restricted; not universal suffrage (✅ owner 26 §8.7).
- Question: no boundary, constitution or population arrangement was on the ballot (⚠️ owner analysis).
- Framing: Anita Inder Singh reports that the League presented the elections as a plebiscite for Pakistan (✅ owner 26 §8.7, attributed).
- Ayesha Jalal: the demand was never coherently defined before 1946 (✅ owner 26 §8.7, attributed).
- Unity survived: both parties accepted the Mission scheme in June–July 1946 (✅ owner 26 §8.10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Franchise about 10 per cent (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4039, MOD-4046, MOD-4052.
**Trap:** The "elections made Partition inevitable" option is wrong as stated; "elections changed bargaining power" is the defensible version (✅ owner 26 §8.2).
**Mains hook:** Answer "Did the 1945–46 elections make Partition inevitable?" with result → what it changed → what it did not settle → qualified verdict.
**Back:** *"A tenth of the people voted for seats, not for a border."*

### Flashcard MOD-4042 — Cabinet Mission (March–June 1946): members, arrival and plan date
**Front:** Who were the Cabinet Mission members, when did they reach Delhi, and when was the plan announced?
**Definition (learn):** The Cabinet Mission of 1946 was a British ministerial mission to India to devise a constitutional settlement. It consisted of Lord Pethick-Lawrence, Sir Stafford Cripps and A.V. Alexander.
**Breakdown:**
- Members: Pethick-Lawrence (Secretary of State for India), Cripps (President of the Board of Trade), A.V. Alexander (First Lord of the Admiralty) (✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission; ✅ owner 26).
- Arrival: reached New Delhi on 24 March 1946 (✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission).
- Plan: announced 16 May 1946 (✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission; ✅ owner 26 §9).
- Outcome: rejected Pakistan but proposed grouping, a weak centre, a Constituent Assembly and an Interim Government (✅ owner 26 §8.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 24 March 1946 (Wikipedia); 16 May 1946 (Wikipedia/owner); Attlee's 15 March 1946 statement is a different event (MOD-4049).
**Links:** MOD-4043, MOD-4044, MOD-4045, MOD-4008.
**Trap:** The Mission rejected Pakistan; it did not create the Constitution or propose Partition (✅ owner 26 §5, §8.12).
**Mains hook:** "The Cabinet Mission was the last serious attempt to preserve a united India" — argue the claim and name the grouping ambiguity.
**Back:** *"Pethick-Lawrence, Cripps, Alexander: March 1946 in, May 1946 plan."*

### Flashcard MOD-4043 — Cabinet Mission plan structure: Union, groups and sections A, B, C
**Front:** How was the Cabinet Mission's three-tier structure organised, and which provinces formed Sections A, B and C?
**Definition (learn):** The plan offered a Union at the top with limited subjects, provincial groups in the middle and provinces at the bottom. Provinces were grouped into Sections A, B and C.
**Breakdown:**
- Union: defence, foreign affairs and communications; Wikipedia also cites currency-related finance (✅ owner 26 adv §1; ✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission).
- Section A: Madras, Bombay, United Provinces, Bihar, C.P. and Berar, Orissa (✅ owner 26 §8.7; ✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission).
- Section B: Punjab, NWFP, Sind (owner); Wikipedia adds Balochistan (✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission).
- Section C: Bengal and Assam (✅ owner 26; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Provinces retained all remaining powers (✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section letters are standard; keep A (Hindu-majority provinces) and B (Punjab, NWFP, Sind) distinct from C (Bengal, Assam).
**Links:** MOD-4042, MOD-4044.
**Trap:** Grouping is not Partition; grouping was a federal compromise, Partition created two sovereign dominions (✅ owner 26 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** Explain why grouping gave the League a bloc without a state.
**Back:** *"Union three subjects, groups A B C, provinces keep the rest."*

### Flashcard MOD-4044 — Grouping ambiguity: compulsory or optional
**Front:** Why did the Cabinet Mission's grouping clause destroy the July 1946 settlement?
**Definition (learn):** The Mission declared grouping optional but group sections compulsory, which allowed each party to read it in its own way: the League saw compulsory grouping as a Muslim bloc, while the Congress saw an optional arrangement.
**Breakdown:**
- Mission's position: grouping optional, sections compulsory (✅ owner 26 §8.7; §8.12).
- League's reading: compulsory grouping, making a Muslim bloc effective (✅ owner 26 §8.8).
- Congress's reading: provinces not obliged to join a group; Constituent Assembly sovereignty (✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission; ✅ owner 26 adv §5).
- Later: HMG accepted the League's interpretation on 6 December 1946 (MOD-4048).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 26 §8.8; the Mission phrasing "deliberately quibbled" is owner-quoted).
**Links:** MOD-4043, MOD-4045, MOD-4048, MOD-4055.
**Trap:** The owner's qualification: ambiguity explains the July rupture but not the complete breakdown (MOD-4055).
**Mains hook:** Use the ambiguity thesis with the qualification to score on counter-evidence.
**Back:** *"Optional groups, compulsory sections: both sides heard what they wanted."*

### Flashcard MOD-4045 — League's acceptance (6 June) → Nehru's statement → League's withdrawal (29 July 1946)
**Front:** What were the sequence and dates from the League's acceptance to its withdrawal of July 1946?
**Definition (learn):** The League accepted the Cabinet Mission plan on 6 June 1946, Nehru's statement that Congress was not bound by the grouping clause came in July, and the League rescinded its acceptance on 29 July 1946.
**Breakdown:**
- 6 June 1946: League Council accepted the proposals, after Wavell's assurance of a place in the interim government if Congress refused (✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission).
- July 1946: Nehru's statement that the Congress was "not bound by a single thing" except going into the Assembly. Owner dates it 7 July (AICC speech); Wikipedia dates the speech 10 July (⚠️ owner-vs-source conflict).
- 29 July 1946: League rescinded its acceptance (✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission; ✅ owner 26 adv §8.7).
- Next: Direct Action (MOD-4046).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 6 June; 7 or 10 July (conflict); 29 July 1946.
**Links:** MOD-4044, MOD-4046, MOD-4047.
**Trap:** Nehru's statement date: check which event the question names — the AICC speech (owner: 7 July) or the Bombay press conference (Wikipedia: 10 July).
**Mains hook:** The July rupture is a dated, named chain; use it in "why did 1945–46 negotiations fail".
**Back:** *"Six June yes, July Nehru's 'not bound', twenty-nine July no."*

### Flashcard MOD-4046 — Direct Action Day (16 August 1946) and Calcutta
**Front:** What was Direct Action Day, when was it, and what followed in Calcutta?
**Definition (learn):** Direct Action Day, called by the Muslim League after its withdrawal from the Cabinet Mission scheme, was observed on 16 August 1946 and led to the Great Calcutta Killings.
**Breakdown:**
- Call: Muslim League political call after constitutional deadlock (✅ owner 26 §5; ✅ owner 26 adv §8.8).
- Date: 16 August 1946 (✅ owner 26 §4).
- Outcome: communal violence in Calcutta, the "Great Calcutta Killings" (✅ owner 26 §1).
- Sequence: DAD preceded the Congress-only Interim Government (2 September 1946) (✅ owner 26 §8.12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 16 August 1946 (✅ owner); Wikipedia's Cabinet Mission page names DAD without its date (✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission).
**Links:** MOD-4045, MOD-4047, MOD-4053.
**Trap:** Direct Action Day was not part of the Mission proposals; it was not a response to the Interim Government (✅ owner 26 §8.12).
**Mains hook:** The chain: election → two mandates → grouping compromise → incompatible readings → League withdrawal → Direct Action → Congress-only interim government.
**Back:** *"Sixteen August: the League's street replaced its pen."*

### Flashcard MOD-4047 — Interim Government (2 September 1946) and the League's entry (26 October 1946)
**Front:** When was the Interim Government formed, who led it, and when did the League join?
**Definition (learn):** The Interim Government was formed on 2 September 1946 with Congress members under Nehru; the League initially stayed out and joined on 26 October 1946 on Jinnah's framing that entry was a continuation of the Pakistan struggle by other means.
**Breakdown:**
- 2 September 1946: Nehru's cabinet installed; Nehru interim prime minister; Liaquat Ali Khan joined as finance minister for the League later (✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission; ✅ owner 26 §2).
- 26 October 1946: League joined, while not accepting the grouping provision in its constitutional sense or the Assembly (✅ owner 26 adv §8.8).
- Jinnah's framing: entry as continuation of Pakistan struggle by other means (✅ owner 26 §8.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2 September 1946; 26 October 1946; Liaquat Ali Khan as finance minister (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-4046, MOD-4048, MOD-4049.
**Trap:** The Interim Government in September 1946 was a Congress-led cabinet; the League's presence came later (October 1946) and was conflictual.
**Mains hook:** Use the League's entry to show that office did not reconcile the sovereign claims.
**Back:** *"Two September: Congress cabinet. Twenty-six October: League in, dispute on."*

### Flashcard MOD-4048 — Constituent Assembly (9 December 1946) and HMG's 6 December decision
**Front:** When did the Constituent Assembly first meet, and what did the British decide on 6 December 1946?
**Definition (learn):** The Constituent Assembly met for the first time on 9 December 1946, which the League boycotted and asked to dissolve. On 6 December 1946 HMG accepted the League's interpretation that grouping was compulsory.
**Breakdown:**
- 6 December 1946: HMG accepted the League's interpretation of compulsory grouping; provinces could not opt out before group constitutions (✅ owner 26 adv §8.8).
- 9 December 1946: Assembly met; League boycotted; demanded dissolution (✅ owner 26 §4).
- Owner's point: even after the ambiguity was resolved in the League's favour, the League did not join (✅ owner 26 adv §8.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 6 and 9 December 1946 (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4044, MOD-4047, MOD-4049, MOD-4055.
**Trap:** The Constituent Assembly is not the Constitution; it framed the Constitution later (✅ owner 26 §5).
**Mains hook:** The final failure shows irreconcilable sovereign claims, not textual ambiguity alone.
**Back:** *"Six December: the British sided with the League; nine December: the League stayed out."*

### Flashcard MOD-4049 — Attlee's two statements: 15 March 1946 vs 20 February 1947
**Front:** What did Attlee say on 15 March 1946 and on 20 February 1947, and why must they not be merged?
**Definition (learn):** On 15 March 1946 Attlee said a minority would not be allowed to veto the majority's progress, marking the British departure from the Simla posture. On 20 February 1947 he announced that Britain would transfer power not later than June 1948.
**Breakdown:**
- 15 March 1946: "a minority will not be allowed to place a veto on the progress of the majority" (✅ owner 26 adv §8.6; ✅ owner 26 §8.12). Preceded the Cabinet Mission.
- 20 February 1947: transfer of power "not later than June 1948" (✅ owner 26; ✅ owner 27).
- Function: 1946 statement rejected the League's veto; 1947 statement set the withdrawal deadline.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15 March 1946; 20 February 1947; June 1948 deadline (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4042, MOD-4060.
**Trap:** "Attlee's statement on veto" and "Attlee's June 1948 deadline" are different statements.
**Mains hook:** Use 1946 for the League-veto logic and 1947 for the timetable that drove partition.
**Back:** *"March 1946: no veto. February 1947: out by June 1948."*

### Flashcard MOD-4050 — Comparison: Nehru Report (1928) vs Cabinet Mission (1946)
**Front:** How did the Nehru Report of 1928 and the Cabinet Mission of 1946 differ as failed settlements?
**Definition (learn):** Both were attempts at a united-India settlement that failed on the mechanism of minority security. One was Indian-authored and the other British.
**Breakdown:**
- Author: Nehru Report 1928 committee chaired by Motilal Nehru; Cabinet Mission British government mission (✅ owner 26 adv §8.8).
- Objective: Nehru Report sought dominion status, fundamental rights and joint electorates; Cabinet Mission sought a united India with weak centre, grouping, Constituent Assembly; Pakistan rejected (✅ owner).
- Minority concession: Nehru Report gave rights guarantees instead of separate electorates; Cabinet Mission offered grouping (✅ owner).
- Failure: Jinnah's Fourteen Points (1929) rejected the Nehru Report; grouping was ambiguous in 1946 (✅ owner).
- Cost: 1928 led to Purna Swaraj at Lahore in 1929; 1946 led to Direct Action Day and partition (✅ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1928; 1929 (Fourteen Points, Purna Swaraj Lahore); 1946 (✅ owner 26 adv).
**Links:** MOD-4043, MOD-4044.
**Trap:** The 1928 failure and 1946 failure differ in stake: 1928 cost a scheme; 1946 cost the constitutional option itself (✅ owner 26 adv §8.8).
**Mains hook:** Argue that between 1928 and 1946 the League's demand escalated from safeguards within India to states outside it.
**Back:** *"1928 lost a scheme; 1946 lost the country's unity."*

### Flashcard MOD-4051 — Weighting the causes of British withdrawal
**Front:** How should an answer rank the causes of British withdrawal in 1947?
**Definition (learn):** The owner ranks six factors: collapse of the collaboration base (highest), post-war British exhaustion, continuous mass nationalism, INA and RIN episodes, international context and constitutional calculation.
**Breakdown:**
- Collaboration base: highest explanatory weight (⚠️ owner).
- British exhaustion: high; Labour government and demobilisation (⚠️ owner).
- Mass nationalism: high cumulative weight of 1920–22, 1930–34, 1942 (⚠️ owner).
- INA and RIN: moderate but sharply timed (⚠️ owner).
- International context: moderate (⚠️ owner).
- Constitutional calculation: moderate; a negotiated transfer preserved British interests (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's weighting is an analytical judgement (⚠️), not a settled historiographical fact.
**Links:** MOD-4025, MOD-4035, MOD-4052.
**Trap:** Do not say INA or RIN alone forced withdrawal (✅ owner 26 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** "Why did the British leave?" must rank factors explicitly and acknowledge the contest among historians.
**Back:** *"Collaboration first, exhaustion next, nationalism always, INA sharpened."*

### Flashcard MOD-4052 — Trap: Cabinet Mission is not Partition and not the Constitution
**Front:** Which three misreadings of the Cabinet Mission should a candidate avoid?
**Definition (learn):** The Cabinet Mission rejected Pakistan, proposed grouping and a Constituent Assembly; it did not partition India and did not write the Constitution.
**Breakdown:**
- Misreading 1: the Mission accepted Pakistan. Correct: it rejected Pakistan (✅ owner 26 §5).
- Misreading 2: the Mission created the Constitution. Correct: it proposed the Constituent Assembly framework; the Assembly drafted the Constitution (✅ owner 26 §5).
- Misreading 3: Direct Action was part of the Mission. Correct: League's call after deadlock (✅ owner 26 §5).
- Misreading 4: the C.R. Formula was a Congress offer. Correct: not official (MOD-4036).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** MOD-4042, MOD-4046, MOD-4036.
**Trap:** Many MCQ options combine these; check the subject of the verb.
**Mains hook:** Use the distinction to structure a "last attempt at unity" answer.
**Back:** *"Mission rejected Pakistan, proposed groups, did not write the Constitution."*

### Flashcard MOD-4053 — Chronology ladder: the 1944–46 negotiation chain
**Front:** Give the ladder of constitutional negotiations from Cripps (1942) to the Interim Government (1946).
**Definition (learn):** A dated ladder for "trace the negotiations from Cripps to Mountbatten" questions, with the sticking point at each step.
**Breakdown:**
- Mar–Apr 1942: Cripps offer (MOD-4006); sticking point: immediate power and province opt-out.
- Apr 1944: C.R. Formula (MOD-4036); sticking point: plebiscite and deferral.
- Jul–Sep 1944: Gandhi–Jinnah talks (MOD-4037); sticking point: separation mechanism.
- 25 Jun–14 Jul 1945: Simla (MOD-4038); sticking point: League monopoly of Muslim nominations.
- Winter 1945–46: elections (MOD-4039); sticking point: two mandates.
- 24 Mar–16 May 1946: Cabinet Mission (MOD-4042); sticking point: grouping.
- 6 Jun–29 Jul 1946: acceptance and withdrawal (MOD-4045).
- 16 Aug 1946: Direct Action (MOD-4046).
- 2 Sep 1946: Interim Government (MOD-4047).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 26 §8.1 sequence and §3 ladder.
**Links:** MOD-4008, MOD-4036, MOD-4037, MOD-4038, MOD-4039, MOD-4042, MOD-4045, MOD-4046, MOD-4047, MOD-4077.
**Trap:** Do not place Direct Action before the League's withdrawal or after the Interim Government.
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to answer "identify the point at which a united settlement became unavailable".
**Back:** *"Cripps, C.R., Gandhi–Jinnah, Simla, polls, Mission, withdrawal, Direct Action, Interim."*

### Flashcard MOD-4054 — PYQ routes: the 1946 transfer and INA questions (2019, 2021)
**Front:** Which verified or routed PYQs connect to the INA, the Cabinet Mission and the transfer of power?
**Definition (learn):** The owners record a 2019 GS-I Q12 Mains demand (15 marks, 250 words) on British imperial power and the transfer of power, a 2019 Prelims Q15 on Indian National Movement leaders, and a 2021 Prelims Q47 on INA officers. Keys are unavailable locally.
**Breakdown:**
- 2019 GS-I Q12: Assess · 15 marks · 250 words; British imperial power and transfer of power (✅ owner 26 adv PYQ block).
- 2019 Prelims Q15: Indian National Movement leaders and organisational positions (routed; key unavailable) (✅ owner 26 basic PYQ block).
- 2021 Prelims Q47: INA officers, colonial India history (routed; key unavailable) (✅ owner 26 basic PYQ block).
- Rule: no option letter is inferred (✅ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed.
**Links:** MOD-4031, MOD-4025, MOD-4053.
**Trap:** Do not guess the 2021 Q47 key; match the stem to the INA officer identities (Sahgal, Dhillon, Shah Nawaz) only if the option names are unambiguous.
**Mains hook:** The 15-mark route: thesis → INA and trials → RIN → Cabinet Mission → verdict.
**Back:** *"Ask about the INA and the transfer: keep the trial names, not the keys."*

### Flashcard MOD-4055 — Concept (advanced): ambiguity is a proximate cause, not the whole breakdown
**Front:** Why does the "ambiguity-collapse" thesis need a qualification after December 1946?
**Definition (learn):** The grouping ambiguity explains the July 1946 rupture but not the final breakdown: HMG resolved the ambiguity in the League's favour on 6 December 1946, yet the League still stayed out of the Assembly, so incompatible sovereign goals were the deeper obstacle.
**Breakdown:**
- Ambiguity thesis: explains July 1946 (✅ owner 26 adv §8.8).
- Counter: HMG accepted the League's interpretation on 6 December 1946 (✅ owner 26 adv §8.8).
- Continued failure: League boycotted the Assembly on 9 December 1946 (✅ owner).
- Owner's recommended qualification sentence (✅ owner 26 adv §8.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 6 December 1946; 9 December 1946; 5 February 1947 Congress nine demand (MOD-4073).
**Links:** MOD-4044, MOD-4048, MOD-4073.
**Trap:** A verdict that makes ambiguity "sole or decisive" cause is weaker than one that adds the qualification.
**Mains hook:** Use the qualification as the counter-evidence paragraph in a 20-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Ambiguity broke July; sovereignty broke December."*

### Flashcard MOD-4056 — Chandra's view of INA and RIN: significance without revolution (advanced)
**Front:** How does the advanced owner assess the INA trials and the RIN revolt in the historiography?
**Definition (learn):** Bipan Chandra gives the INA agitation and RIN revolt major political significance but rejects making the British decision a response to the winter of 1945–46; the INA and RIN sharpened the endgame but did not independently determine the transfer date.
**Breakdown:**
- Significance: sharpened the endgame; crystallised the loyalty question (✅ owner 26 adv historiography note, attributed to Chandra).
- Limit: rejected as sole explanation; urban upsurges brief and limited (✅ owner 26 adv §5).
- Balance: mass struggle and long decline of imperial legitimacy are the stronger causes (✅ owner 26 adv historiography note).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Attributed to Bipan Chandra as stated in the owner (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4033, MOD-4051, MOD-4025.
**Trap:** Do not quote Chandra as saying the INA forced British withdrawal; the owner rejects that reading.
**Mains hook:** Evaluate Chandra's view using the factor weighting: INA/RIN sharpened, not replaced, mass struggle.
**Back:** *"The INA and RIN lit the fuse; they did not build the house."*

### GROUP — Independence & Partition (1946–1947)

### Flashcard MOD-4057 — Concept: Partition as the residual option of a communal-representation system
**Front:** Why was Partition the outcome of 1947, and was it inevitable in 1946?
**Definition (learn):** Partition emerged from a long process in which Indian politics was organised by religious community, followed by the failure of the 1944–46 schemes and the British decision to transfer power quickly. The owners treat it as multi-causal, not as a single decision.
**Breakdown:**
- Long term: separate electorates (1909), their acceptance at Lucknow (1916), extension in 1919 and the Communal Award (1932) (✅ owner 27 §8.4).
- Medium term: 1937 ministries, the Lahore Resolution (March 1940), the Congress's absence from legal politics 1942–45 (✅ owner 27 §8.4).
- Short term: failed negotiations of 1944–45, the 1945–46 elections, the Cabinet Mission's collapse, the accelerated 1947 timetable (✅ owner 27 §8.4).
- Owner's caution: inevitability in 1946 is a disputed counterfactual; both parties accepted the Mission scheme after the elections (✅ owner 27 §8.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1909, 1916, 1919, 1932, March 1940 (✅ owner 27). Interior ladder in MOD-4059.
**Links:** MOD-4059, MOD-4058, MOD-4068, MOD-4072.
**Trap:** "Partition was inevitable from 1906" is wrong (✅ owner 27 adv §5); say "made likely, then unavoidable in negotiation" with dated mechanisms.
**Mains hook:** "Partition was made possible by decades of communal representation, made likely by the 1942–45 vacuum, and made certain by the failure of the Cabinet Mission." (✅ owner 27 §8.9 causal verdict.)
**Back:** *"Structure set the stage, contingency rang the bell."*

### Flashcard MOD-4058 — Lahore Resolution (March 1940): separate states, not a named Pakistan
**Front:** What did the Lahore Resolution of March 1940 demand, and what did it not name?
**Definition (learn):** The Lahore Resolution of March 1940 claimed separate states for Muslim-majority areas; it did not name "Pakistan".
**Breakdown:**
- Date: March 1940 (✅ owner 27 §8.4, §9).
- Demand: separate independent states (✅ owner 27 §8.10).
- Naming: the term "Pakistan" is not in the original resolution (✅ owner 27 §8.10).
- Role: medium-term cause in the owner's three-level structure (✅ owner 27 §8.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** March 1940 (✅ owner 27).
**Links:** MOD-4057, MOD-4059.
**Trap:** The Lahore Resolution is 1940; the Pakistan name is later popular usage (⚠️ owner). Do not treat the resolution as a Partition decision.
**Mains hook:** Use the gap between "separate states" and "Pakistan" to argue that the League's demand was ambiguous until 1946.
**Back:** *"1940 Lahore: states, plural; the name came later."*

### Flashcard MOD-4059 — Causes at three levels: long-term, medium-term, short-term contingency
**Front:** How should a candidate structure the causes of Partition in three levels?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's three-level device separates long-term structure (communal representation), medium-term politics (1937–45) and short-term contingency (1944–47).
**Breakdown:**
- Long-term structure: communal representation; uneven development and elite competition (✅ owner 27 §8.4).
- Medium-term politics: 1937 ministries and League exclusion; Lahore Resolution 1940; Congress's absence 1942–45 (✅ owner 27 §8.4).
- Short-term contingency: Cabinet Mission ambiguity; 1944–45 failed negotiations; 1945–46 elections; communal violence in Noakhali, Bihar and Punjab; accelerated 1947 timetable (✅ owner 27 §8.4; §1).
- Owner's method: this device prevents both "ancient hatred" and "British conspiracy" answers (✅ owner 27 §8.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 27 §8.4 ladder.
**Links:** MOD-4057, MOD-4058, MOD-4039, MOD-4042, MOD-4066, MOD-4077.
**Trap:** Do not collapse the three levels into one; "British policy alone" and "communalism alone" are both partial.
**Mains hook:** Use the three levels to weight causes in a 15 or 20-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Structure, politics, contingency: three floors, one house."*

### Flashcard MOD-4060 — Attlee's 20 February 1947 announcement: transfer by June 1948
**Front:** What did Attlee announce on 20 February 1947, and what did it change?
**Definition (learn):** On 20 February 1947 Attlee announced that British power would be transferred to Indian hands not later than June 1948. The announcement shifted the question from whether to partition to how to partition quickly.
**Breakdown:**
- Date and deadline: 20 February 1947; not later than June 1948 (✅ owner 27 basic §1, §4).
- Effect: accelerated the 1947 timetable and the 3 June Plan (✅ owner 27 basic §1; §8.4 short-term contingency).
- Owner's distinction: 15 March 1946 veto statement is a different event (✅ owner 27 basic §4; MOD-4049).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 20 February 1947 (✅ owner); June 1948 (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4049, MOD-4062, MOD-4063.
**Trap:** Do not merge Attlee's 1946 veto statement with the 1947 transfer deadline (✅ owner 27 basic §4).
**Mains hook:** The 1947 deadline drove the speed of the 3 June Plan; argue that speed heightened violence (⚠️ owner's argument).
**Back:** *"Twenty February 1947: a date on the calendar drives a border."*

### Flashcard MOD-4061 — Lord Mountbatten: last Viceroy (arrived March 1947)
**Front:** Who was the last Viceroy, when did he arrive, and what was his role in the 1947 settlement?
**Definition (learn):** Lord Mountbatten arrived in India in March 1947 as the last Viceroy and presented the 3 June Plan that accepted Partition.
**Breakdown:**
- Office: last Viceroy (✅ owner 27 basic §4).
- Arrival: March 1947 (✅ owner 27).
- Role: presented the 3 June Plan (✅ owner 27 §2).
- Note: the Wikipedia Partition article records Mountbatten's press conference announcing the independence date as 14 August 1947 on 3 June (✅ Wikipedia: Partition of India).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** March 1947 (✅ owner); 3 June 1947 press conference (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-4062, MOD-4060.
**Trap:** Keep "last Viceroy" (the post ended in 1947) distinct from any later office; the owners do not describe his post-August 1947 role, so this card does not rely on it (⚠️ owners silent).
**Mains hook:** Mountbatten's haste is a standard blame point; qualify with the Congress's own acceptance.
**Back:** *"March 1947 in, three June out."*

### Flashcard MOD-4062 — The 3 June Plan (Mountbatten Plan), 1947
**Front:** What did the 3 June Plan provide?
**Definition (learn):** The 3 June 1947 Plan, also called the Mountbatten Plan, accepted Partition, divided Punjab and Bengal, provided for referendums in NWFP and Sylhet, and set up boundary commissions.
**Breakdown:**
- Political decision: accepted Partition (✅ owner 27 §1, §2).
- Division: Punjab and Bengal (✅ owner 27 §2).
- Referendums: NWFP and Sylhet (✅ owner 27 adv §4).
- Boundary commissions: appointed to demarcate (✅ owner 27 §1).
- Date: announced 3 June 1947 (✅ Wikipedia: Partition of India; the press conference also announced 14 August 1947 as the independence date).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 3 June 1947 (✅ owner and Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-4061, MOD-4063, MOD-4065.
**Trap:** The 3 June Plan is a political plan, not an Act of Parliament (✅ owner 27 basic §5, §8.5).
**Mains hook:** "The 3 June Plan decided it, the Independence Act enacted it, and the Radcliffe award located it" (✅ owner 27 §8.9).
**Back:** *"Three June: partition decided, Punjab and Bengal split."*

### Flashcard MOD-4063 — Indian Independence Act 1947: royal assent, commencement and lapse
**Front:** What did the Indian Independence Act 1947 do, and when did it take effect?
**Definition (learn):** The Indian Independence Act 1947 created two dominions, India and Pakistan, from 15 August 1947 and ended British suzerainty over the princely states.
**Breakdown:**
- Passage: Commons voted 15 July 1947; Royal Assent 18 July 1947 (✅ Wikipedia: Indian Independence Act 1947).
- Commencement: 15 August 1947 (✅ Wikipedia: Indian Independence Act 1947; ✅ owner 27).
- Lapse: suzerainty of the Crown over the Indian States lapsed; treaties lapsed (✅ Wikipedia: Indian Independence Act 1947).
- Owner's term: "paramountcy" (British paramountcy lapsed) (✅ owner 27 §2; the Act itself uses "suzerainty").
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15 July, 18 July, 15 August 1947 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-4062, MOD-4078, MOD-4064.
**Trap:** The Act is the legal enactment, the 3 June Plan was the political plan; the Act did not decide the princely question but left it open (✅ owner 27 §8.5; owner 28 §8.4).
**Mains hook:** Distinguish the three 1947 instruments in one paragraph: political plan, legal Act, boundary award.
**Back:** *"Eighteenth of July, Royal Assent; fifteenth of August, two dominions."*

### Flashcard MOD-4064 — Radcliffe and the boundary commissions (Punjab and Bengal)
**Front:** Who was Cyril Radcliffe, what did he chair, and why does the boundary decision matter?
**Definition (learn):** Sir Cyril Radcliffe, a British lawyer, chaired the boundary commissions for Punjab and Bengal, drawing the lines that separated India and Pakistan in those provinces.
**Breakdown:**
- Person: British lawyer, not an Indian leader (✅ owner 27 basic §5, §8.5).
- Task: boundary commissions for Punjab and Bengal (✅ owner 27).
- Timing: hurried award; the owner says the late and hurried award contributed to uncertainty (✅ owner 27 adv §5).
- Date: award announced 17 August 1947 (⚠️ not verified by the fetched text; widely cited but not in owners).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Punjab and Bengal (✅ owner); 17 August 1947 (⚠️).
**Links:** MOD-4065, MOD-4063, MOD-4066.
**Trap:** Radcliffe is not the source of the partition decision; the award is an administrative boundary (✅ owner 27 §8.5).
**Mains hook:** The separation of political decision, legal Act and boundary award created an administrative vacuum in August 1947 (⚠️ owner's argument).
**Back:** *"Radcliffe drew the line; he did not choose the partition."*

### Flashcard MOD-4065 — Three instruments of 1947 compared: political, legal, administrative
**Front:** Which 1947 instrument does each of the 3 June Plan, the Independence Act and the Radcliffe award represent?
**Definition (learn):** The three instruments are distinct acts of authority: a political plan, a legal enactment and an administrative boundary decision.
**Breakdown:**
- 3 June Plan: political plan that set out terms for partition and transfer; not an Act (✅ owner 27 §8.5).
- Indian Independence Act: legal enactment that created dominions from 15 August 1947 and lapsed suzerainty (✅ owner 27 §8.5; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Radcliffe boundary commissions: administrative/technical boundary in Punjab and Bengal (✅ owner 27 §8.5).
- Sequence: plan → Act → boundary (✅ owner 27 §8.5, "the Act followed, and did not precede, the political plan").
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed.
**Links:** MOD-4062, MOD-4063, MOD-4064.
**Trap:** Common MCQ swap: "Act passed by Parliament = 3 June Plan" (wrong) or "Radcliffe = Indian leader" (wrong).
**Mains hook:** Use the three-instrument distinction to open a "Partition" answer with a precise statement of responsibility.
**Back:** *"Plan decides, Act enacts, award locates."*

### Flashcard MOD-4066 — Communal violence 1946–47: Calcutta, Noakhali, Bihar and Punjab
**Front:** Which sites of communal violence between August 1946 and 1947 does the owner name, and what did each do to the political timeline?
**Definition (learn):** After Direct Action Day in Calcutta (16 August 1946), violence spread to Noakhali, Bihar and Punjab in 1946–47; these made a rapid settlement seem more urgent.
**Breakdown:**
- Calcutta: Direct Action Day killings, 16 August 1946 (✅ owner 27 §1).
- Noakhali, Bihar, Punjab: violence in 1946–47 (✅ owner 27 mini-timeline).
- Effect: distrust between Congress, League and communities; quick settlement became urgent (✅ owner 27 §1).
- Gandhi's Bengal work for communal peace (MOD-4070).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner gives no casualty numbers; do not state them (✅ owner 27 §8.8).
**Links:** MOD-4046, MOD-4059, MOD-4070.
**Trap:** Communal violence is not the same as the 1946 Direct Action call, though DAD triggered Calcutta.
**Mains hook:** Violence accelerated the settlement; argue whether it caused partition or only shaped its form (⚠️ owner's argument).
**Back:** *"Calcutta first, then Noakhali, Bihar and Punjab: the timetable chased the violence."*

### Flashcard MOD-4067 — Congress's acceptance of Partition: the strong-centre thesis
**Front:** Why did Congress leaders accept Partition in 1947?
**Definition (learn):** Congress leaders accepted Partition reluctantly to secure a viable, strong central state and to end the communal deadlock and prolonged violence, judging the Cabinet Mission framework unworkable.
**Breakdown:**
- Motive 1: fear of a weak centre under the grouping scheme (✅ owner 27 adv §1).
- Motive 2: end of communal paralysis and prolonged violence (✅ owner 27 adv §1).
- Owner's caution: acceptance was not enthusiasm; the leadership faced a collapsing situation (⚠️ owner 27 §8.7).
- Earlier acceptance: C.R. Formula (1944) showed Congress contemplated separation (MOD-4036).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's argument (⚠️).
**Links:** MOD-4036, MOD-4044, MOD-4068.
**Trap:** Congress "always supported Partition" is wrong (✅ owner 27 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** Evaluate Congress's acceptance as "pragmatic decision rather than ideological surrender" (✅ owner 27 adv §7 Mains angle).
**Back:** *"Accepted with a heavy heart, for a strong centre."*

### Flashcard MOD-4068 — Responsibility for Partition: the actor matrix
**Front:** How should a candidate answer "Who was responsible for Partition?"
**Definition (learn):** The owner's answer rule is to refuse single-actor blame: specify each actor's contribution and constraint.
**Breakdown:**
- British policy: created and extended communal representation; accelerated the 1947 timetable; caution against a British plot (⚠️ owner 27 §8.7).
- Muslim League: converted safeguards into a nationhood claim after 1940; caution about attributing the demand to a community (⚠️ owner 27 §8.7).
- Congress: accepted separate electorates in 1916; underestimated the League after 1937; accepted Partition in 1947 (⚠️ owner 27 §8.7).
- Communal organisations on all sides: hardened boundaries; legitimised violence; agency belongs to leaderships, not communities (⚠️ owner 27 §8.7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's matrix (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** MOD-4057, MOD-4067, MOD-4059.
**Trap:** Do not blame "a religious community" as such; assign agency to organisations and leaderships.
**Mains hook:** The answer is structurally multi-actor; name one contribution and one constraint per actor.
**Back:** *"Blame the structures, the leaders and the organisations, never the community as a whole."*

### Flashcard MOD-4069 — Human cost of Partition: displacement and death estimates
**Front:** What displacement and death estimates should a candidate use, and where do the sources conflict?
**Definition (learn):** The owner says displacement is commonly estimated at about 10–15 million and that death figures must not be quantified precisely. The Wikipedia Partition article gives 12–20 million displaced and 200,000 to 2 million deaths as of 2009.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: displacement about 10–15 million; avoid false precision; deaths contested and qualitative (✅ owner 27 basic §2, §8.6).
- Wikipedia: "12 and 20 million people" displaced along religious lines; deaths estimates "between two hundred thousand and two million" as of 2009 (✅ Wikipedia: Partition of India).
- Conflict: ⚠️ owner's 10–15 million range differs from Wikipedia's 12–20 million; use an explicit range and attribute.
- Owner's 500,000 figure: Chandra's estimate for the communal holocaust (✅ owner 29; see MOD-4102).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12–20 million (Wikipedia); 10–15 million (owner, ⚠️ conflict); 200,000–2 million deaths (Wikipedia 2009).
**Links:** MOD-4102, MOD-4068.
**Trap:** Never give a single exact figure; a candidate who quotes "10 million" as settled is wrong.
**Mains hook:** "Independence as triumph and catastrophe": the human cost is best shown by a range and a qualitative description.
**Back:** *"Ten to twenty million on the road; figures argued, lives counted in the stories."*

### Flashcard MOD-4070 — Gandhi in Bengal at independence (Noakhali and communal peace)
**Front:** Where was Gandhi on 15 August 1947, and why does it matter?
**Definition (learn):** Gandhi was in Bengal, working for communal peace, not in Delhi celebrating independence.
**Breakdown:**
- Location: Bengal, working on communal peace (✅ owner 27 basic §2, §4; ✅ owner 27 adv §4).
- Symbolism: the two registers of 15 August: political triumph in Delhi, communal danger in Bengal (✅ owner 27 basic §8.6).
- Owner's wording: "in Calcutta/Bengal" (✅ owner 27 adv §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15 August 1947 (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4066, MOD-4071.
**Trap:** Do not say Gandhi was in Delhi on 15 August 1947; the owner's point is the opposite (✅ owner 27 basic §5).
**Mains hook:** Use the image to argue that independence was both a political triumph and a humanitarian catastrophe.
**Back:** *"Gandhi was in Bengal, not in the Delhi crowd."*

### Flashcard MOD-4071 — The "Tryst with Destiny" speech (night of 14 August 1947)
**Front:** Who delivered the "Tryst with Destiny" speech, when, and what is its significance?
**Definition (learn):** Jawaharlal Nehru delivered the "Tryst with Destiny" address on the night of 14 August 1947 marking the transfer of power to India.
**Breakdown:**
- Speaker: Nehru (✅ owner 27 §4; ✅ owner 29 §4).
- Timing: night of 14 August, as the owner's basic 29 specifies (✅ owner 29 §4).
- Pairing: Gandhi in Bengal, not in the speech audience (MOD-4070).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 14 August 1947 night (✅ owner 29).
**Links:** MOD-4070, MOD-4074, MOD-4098.
**Trap:** The speech is on the night of 14 August; the legal commencement of the dominions is 15 August (✅ owner 29 §4; ✅ Wikipedia: Indian Independence Act 1947).
**Mains hook:** A speech is not a legal act; separate the rhetorical date from the legal date.
**Back:** *"Fourteen August night: Nehru's tryst; fifteen August: the law begins."*

### Flashcard MOD-4072 — Counterfactual: unity remained constitutionally available until December 1946
**Front:** Why does the owner say unity was still constitutionally available in 1946?
**Definition (learn):** Both parties accepted the Cabinet Mission scheme in some form in June–July 1946, so unity was not closed by the 1945–46 elections alone; it closed only when the grouping interpretations became irreconcilable and the Interim Government was formed without the League.
**Breakdown:**
- League acceptance: 6 June 1946 (✅ owner 27 adv §8.8; ✅ Wikipedia: Cabinet Mission).
- Congress: going into the Constituent Assembly (✅ owner 27 adv §8.8).
- Closing chain: Nehru's statement (7 or 10 July), League withdrawal (29 July), Direct Action (16 August), Congress-only interim government (2 September) (✅ owner 27 adv §8.8).
- Against contingency alone: the Gandhi–Jinnah talks (1944) and Simla (1945) already failed on the same question (✅ owner 27 adv §8.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 27 adv.
**Links:** MOD-4045, MOD-4046, MOD-4047, MOD-4055.
**Trap:** Do not assert that Partition was inevitable from the 1945–46 elections (✅ owner 27 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** Use this as the counter-evidence paragraph: "Mandate, not inevitability".
**Back:** *"Unity survived the polls by a year; it died in the grouping dispute."*

### Flashcard MOD-4073 — Interim Government ungovernable: 5 February 1947 demand
**Front:** What did the nine Congress members of the Interim Government demand on 5 February 1947, and what did it show?
**Definition (learn):** On 5 February 1947 nine Interim Government members from the Congress side formally demanded the resignation of the League ministers, showing that the coalition had become ungovernable from within.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 5 February 1947 (✅ owner 27 basic §2 timeline; ✅ owner 26 basic §1).
- Demand: resignation of League ministers (✅ owner 26 basic §1).
- Result: remaining question was how and when power would be transferred (✅ owner 26 adv endgame table).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 5 February 1947 (✅ owner 26 basic).
**Links:** MOD-4047, MOD-4055, MOD-4060.
**Trap:** Do not say the League resigned in February 1947; the Congress members demanded the League's resignation.
**Mains hook:** The breakdown from within is evidence for "irreconcilable sovereign claims".
**Back:** *"Nine Congress men, five February: resign, League ministers."*

### Flashcard MOD-4074 — Trap: 14 August and 15 August 1947
**Front:** Which date is the independence date in the owner's usage, and why must a candidate be careful with 14 and 15 August?
**Definition (learn):** The owners use 15 August 1947 as the date of independence and the transfer of power; the Wikipedia article records 14 August as the date announced in the 3 June plan and the date of Nehru's address the night before.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: 15 August 1947 is the date India became independent and the dominions were created (✅ owner 27 §4; ✅ Wikipedia: Indian Independence Act 1947, commencement).
- Wikipedia: 3 June press conference announced 14 August as date of independence (✅ Wikipedia: Partition of India).
- Nehru's address: night of 14 August (✅ owner 29 §4).
- Pakistan's Independence Day is observed on 14 August (⚠️ memory-only, not in owners).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 14 August (announced/speech), 15 August (commencement under the Act) (✅ owners and Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-4063, MOD-4071.
**Trap:** Do not state "India became a republic on 15 August 1947" (✅ owner 28 §5; the Republic is 26 January 1950).
**Mains hook:** Distinguish the rhetorical date, the legal date and the observance date.
**Back:** *"Announced fourteenth, commenced fifteenth, republic only in 1950."*

### Flashcard MOD-4075 — PYQ route: Bangla language movement and International Mother Language Day (2021 Prelims Q50)
**Front:** What does the owner say about the 2021 Prelims Q50 route on the Bangla language movement?
**Definition (learn):** The owner records a routed objective demand on International Mother Language Day and the Bangla language movement; no supporting content exists in the source books held locally.
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: International Mother Language Day, Bangla language movement (✅ owner 27 PYQ block).
- Status: routed; official key unavailable locally (✅ owner 27 PYQ block).
- Rule: do not assert dates, events or UNESCO designations from memory (✅ owner 27 adv §10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted; the owner refuses.
**Links:** MOD-4063 (post-1947 East Pakistan is outside this owner's scope).
**Trap:** The answer key is not supplied locally; do not guess.
**Mains hook:** Outside Mains scope in this owner; treat as a single-fact Prelims point.
**Back:** *"Mother-tongue day: a fact to verify, not to guess."*

### Flashcard MOD-4076 — Mains hook: "Partition was inevitable" and the qualified verdict
**Front:** How should a candidate handle "The elections of 1945–46 made Partition inevitable. Critically examine."?
