**Links:** MOD-4142, MOD-4139.
**Trap:** Do not credit Elwin with writing the Panchsheel; the owners say he was an influence (✅ owner 31 adv §4).
**Mains hook:** Use Elwin to show the policy's intellectual sources.
**Back:** *"Elwin's phrase: integrate, but do not impose."*

### Flashcard MOD-4144 — Fifth and Sixth Schedules: two designs for two problems
**Front:** What is the difference between the Fifth and Sixth Schedules, and what other instruments protect tribal areas?
**Definition (learn):** The Sixth Schedule grants autonomous district and regional councils to tribal areas of the North-East; the Fifth Schedule and Tribal Advisory Councils cover other Scheduled Areas where tribal communities were dispersed.
**Breakdown:**
- Sixth Schedule: North-Eastern tribal areas; autonomous district and regional councils (✅ owner 31 basic §1, §2; §8.4 C).
- Fifth Schedule: other Scheduled Areas; Tribal Advisory Councils (✅ owner 31 basic §8.4 C).
- Article 46: state directs protection of tribal interests (✅ owner 31 basic §4; adv §4).
- Other: reserved seats; an SC/ST Commissioner (✅ owner 31 basic §2).
- Logic: self-government where a community is territorially concentrated; protective regulation where it is not (✅ owner 31 basic §8.4 C).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner; Article numbers as cited by owner).
**Links:** MOD-4139, MOD-4141, MOD-4145.
**Trap:** The Fifth and Sixth Schedules are not interchangeable; "Fifth Schedule governs the North-East" is wrong (✅ owner 31 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** Use the design logic to explain why the North-East and central-Indian belt got different instruments.
**Back:** *"Sixth for the hills of the North-East, Fifth for the rest of the Scheduled Areas."*

### Flashcard MOD-4145 — Nagaland (1963) and the Naga rebellion (1955–57)
**Front:** What was the Naga rebellion, who led it, and how did it end in statehood?
**Definition (learn):** The Phizo-led separatist movement in Nagaland formed an "independent government" from 1955–57; the army was deployed in early 1956 and the rebellion's main force was broken by mid-1957; Nagaland became a state in 1963.
**Breakdown:**
- Leader: A.Z. Phizo (✅ owner 31 basic §4).
- Phase: 1955–57 separatists formed an "independent government" (✅ owner 31 basic §1 mini-timeline).
- Military: army deployed early 1956; main force broken by mid-1957 (✅ owner 31 basic §8.4 D).
- Settlement: Nagaland statehood in 1963 (✅ owner 31 basic §2).
- Owner's note: not every North-Eastern conflict settled on the Mizoram model (✅ owner 31 basic §8.4 D).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1955–57; early 1956; mid-1957; 1963 (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4146, MOD-4144, MOD-4152.
**Trap:** Nagaland was not created in 1947; it became a state in 1963 (✅ owner 31 basic §5).
**Mains hook:** Nagaland illustrates force followed by a slow political settlement; qualify with the wider Naga story (⚠️ owner).
**Back:** *"Phizo's government in 1955; statehood in 1963."*

### Flashcard MOD-4146 — Mizo National Front, Laldenga, the Mizo Accord (1986) and statehood (1987)
**Front:** What was the Mizo insurgency, who led it, and how did it end?
**Definition (learn):** The Mizo National Front under Laldenga launched an armed uprising in 1966; the Mizo Accord of 1986 ended it, Laldenga became Chief Minister, and Mizoram became a state in 1987.
**Breakdown:**
- Insurgency: MNF uprising 1966 (✅ owner 31 basic §1, §2).
- Leader: Laldenga (✅ owner 31 basic §2).
- Accord: 1986 (✅ owner 31 basic §4).
- Result: Laldenga became Chief Minister; Mizoram statehood 1987 (✅ owner 31 basic §8.4 D).
- Model: the owner calls Mizoram the model case, not the universal outcome (✅ owner 31 basic §8.4 D).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1966; 1986; 1987 (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4145, MOD-4147, MOD-4153.
**Trap:** The Mizo Accord did not produce independence; "insurgents were granted independence" is wrong (✅ owner 31 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** Mizoram shows insurgent leadership absorbed into constitutional politics; qualify it as a model, not universal.
**Back:** *"Sixty-six the uprising, eighty-six the accord, eighty-seven the state."*

### Flashcard MOD-4147 — North-East reorganisation (1972) and NEFA renamed Arunachal Pradesh (1987 statehood)
**Front:** What reorganisation happened in the North-East in 1972, and when did Arunachal become a state?
**Definition (learn):** In 1972 Meghalaya, Manipur and Tripura were reorganised, and NEFA was renamed Arunachal Pradesh; Arunachal became a state in 1987.
**Breakdown:**
- 1972: Meghalaya, Manipur, Tripura reorganised (✅ owner 31 basic §1, §2, §8.4 D).
- 1972: NEFA renamed Arunachal Pradesh (✅ owner 31 basic §4, adv §4).
- 1987: Arunachal statehood (✅ owner 31 basic §2; adv §4).
- Note: Mizoram attained statehood in 1987 too (MOD-4146).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1972 (reorganisation and rename); 1987 (Arunachal and Mizoram statehood) (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4146, MOD-4144.
**Trap:** Do not confuse 1972 (rename and reorganisation) with 1987 (statehood); the owner's sequence is precise.
**Mains hook:** The North-East was reorganised on tribal and ethnic lines, not linguistic ones (✅ owner 30 adv §8.6).
**Back:** *"Seventy-two: three states reorganised and NEFA renamed; eighty-seven: statehood."*

### Flashcard MOD-4148 — Jharkhand Party (1950, Jaipal Singh) and its evolution to the JMM
**Front:** When was the Jharkhand Party founded, by whom, how did it do in 1952, and what came next?
**Definition (learn):** Jaipal Singh founded the Jharkhand Party in 1950; it won 32 seats in 1952; the Jharkhand movement later passed to the JMM under Shibu Soren.
**Breakdown:**
- Founder: Jaipal Singh (✅ owner 31 basic §2, §4).
- Date: 1950 (✅ owner 31 basic §1).
- 1952 election: 32 seats (✅ owner 31 basic §8.4 E).
- Successor: JMM (Shibu Soren) (✅ owner 31 basic §2).
- Note: the owners do not give the JMM's founding date; do not add it (✅ owner 31 basic §8.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1950; 32 seats in 1952 (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4149, MOD-4150.
**Trap:** "Jharkhand movement stayed narrowly tribal" is wrong (✅ owner 31 basic §5).
**Mains hook:** Use the Jharkhand trajectory to show identity politics adapting to demography.
**Back:** *"Jaipal Singh's party in 1950; Shibu Soren's JMM after."*

### Flashcard MOD-4149 — Jharkhand's demographic turn: from tribal nationalism to regional politics
**Front:** Why did the Jharkhand movement broaden from a tribal demand to a regional one?
**Definition (learn):** As tribals became a local minority in the region they claimed, an exclusively tribal demand became electorally unviable and had to be broadened into a territorial and regional-class platform.
**Breakdown:**
- Demographic fact: tribals a local minority (roughly a third of the region, per owner) (✅ owner 31 adv §1).
- Result: JMM regional and class-based platform (✅ owner 31 adv §1).
- Owner's transferable point: identity politics adapts to demography or loses (✅ owner 31 basic §8.7).
- Limit: do not carry the narrative into 2000 without verified support (✅ owner 31 basic §8.4 E).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ "roughly a third" is the owner's approximation.
**Links:** MOD-4148, MOD-4153.
**Trap:** Do not claim the Jharkhand movement's creation of a state in 2000 from these owners (✅ owner 31 basic §8.4 E).
**Mains hook:** "Demography, not ideology, drove the shift" (⚠️ owner's thesis).
**Back:** *"When the tribe became a minority, the demand became a region."*

### Flashcard MOD-4150 — Chandra's critique: implementation, not design, produced alienation (advanced)
**Front:** What does the advanced owner say caused tribal alienation: design or implementation?
**Definition (learn):** Bipan Chandra's assessment is that the constitutional intent was sound, but implementation was often dismal: neglect, corruption and elite capture, rather than the design, produced alienation.
**Breakdown:**
- Constitutional intent: sound (✅ owner 31 adv §1, attributed to Chandra).
- Implementation failures: neglect, corruption, elite capture (✅ owner 31 adv §1).
- Rival critique: subaltern and rights-based scholars argue the development model itself dispossessed (✅ owner 31 adv historiography note).
- Trap: "Alienation flowed from a flawed Constitution" is wrong per Chandra (✅ owner 31 adv §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner attribution).
**Links:** MOD-4141, MOD-4155, MOD-4154.
**Trap:** Do not present Chandra's view as the only reading; the owner gives the critique too.
**Mains hook:** "To what extent has the constitutional promise to tribals been realised since 1947?" (✅ owner 31 adv §7).
**Back:** *"The promise was written well; it was delivered badly."*

### Flashcard MOD-4151 — Trap: Fifth vs Sixth, Nagaland 1947 and assimilation
**Front:** Which three tribal-policy misreadings does the owner warn against?
**Definition (learn):** The main misreadings are that Nehru favoured assimilation, that Nagaland became a state in 1947 and that the Fifth and Sixth Schedules are the same.
**Breakdown:**
- Misreading 1: policy aimed at assimilation; correct: integration with identity (✅ owner 31 basic §5).
- Misreading 2: Nagaland created 1947; correct: 1963 (✅ owner 31 basic §5).
- Misreading 3: Fifth and Sixth are the same; correct: Sixth for North-East tribal areas, Fifth for other Scheduled Areas (✅ owner 31 basic §5).
- Misreading 4: Fifth Schedule governs North-East; wrong (✅ owner 31 adv §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4139, MOD-4144, MOD-4145.
**Trap:** Options naming the Fifth Schedule for the North-East are wrong.
**Mains hook:** Use the Schedule distinction to open any tribal-autonomy answer.
**Back:** *"Not assimilation, not 1947, not the same schedule."*

### Flashcard MOD-4152 — Trap: Mizoram was not independence and not the universal model
**Front:** Why should a candidate avoid presenting the Mizo settlement as a universal model or as an independence settlement?
**Definition (learn):** The owner says insurgencies in the North-East ended in autonomy or statehood within the Union, not in independence, and that Mizoram is a successful case rather than the universal outcome.
**Breakdown:**
- Settlement type: autonomy or statehood within the Union (✅ owner 31 adv §5).
- Universal claim: not every North-Eastern conflict followed the Mizo model (✅ owner 31 basic §8.4 D).
- Successes: no tribal region seceded (✅ owner 31 basic §8.6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4146, MOD-4145, MOD-4153.
**Trap:** "Nagaland and Mizoram were granted independence" is wrong (✅ owner 31 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** "Every durable settlement followed the same formula: refuse secession, concede self-government" (⚠️ owner's scaffold; qualify).
**Back:** *"Autonomy, not secession; Mizoram is a model, not the rule."*

### Flashcard MOD-4153 — Conflict-resolution engine: insurgency to accommodation
**Front:** What is the owner's causal sequence from land pressure to electoral absorption of insurgent leaders?
**Definition (learn):** The sequence runs from land, forest and outsider pressure through self-rule demands, armed challenge, military containment, negotiated autonomy or statehood and electoral absorption, converting demand from exit to voice.
**Breakdown:**
- Land, forest and outsider pressure → loss of resource control and status (✅ owner 31 basic §8.5).
- Self-rule demand; state treats it as law and order (✅ owner 31 basic §8.5).
- Armed challenge (Naga 1955–57; Mizo from 1966) (✅ owner 31 basic §8.5).
- Military containment without political settlement (✅ owner 31 basic §8.5).
- Negotiation offering autonomy or statehood within the Union (✅ owner 31 basic §8.5).
- Insurgent leadership enters electoral politics (Laldenga, 1986–87) (✅ owner 31 basic §8.5).
- Demand converted from exit to voice (✅ owner 31 basic §8.5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4145, MOD-4146, MOD-4152.
**Trap:** The engine is analytical; do not present it as a dated list of official steps.
**Mains hook:** Use the engine for "insurgency to accommodation" in 15 marks.
**Back:** *"Land, arms, containment, deal, ballot: exit turned into voice."*

### Flashcard MOD-4154 — Developmental shortfall: political accommodation outran material delivery
**Front:** What developmental failures does the owner list after the political settlements?
**Definition (learn):** Political accommodation was not matched by material delivery: displacement by mining, dams and industry, continued land alienation, poor human-development outcomes and long periods of militarised administration.
**Breakdown:**
- Displacement by mining, dams and industry (✅ owner 31 basic §8.6).
- Land alienation and indebtedness (⚠️ owner 31 basic §8.4 F).
- Poor human-development outcomes (✅ owner 31 basic §8.6).
- Long militarised administration before settlements (✅ owner 31 basic §8.6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No contemporary statistics in the owner; do not add them (✅ owner 31 basic §8.8).
**Links:** MOD-4140, MOD-4150, MOD-4155.
**Trap:** The owner's critical paragraph must not become celebratory; the census attribution must accompany the figures.
**Mains hook:** Split verdict: politically successful, developmentally incomplete (✅ owner 31 basic §8.2 split-verdict thesis).
**Back:** *"Identity protected on paper; land and forests lost in practice."*

### Flashcard MOD-4155 — Historiography: developmental displacement and Naxal mobilisation (advanced)
**Front:** How do critics of Chandra's account read tribal integration, and what link does the advanced owner draw?
**Definition (learn):** Subaltern and rights-based scholars argue the development paradigm dispossessed tribes through dams, mines and forests; autonomous councils were captured by elites; and Naxalite mobilisation reflects structural injustice.
**Breakdown:**
- Critique 1: development dispossessed tribes (✅ owner 31 adv historiography note).
- Critique 2: autonomous councils captured by elites (✅ owner 31 adv historiography note).
- Critique 3: Naxalite mobilisation reflects structural injustice (✅ owner 31 adv historiography note; topic 38 link).
- Balanced answer: credit the North-East autonomy model and acknowledge displacement critique (✅ owner 31 adv historiography note).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner's attributed positions).
**Links:** MOD-4150, MOD-4154, MOD-4156.
**Trap:** Do not present the Naxal link as a settled cause; the owner frames it as a competing reading.
**Mains hook:** Pair tribal land and forest questions with Left-extremism prompts to show cause-and-effect across topics.
**Back:** *"Dams took the land; councils took the rest, say critics."*

### Flashcard MOD-4156 — PYQ route: tribal and North-East questions are not Modern History routes
**Front:** Which tribal-integration PYQs route to this owner, and which owners own the others?
**Definition (learn):** The owner records zero direct Modern History routes. Indian Society owns the 2021 and 2022 tribal-society Mains demands, Topic 23 owns the 2023 colonial-response demand and Polity owns the Scheduled Areas objective demands.
**Breakdown:**
- Modern History: zero direct routes (✅ owner 31 basic §9).
- Indian Society: 2021 and 2022 tribal-society Mains demands (✅ owner 31 basic §9).
- Topic 23: 2023 colonial response (✅ owner 31 basic §9).
- Polity: Scheduled Areas objective demands (✅ owner 31 basic §9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner).
**Links:** MOD-4155, MOD-4144.
**Trap:** Do not invent a Modern History PYQ route for tribal integration (✅ owner 31 basic §9).
**Mains hook:** Use the Modern History answer for the historical integration record; leave current schemes and security to other owners.
**Back:** *"No Modern History route; ask the Society and Polity owners."*

### Flashcard MOD-4157 — Chronology ladder: tribal integration and the North-East (1950–1987)
**Front:** Give the dated ladder for tribal safeguards, the North-East and Jharkhand from 1950 to 1987.
**Definition (learn):** The sequence of tribal and North-East settlements in dated steps.
**Breakdown:**
- 1950 → constitutional safeguards (Fifth and Sixth Schedules; Art. 46); Jharkhand Party founded (MOD-4144, MOD-4148).
- Early post-independence years → Nehru's five guidelines (later Tribal Panchsheel) (MOD-4142).
- 1952 → Jharkhand Party wins 32 seats (MOD-4148).
- 1955–57 → Phizo-led separatists; army deployed early 1956; main force broken by mid-1957 (MOD-4145).
- 1963 → Nagaland statehood (MOD-4145).
- 1966 → Mizo National Front uprising (MOD-4146).
- 1972 → Meghalaya, Manipur, Tripura; NEFA renamed Arunachal (MOD-4147).
- 1986 → Mizo Accord; Laldenga becomes Chief Minister (MOD-4146).
- 1987 → Mizoram and Arunachal statehood (MOD-4146, MOD-4147).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 31).
**Links:** MOD-4142, MOD-4144, MOD-4145, MOD-4146, MOD-4147, MOD-4148.
**Trap:** Keep 1972 (reorganisation and rename) apart from 1987 (statehood).
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder for "trace the North-East trajectory" in 250 words.
**Back:** *"Fifty, sixty-three, sixty-six, eighty-six, eighty-seven: from guns to statehood."*

## MODERN INDIAN HISTORY — Part 5: Post-Independence India (Nehru Era to the Millennium)

### GROUP — 32 · The Nehru Era: Hope, Foreign Policy & Legacy

### Flashcard MOD-5001 — Concept: The Nehruvian institutional framework
**Front:** What did the Nehru years build that outlived Nehru, and why is the era judged on institutions and deliveries separately?
**Definition (learn):** The 1947–64 settlement that institutionalised competitive democracy on universal franchise, state-led planning, secular citizenship and non-alignment, even where social outcomes fell short. (✅ owner 32)
**Breakdown:**
- Political: universal adult franchise, competitive elections, a plural Congress
- Economic: Planning Commission and Five Year Plans; mixed economy with a public-sector core
- Foreign: non-alignment as an active, independent judgement on each issue
- Scientific: Atomic Energy Commission, CSIR laboratories, IITs
- Deficits: mass literacy, land-reform delivery, agriculture, party organisation, 1962
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Core era 1950–64 (Planning Commission 1950 to Nehru's death in May 1964). Owner calls Nehru the "architect of modern India" as a considered assessment, not a settled verdict (⚠️ owner 32 §1).
**Links:** MOD-5002, MOD-5003, MOD-5005, MOD-5011, MOD-5019, MOD-5020, MOD-5042.
**Trap:** Framework success is not outcome success. Literacy and land reform were structurally deprioritised, not accidentally neglected.
**Mains hook:** "The Nehru era laid the institutional foundations of modern India — discuss." Thesis: the decisive contribution was institutional rather than programmatic; verdict: institutions built in one decade outlived their creator, while the social investments were postponed.
**Back:** *"Vote, Plan, Science, Stand Apart — built to outlast the builder, delivered unevenly."*

### Flashcard MOD-5002 — Planning Commission (15 March 1950)
**Front:** When and how was the Planning Commission set up, and what is its constitutional status?
**Definition (learn):** The planning body established on 15 March 1950 to frame the Five Year Plans for a self-reliant, mixed economy. (✅ owner 32)
**Breakdown:**
- Created by executive resolution, not by the Constitution
- Therefore extra-constitutional in status
- Its successive Five Year Plans carried the planning strategy of 1951–66
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15 March 1950 (✅ owner 32 §4; date control §9).
**Links:** MOD-5008 (Second Plan), MOD-5023 (trap bundle), MOD-5025 (ladder).
**Trap:** It is not a constitutional body. Do not confuse the 1950 Commission with the Constitution's own bodies.
**Mains hook:** Use it as evidence that planning was a policy choice of the executive, which later made it contestable and reversible in 1991.
**Back:** *"March 1950, by resolution not by Article — planning by executive order."*

### Flashcard MOD-5003 — First General Election (1951–52)
**Front:** What made the first general election the "biggest experiment in democracy" in the owner's account?
**Definition (learn):** The first Indian general election, held on universal adult franchise over 1951–52, establishing competitive democracy in a poor, largely illiterate society. (✅ owner 32)
**Breakdown:**
- Franchise: universal adult, not property- or literacy-based
- Scale: about 173 million voters
- Turnout: roughly 46 per cent
- Significance: democracy's institutional foundation before social conditions were met
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1951–52; ~173 million voters; ~46% turnout (✅ owner 32 §1–2). Quoted phrase: "the biggest experiment in democracy anywhere in the world" (✅ owner 32).
**Links:** MOD-5009 (Kerala 1957 test), MOD-5001, MOD-5033 (CPI electoral turn).
**Trap:** The election is 1951–52, not 1950. Do not state turnout as 60 per cent or above; the owner records ~46 per cent.
**Mains hook:** "How was democracy consolidated after 1947?" Franchise, elections, tolerated opposition and federal accommodation; qualify with the literacy gap.
**Back:** *"173 million, 46 per cent: the world's biggest democracy experiment."*

### Flashcard MOD-5004 — Community Development Programme (2 October 1952)
**Front:** What is the date control for the Community Development Programme in Nehru-era planning?
**Definition (learn):** A rural development programme launched in 1952 as part of the early planning framework. (✅ owner 32 §9 date control; purpose ⚠️ standard knowledge, not stated in owner)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 2 October 1952 (✅ owner 32 §9)
- Sphere: rural development within the planned economy
- Owner treats it as a distinct date from the Planning Commission and the First Plan
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2 October 1952 (✅ owner 32); 1952 is the same year as the election, not the Planning Commission.
**Links:** MOD-5002, MOD-5008, MOD-5025.
**Trap:** Do not merge the 1950 Planning Commission with the 1952 Community Development date. The owner keeps them separate on purpose.
**Mains hook:** Use it as a rural-development instrument only with the owner's caveat that rural outcomes lagged (see MOD-5019).
**Back:** *"Rural uplift on Gandhi's birthday, 1952 — a date, not a plan."*

### Flashcard MOD-5005 — Avadi Congress and the "socialistic pattern of society" (1955)
**Front:** What did the Avadi session of 1955 commit Congress to, and what did it not mean?
**Definition (learn):** The 1955 Avadi resolution that adopted the "socialistic pattern of society" as the party's goal, meaning growth with equitable distribution inside a mixed economy. (✅ owner 32; ✅ owner 33)
**Breakdown:**
- Adopted at Avadi, 1955
- Flexible, not doctrinaire
- Combined a large public sector with private enterprise and civil liberties
- Later absorbed the Socialists' programme (co-option, MOD-5036)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Avadi 1955 (✅ owner 32 §1–2; owner 33 §8.4).
**Links:** MOD-5008, MOD-5023, MOD-5036, MOD-5038.
**Trap:** The socialistic pattern meant a mixed economy, not full state ownership or abolition of private enterprise.
**Mains hook:** "Was socialism rhetoric or policy?" Answer: a flexible frame that shaped planning but left land and distribution to weak implementation (see MOD-5158).
**Back:** *"Avadi 1955: socialism as a pattern, not a doctrine."*

### Flashcard MOD-5006 — Panchsheel and the 1954 India–China (Tibet) agreement
**Front:** What is Panchsheel, when was it enunciated and in what context?
**Definition (learn):** The five principles of peaceful coexistence stated in the 1954 India–China agreement over Tibet. (✅ owner 32)
**Breakdown:**
- Context: India–China (Tibet) agreement, 1954
- Five principles (⚠️ standard list, not in owner): mutual respect for territorial integrity and sovereignty; non-aggression; non-interference; equality and mutual benefit; peaceful coexistence
- Later undermined by the 1962 war
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1954 (✅ owner 32 §1, §4). Bandung (1955) and NAM (1961) built on it.
**Links:** MOD-5007, MOD-5014, MOD-5015, MOD-5024.
**Trap:** Panchsheel is 1954, not 1962. The 1962 war did not create it; it tested the framing.
**Mains hook:** The Panchsheel framing of China is the owner's reason the 1962 war was a moral and strategic shock (see MOD-5015).
**Back:** *"Five principles, 1954, Tibet — a trust that 1962 broke."*

### Flashcard MOD-5007 — Bandung Conference (1955)
**Front:** What did the 1955 Bandung Conference establish for India's role in the decolonising world?
**Definition (learn):** The 1955 Asian-African gathering that made India a leader of newly decolonising states. (✅ owner 32)
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 1955
- Role: India as a leader of the newly decolonising world
- Sequence: preceded the Belgrade founding of the Non-Aligned Movement in 1961
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1955 (✅ owner 32 §2; §9 date control).
**Links:** MOD-5006, MOD-5010, MOD-5011.
**Trap:** Bandung (1955) is not Belgrade (1961). Bandung was an Afro-Asian meeting; NAM was launched at Belgrade.
**Mains hook:** Use Bandung as evidence that non-alignment gave a poor state disproportionate voice (owner 32 adv).
**Back:** *"Bandung 1955 gathered the decolonising world; Belgrade 1961 founded NAM."*

### Flashcard MOD-5008 — Second Five Year Plan (1956–61) and the Mahalanobis model
**Front:** What strategy did the Second Five Year Plan follow and what were its consequences?
**Definition (learn):** The 1956–61 plan that prioritised heavy and capital-goods industry in the public sector under the Mahalanobis model. (✅ owner 32; ✅ owner 38)
**Breakdown:**
- Strategy: capital goods first (self-reliance as capacity to make machines)
- Sector: public-sector heavy industry, mixed economy
- Consequence: built an industrial base but under-delivered on agriculture and employment (owner 38)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1956–61 (✅ owner 32 §4). IPR 1956 is the parallel policy anchor (MOD-5152).
**Links:** MOD-5005, MOD-5152, MOD-5154, MOD-5019.
**Trap:** The model was not consumer-goods led; do not describe it as private-sector led.
**Mains hook:** "Planning delivered the industrial base it promised and the agricultural crisis it did not anticipate; the Mahalanobis choice explains both."
**Back:** *"Second Plan: machines that make machines, public sector first."*

### Flashcard MOD-5009 — Kerala 1957: the first elected communist government
**Front:** Who led Kerala's 1957 government and why is it the owner's key test case for democratic institutionalisation?
**Definition (learn):** The 1957 government in Kerala formed after the communists won the state assembly, led by E.M.S. Namboodiripad, the first elected communist state government in India. (✅ owner 32; ✅ owner 33 "CPI won Kerala")
**Breakdown:**
- Leader: E.M.S. Namboodiripad
- Party: communist (CPI in the owner's party account, MOD-5033)
- Significance: a ruling party accepted an ideological opponent taking office in a state
- Later dismissal is part of the record (⚠️ owner 32 §8.4, no date given)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1957 (✅ owner 32; owner 33 Must-Know).
**Links:** MOD-5003, MOD-5033, MOD-5034, MOD-5046.
**Trap:** Kerala's communist government is 1957, not the 1960s. The later dismissal is a qualification, not a cancellation.
**Mains hook:** "Democracy in the Nehru era: form or substance?" Kerala 1957 is the strongest single piece of evidence for substance; state the later dismissal alongside.
**Back:** *"1957: Kerala votes Reds in, EMS takes office, democracy passes."*

### Flashcard MOD-5010 — Belgrade NAM summit (1961) and Goa liberation (1961)
**Front:** What did the 1961 Belgrade summit launch and what did Goa's liberation in the same year show?
**Definition (learn):** The Belgrade summit launched the Non-Aligned Movement with Nasser and Tito; in 1961 India also liberated Goa by force. (✅ owner 32; ✅ owner 32 adv)
**Breakdown:**
- NAM: formalised at Belgrade with Nasser and Tito (✅ owner 32 adv)
- Goa: liberated 1961 (✅ owner 32)
- Lesson: non-alignment was not pacifism; India used force in its own territorial interest the same year
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Belgrade 1961; Goa 1961 (✅ owner 32 §1 and adv §8.7 Bank D).
**Links:** MOD-5007, MOD-5011, MOD-5015.
**Trap:** Do not read non-alignment as pacifism; Goa is the counter-example owner 32 uses.
**Mains hook:** Use Goa with Belgrade to argue that non-alignment was active autonomy, not abstention.
**Back:** *"Belgrade 61 founded the non-aligned club; Goa 61 showed it had teeth."*

### Flashcard MOD-5011 — Non-alignment as an active doctrine
**Front:** Why was non-alignment an active doctrine rather than neutrality?
**Definition (learn):** A foreign-policy line of independent judgement on each issue, anchored in anti-colonialism and disarmament, not abstention from every dispute. (✅ owner 32 §1, §8.7)
**Breakdown:**
- Principle: judge each issue on its merits
- Anchors: anti-colonialism, disarmament
- Instances: Panchsheel (1954), Bandung (1955), NAM (1961)
- Test: 1962 exposed capability limits without repudiating the line
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1954, 1955, 1961 milestones (✅ owner 32). Chandra: Hungary 1956 criticised (MOD-5013).
**Links:** MOD-5006, MOD-5007, MOD-5010, MOD-5013, MOD-5015.
**Trap:** Non-alignment ≠ neutrality. Do not carry the 1990s "multi-alignment" argument into this historical card (owner 32 §8.9).
**Mains hook:** "Non-alignment: active or passive?" Thesis: an assertion of agency; verdict: effective as diplomacy, unsupported by capability in 1962.
**Back:** *"Judge each issue, not each bloc: active, never neutral."*

### Flashcard MOD-5012 — Science and education institutions (AEC 1948, CSIR, IITs)
**Front:** Which scientific and education institutions did the Nehru era build, and where did it fail?
**Definition (learn):** The capability-building layer of Nehruvian development: nuclear, laboratory and technical institutions alongside expanded universities, while mass literacy lagged. (✅ owner 32 §1–2, §8.6)
**Breakdown:**
- Atomic Energy Commission (1948)
- CSIR laboratories
- IITs and technical education
- Universities expanded
- Failure: mass literacy and primary education
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** AEC 1948 (✅ owner 32 §2).
**Links:** MOD-5001, MOD-5019, MOD-5022 (PYQ 2025 education dimension).
**Trap:** Atomic Energy Commission is 1948, before the Planning Commission (1950); do not sequence it after the planning bodies.
**Mains hook:** "Elite-first capability on a largely illiterate base" — the split outcome is the analytic point.
**Back:** *"Elite labs rose; mass literacy lagged."*

### Flashcard MOD-5013 — Hungary 1956: a non-alignment test case
**Front:** How did India respond to the Soviet action in Hungary in 1956 and why does it matter for non-alignment?
**Definition (learn):** Nehru criticised the Soviet action, while India abstained from a UN condemnation. (✅ owner 32 adv §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Action: Nehru criticised the Soviet intervention
- UN: India abstained from condemnation
- Lesson: independent judgement, not bloc loyalty
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1956 (✅ owner 32 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5011, MOD-5010.
**Trap:** Abstention ≠ endorsement; India did not side with the Soviet bloc.
**Mains hook:** Use as the example for "active not passive" non-alignment, with the qualification that criticism did not become a vote.
**Back:** *"Hungary 1956: criticise Moscow, abstain at the UN."*

### Flashcard MOD-5014 — Tibet and the Dalai Lama (1959)
**Front:** What was the 1959 Tibet event in relation to India–China relations and Panchsheel?
**Definition (learn):** The 1959 Tibetan uprising and the Dalai Lama's flight to India, which strained the Panchsheel relationship. (✅ owner 32 adv timeline shows "Tibet/Dalai Lama" 1957–59; the 1959 flight is ⚠️ standard knowledge)
**Breakdown:**
- 1954: Panchsheel framed the Tibet agreement (MOD-5006)
- 1957–59: Tibet/Dalai Lama issue in owner timeline (✅ owner 32 adv)
- 1959: Dalai Lama's arrival in India ⚠️ standard, not in owner
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1959 (⚠️ standard; owner gives 1957–59 range).
**Links:** MOD-5006, MOD-5015.
**Trap:** Owner gives Tibet as a range; do not state exact events beyond what the owner records without verification.
**Mains hook:** The Tibet question is why Panchsheel's trust broke before the 1962 war.
**Back:** *"1954 framed Tibet; 1959 brought the Dalai Lama — trust began to slip."*

### Flashcard MOD-5015 — India–China war (October–November 1962)
**Front:** What was the 1962 war's place in the Nehru era, and how do the owners read its causes?
**Definition (learn):** The October–November 1962 war with China, which damaged Nehru's prestige and non-alignment's optimism. (✅ owner 32)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: October–November 1962
- Chandra: Chinese strategic compulsions, rejecting "naive Nehru" and "provocateur" caricatures
- Owned failures: intelligence and preparedness
- Causation remains contested (✅ owner 32 adv interpretation)
- Effect: reversal, not repudiation, of the foreign-policy framework
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Oct–Nov 1962 (✅ owner 32 §1, §8.8).
**Links:** MOD-5006, MOD-5011, MOD-5019, MOD-5021.
**Trap:** The 1962 war was not the end of non-alignment; the framework survived Nehru.
**Mains hook:** "1962 was a reversal, not a repudiation, of Nehruvian foreign policy — discuss." Verdict: non-alignment was effective diplomacy and unsupported by capability in 1962.
**Back:** *"1962: a reversal, not a repudiation of the line."*

### Flashcard MOD-5016 — Kamaraj Plan (1963)
**Front:** What was the Kamaraj Plan and what problem did it target?
**Definition (learn):** A 1963 proposal under which senior Congress leaders would resign government office to revive the party organisation. (✅ owner 32 adv §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Target: Congress organisational decay
- Mechanism: senior leaders resign office for party work
- Context: Nehru's decline; succession pressures
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1963 (✅ owner 32 adv; §9 date control).
**Links:** MOD-5017, MOD-5042, MOD-5052.
**Trap:** The Kamaraj Plan is 1963, not 1967. It was an attempt to fix the party before the 1967 breakdown.
**Mains hook:** Use it as evidence that the Congress organisation was weakening before Nehru's death.
**Back:** *"1963: ministers resign to rebuild the party machine."*

### Flashcard MOD-5017 — Nehru's death (27 May 1964)
**Front:** When did Nehru die and what succession followed?
**Definition (learn):** Nehru died on 27 May 1964; Lal Bahadur Shastri became PM in June 1964. (✅ owner 32 §9; ✅ owner 34)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 27 May 1964
- Successor: Lal Bahadur Shastri, June 1964
- Significance: the first succession test of the democratic system
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 27 May 1964 (✅ owner 32 §9); Shastri PM June 1964 (✅ owner 34).
**Links:** MOD-5052, MOD-5016, MOD-5025.
**Trap:** Nehru died in 1964, not 1962 (the war). Do not confuse the 1962 shock with the 1964 death.
**Mains hook:** Use the succession as the first evidence for democratic resilience (MOD-5051).
**Back:** *"27 May 1964: the architect leaves; Shastri takes over in June."*

### Flashcard MOD-5018 — Savings, growth and the "four times colonial" claim
**Front:** What growth and savings claims does the advanced owner attribute to Chandra, and what are their limits?
**Definition (learn):** Chandra's argument that planning raised savings and output in the 1950s–60s. (⚠️ owner 32 adv inference; ✅ Must-Know list in owner 32 adv)
**Breakdown:**
- Savings: rose from about 5.5% to about 10.5% of national income, 1950–51 to mid-1960s (✅ owner 32 adv §4)
- Growth: about four times the colonial rate (⚠️ Chandra's interpretation)
- Limit: poverty persisted (⚠️ owner)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 5.5% → 10.5% (✅ owner 32 adv); "~4× colonial" (⚠️ inference).
**Links:** MOD-5154, MOD-5008, MOD-5019.
**Trap:** Do not attach these growth or savings figures to later decades. Owner 32 forbids growth rates as a standalone claim beyond the mid-1960s.
**Mains hook:** Use as the "state capacity" argument, with the poverty qualification.
**Back:** *"Savings doubled, growth quadrupled — poverty still stayed."*

### Flashcard MOD-5019 — Balance sheet of the Nehru era
**Front:** What is the achievement-versus-failure balance sheet for 1947–64?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's two-column assessment of what the era delivered and what it failed to do. (✅ owner 32 §8.8)
**Breakdown:**
- Achievements: universal franchise and competitive elections; planning and public-sector industry; atomic energy, CSIR, IITs; non-alignment and NAM leadership; secular constitutional order
- Failures: mass literacy (major failure); agriculture, employment and land reform under-delivered (⚠️); primary education and health neglected (⚠️); 1962 defence unpreparedness; Congress organisational decay begins (⚠️)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 32 §8.8 table).
**Links:** MOD-5001, MOD-5012, MOD-5015, MOD-5042.
**Trap:** Do not present only one column. A balanced answer carries both.
**Mains hook:** Balance sheet closes a 20-mark assessment with a graded verdict.
**Back:** *"Built the institutions, postponed the social investments."*

### Flashcard MOD-5020 — Historiography of Nehru's legacy: three schools
**Front:** What are the three contending readings of Nehru's legacy in the advanced owner?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's map of nationalist-modernist, market-liberal and left/subaltern readings of the Nehru era. (⚠️ owner 32 adv historiography note)
**Breakdown:**
- Nationalist-modernist (Chandra): celebrates institutional foundation
- Market-liberal: blames licence-permit-quota raj for later stagnation (developed in topic 38)
- Left/subaltern: "socialism" as rhetoric that shielded landlords and capitalists
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner 32 adv historiography note).
**Links:** MOD-5001, MOD-5023, MOD-5165 (Green Revolution debate), MOD-5177.
**Trap:** Chandra is one school, not the consensus. Name the school when you cite it.
**Mains hook:** Use the three-way framing for any "assess the Nehruvian era" prompt, then adjudicate: nation-building was genuine, the socialist promise was not delivered in land reform.
**Back:** *"Modernists praise, liberals blame, subalterns unmask."*

### Flashcard MOD-5021 — PYQ 2018 Prelims: post-independence key events in sequence
**Front:** What did the 2018 Prelims GS-I (Q91) demand, and what must the aspirant be able to do?
**Definition (learn):** Routed demand: the chronological sequence of post-independence key events. (✅ owner 32 PYQ integration 2018–23)
**Breakdown:**
- Paper: Prelims GS-I, Q91
- Demand: chronological sequencing of key events
- Key: official answer key not held locally; no answer inferred here (✅ owner 32)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018, Q91 (✅ owner 32).
**Links:** MOD-5025 (ladder), MOD-5017, MOD-5015.
**Trap:** Do not guess the answer. Sequence the events using the ladder, not memory alone.
**Mains hook:** Chronology is an entry route into the 2025 Mains consolidation question (MOD-5022).
**Back:** *"Q91, 2018: sequence the Nehru-era events; don't guess the key."*

### Flashcard MOD-5022 — PYQ 2025 GS-I: consolidation across polity, economy, education and IR
**Front:** What is the 2025 GS-I Mains question on early consolidation, and what four dimensions must the answer cover?
**Definition (learn):** The verbatim question: "Trace India's consolidation process during early phase of independence in terms of polity, economy, education and international relations. (Answer in 250 words)" (✅ owner 32 adv, PYQ 2025 block)
**Breakdown:**
- Polity: universal franchise, competitive elections, parliamentary institutions, secular citizenship, accommodation of linguistic and tribal diversity
- Economy: Planning Commission, mixed economy, public-sector heavy industry, irrigation, scientific capacity
- Education: universities, laboratories, IITs and technical training; low literacy as the gap
- IR: non-alignment, anti-colonial solidarity, Panchsheel and Bandung
- Verdict: consolidation meant legitimate institutions and capabilities, not merely integrating territory
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025, GS-I, 250 words (✅ owner 32 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5001, MOD-5012, MOD-5006, MOD-5007.
**Trap:** Do not write only about territorial integration (owner 32 §7).
**Mains hook:** Four-dimension spine, one qualified verdict.
**Back:** *"Polity, Economy, Education, IR — four pillars, one verdict."*

### Flashcard MOD-5023 — Trap bundle: non-alignment, planning and socialistic pattern
**Front:** Which three common misreadings of the Nehru era should the aspirant reject?
**Definition (learn):** Three paired traps from owner 32 §5 and adv §5.
**Breakdown:**
- Non-alignment is not neutrality; it is judgement on each issue (❌ neutral in every dispute)
- The Planning Commission is not a constitutional body; it was created by executive resolution in 1950
- The socialistic pattern does not mean full state ownership; it means a mixed economy
- Also: the Congress was a dominant but plural party, not a monolithic one-party state (adv §5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1950 resolution; 1955 Avadi; 1961 Belgrade (✅ owner 32).
**Links:** MOD-5002, MOD-5005, MOD-5011, MOD-5033.
**Trap:** This card is the trap summary; do not generalise these claims to later decades.
**Mains hook:** Use the trap-bundle to frame any "nature of the era" question.
**Back:** *"Not neutral, not constitutional, not nationalised."*

### Flashcard MOD-5024 — Date control: 1954, 1955, 1956, 1957, 1961, 1962, 1964
**Front:** Which Nehru-era dates are most often confused and what is the correct sequence?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's date control for the era, kept as separate events. (✅ owner 32 §9; §8.11)
**Breakdown:**
- 1950: Planning Commission (15 March)
- 1952: Community Development (2 October)
- 1954: Panchsheel
- 1955: Avadi and Bandung
- 1956–61: Second Plan
- 1957: Kerala communist government
- 1961: Belgrade and Goa
- 1962: India–China war
- 1963: Kamaraj Plan
- 1964: Nehru's death (27 May)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 32 §9, §8.11).
**Links:** MOD-5025 (ladder), MOD-5002, MOD-5006, MOD-5009.
**Trap:** Panchsheel is 1954 not 1962; Kerala is 1957 not the 1960s; Second Plan is 1956–61 not 1951.
**Mains hook:** Use the sequence to avoid anachronism in the 20-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Fifty-four Panchsheel, fifty-five Avadi, sixty-two war — keep them apart."*

### Flashcard MOD-5025 — Chronology ladder: Nehru era (1950–1964)
**Front:** What is the complete chronology ladder for the Nehru era as the owner sets it?
**Definition (learn):** A single ladder of the era's anchor events, from planning to death. (✅ owner 32 mini-timeline; ✅ owner 32 adv mini-timeline)
**Breakdown:**
- 1950 (15 Mar): Planning Commission
- 1951–52: First general election
- 1952: Community Development
- 1954: Panchsheel
- 1955: Avadi; Bandung
- 1956–61: Second Five Year Plan
- 1957: Kerala communist government
- 1961: Belgrade NAM; Goa
- 1962 (Oct–Nov): India–China war
- 1963: Kamaraj Plan
- 1964 (27 May): Nehru dies
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 32).
**Links:** MOD-5002 to MOD-5017.
**Trap:** The 1962 war and the 1964 death are separate ladder steps.
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to frame a chronological answer.
**Back:** *"Plan 50, vote 51, Panch 54, Avadi 55, Plan2 56, Kerala 57, Belgrade 61, war 62, Kamaraj 63, death 64."*

### GROUP — 33 · Party Politics 1947–67: The Congress System & the Opposition

### Flashcard MOD-5026 — Concept: The "Congress system"
**Front:** What is the "Congress system", and how did one-party dominance coexist with competition?
**Definition (learn):** Rajni Kothari's model of one-party dominance sustained by internal factional pluralism, consensus and co-option of rival agendas, not by one-party rule. (✅ owner 33 §4; ⚠️ concept attribution, owner 33 §8.4)
**Breakdown:**
- Congress: a broad, plural, consensual party absorbing many social groups
- Opposition: "parties of pressure" whose agendas Congress absorbed
- Electoral: large seat majorities on under half the popular vote (✅ owner 33 §1)
- Internal: dissent within Congress performed the function an opposition would
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Congress won large seat majorities while polling under 50% of votes (✅ owner 33 §1). Patel's 1948 rule and Tandon–Nehru 1950–51 are the anchor events (MOD-5027, MOD-5028).
**Links:** MOD-5027, MOD-5036, MOD-5037, MOD-5038, MOD-5044, MOD-5046.
**Trap:** Dominance is not rule. Never claim Congress won popular-vote majorities.
**Mains hook:** "The Congress system was one-party dominance without one-party rule — critically examine." Qualified thesis: the system worked because Congress was itself a coalition.
**Back:** *"Dominant, not dictatorial: Congress as several parties at once."*

### Flashcard MOD-5027 — Patel's 1948 dual-membership rule and the Socialist exit
**Front:** What did the 1948 Congress rule on dual membership do to the Socialists?
**Definition (learn):** A 1948 rule barring members from belonging to a rival party with its own organisation; the Socialists left Congress as a result. (✅ owner 33 §8.4)
**Breakdown:**
- Rule: no dual membership with rival organisations
- Effect: Socialists exit
- Function: defined Congress's organisational boundary and set the terms of the first split
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1948 (✅ owner 33 §4, §8.10). Attribution "Patel's rule" is standard (⚠️ standard attribution; owner 33 names it "Patel's rule (1948)" in §1).
**Links:** MOD-5030, MOD-5031, MOD-5040 (1979 parallel).
**Trap:** The 1948 rule is about rival party organisation; it is not the same as the 1979 Janata dual-membership dispute about RSS ties (MOD-5105), though they are linked.
**Mains hook:** Use the 1948 rule as the first instance of the "dual membership" problem that recurs in 1979.
**Back:** *"1948: no dual membership, so the Socialists left."*

### Flashcard MOD-5028 — Tandon–Nehru contest for the Congress presidency (1950–51)
**Front:** How was the 1950–51 contest for the Congress presidency settled, and why does it matter?
**Definition (learn):** Nehru's contest with Tandon for Congress presidency, which Nehru won, confirming his grip on party and government. (✅ owner 33 §8.4; ✅ owner 33 adv §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Contest: Tandon vs Nehru, 1950–51
- Result: Nehru prevails
- Significance: direction settled internally, not by opposition pressure
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1950–51 (✅ owner 33).
**Links:** MOD-5026, MOD-5017, MOD-5039.
**Trap:** The contest is about the party presidency, not the prime ministership.
**Mains hook:** Use it to show that the party's leadership was settled internally, a point that qualifies the "plural party" claim.
**Back:** *"1950-51: Tandon loses, Nehru holds party and government."*

### Flashcard MOD-5029 — Bharatiya Jana Sangh (1951)
**Front:** Who founded the Bharatiya Jana Sangh, when, and what was its organisational link?
**Definition (learn):** The right-wing party founded in 1951 by Shyama Prasad Mookerjee, closely linked to the RSS. (✅ owner 33 §2, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Founded: 1951
- Founder: Shyama Prasad Mookerjee
- Link: RSS-linked
- Ideology: cultural nationalism (owner 33 §8.5)
- Trajectory: steady organisational growth; coalition partner by 1967
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1951 (✅ owner 33; PYQ 2024 party-leader matching, MOD-5045).
**Links:** MOD-5040, MOD-5041, MOD-5045, MOD-5105.
**Trap:** Jana Sangh had a clear RSS link; it did not lack an organisational parent.
**Mains hook:** Use the Jana Sangh's growth as evidence of the Right's rise against Congress socialist rhetoric (MOD-5036).
**Back:** *"1951: Mookerjee's Jana Sangh, RSS-linked, cultural nationalist."*

### Flashcard MOD-5030 — Kisan Mazdoor Praja Party (KMPP) and the PSP (1952)
**Front:** How was the Praja Socialist Party formed in 1952, and which groups merged?
**Definition (learn):** The PSP formed in 1952 from the Kisan Mazdoor Praja Party (Kripalani) and the Socialist Party. (✅ owner 33 §2, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- KMPP: formed in 1951 by Kripalani (✅ owner 33 mini-timeline)
- Socialist Party: merged with KMPP
- PSP: formed 1952
- Trap: the KMPP merged into PSP and did not survive separately (✅ owner 33 §8.5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1951 KMPP; 1952 PSP (✅ owner 33).
**Links:** MOD-5027, MOD-5031, MOD-5045.
**Trap:** The KMPP merged into PSP; do not treat KMPP as a separately surviving party.
**Mains hook:** Use the PSP as a case of socialist fragmentation under Congress's gravitational pull.
**Back:** *"KMPP plus Socialists became PSP in 1952."*

### Flashcard MOD-5031 — PSP split: Asoka Mehta versus Lohia
**Front:** What was the core cleavage that split the Socialists and PSP?
**Definition (learn):** The PSP was paralysed by a choice between Asoka Mehta's cooperation with Congress and Lohia's militant anti-Congressism. (✅ owner 33 adv §1, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Asoka Mehta: cooperation strategy
- Rammanohar Lohia: militant opposition
- Drain: defections back to Congress
- Figures: JP, Narendra Dev, Lohia, Asoka Mehta (✅ owner 33 §8.5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PSP formed 1952 (✅ owner 33 §2).
**Links:** MOD-5030, MOD-5032, MOD-5038.
**Trap:** The split was over strategy towards Congress, not over socialism itself.
**Mains hook:** The Socialists' split is the best example of the opposition's structural fragmentation (MOD-5038).
**Back:** *"Mehta cooperates, Lohia confronts — the PSP splits on Congress."*

### Flashcard MOD-5032 — Lohia socialists (SSP) and the anti-Congress coalition formula
**Front:** What did Lohia's socialists stand for, and what coalition role did they play by 1967?
**Definition (learn):** The Lohiaite wing of socialism, militant anti-Congressism, backward-caste reservation and Hindi as link language. (✅ owner 33 §2, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Stance: militant anti-Congressism
- Demands: backward-caste reservation; Hindi as link language
- Role: supplied the ideology of non-Congress coalitions in 1967 (✅ owner 33 §8.5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Lohia's wing (✅ owner 33). The SSP label is in owner 33 §2.
**Links:** MOD-5031, MOD-5042, MOD-5060.
**Trap:** Lohia's anti-Congressism is a formula for coalitions, not a Congress-friendly party.
**Mains hook:** Use Lohia as the bridge between 1952 fragmentation and the 1967 coalition breakthrough.
**Back:** *"Lohia: anti-Congress everywhere, Hindi for the nation."*

### Flashcard MOD-5033 — CPI: insurrection to elections (1948–51) and the Kerala turn
**Front:** How did the CPI move from insurrection to the electoral path, and what did it win?
**Definition (learn):** The CPI's shift from the 1948–51 insurrectionary line (B.T. Ranadive) to a parliamentary path (Ajoy Ghosh), which took it to power in Kerala in 1957. (✅ owner 33 adv §1, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- 1948–51: insurrectionary line (Ranadive)
- Later: parliamentary path (Ajoy Ghosh)
- Electoral success: Kerala 1957
- Unresolved: the class character of the Indian state (adv §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1948–51; 1957 (✅ owner 33).
**Links:** MOD-5009, MOD-5034, MOD-5003.
**Trap:** The 1948–51 insurrection is not the same as the 1957 electoral turn.
**Mains hook:** The CPI's oscillation shows an unresolved question about the state's class character, not merely a failure of tactics.
**Back:** *"1948 insurrection, 1957 Kerala ballots — the Communists turn to elections."*

### Flashcard MOD-5034 — CPI split into CPI and CPM (1964)
**Front:** When and why did the CPI split into CPI and CPM?
**Definition (learn):** The 1964 split of the Communist Party of India into the CPI and the CPM over the parliamentary road and the class character of the state. (✅ owner 33 §2, adv §1)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 1964
- Parties: CPI and CPM (Communist Party of India (Marxist))
- Dispute: parliamentary road and class character of the state (not foreign policy) (✅ owner 33 adv §5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1964 (✅ owner 33 §4).
**Links:** MOD-5033, MOD-5047 (trap), MOD-5041.
**Trap:** The CPM was created by the 1964 split, not founded in 1947 (✅ owner 33 §5). The split was not over foreign policy.
**Mains hook:** "Trace the evolution of the Left up to 1967." The 1964 split is the institutional marker.
**Back:** *"1964: CPI splits; CPM is born of a split, not of 1947."*

### Flashcard MOD-5035 — Swatantra Party (1959)
**Front:** Who founded the Swatantra Party, when, and what was its economic position?
**Definition (learn):** A right-of-centre party founded in 1959 by C. Rajagopalachari with Minoo Masani and N.G. Ranga, advocating free enterprise and opposing planning. (✅ owner 33 §2, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Founded: 1959
- Founders: C. Rajagopalachari, Minoo Masani, N.G. Ranga
- Ideology: pro-free-enterprise, anti-planning
- Role: the principal conservative critique of the Nehruvian economy (✅ owner 33 §8.5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1959 (✅ owner 33). PYQ 2024 party matching (MOD-5045).
**Links:** MOD-5036, MOD-5041, MOD-5045, MOD-5047.
**Trap:** Swatantra was right-wing, not left-wing (✅ owner 33 §5).
**Mains hook:** Swatantra's grievance against planning is the early form of the 1991 argument.
**Back:** *"1959: Rajaji's Swatantra — free market, anti-plan."*

### Flashcard MOD-5036 — Avadi and co-option of rival agendas
**Front:** How did Congress absorb the Socialists' programme through the Avadi resolution?
**Definition (learn):** The co-option technique, in which Congress adopted rival planks (the socialistic pattern at Avadi 1955), leaving the Socialists without a distinctive appeal. (✅ owner 33 §8.4 Bank A; ⚠️ concept)
**Breakdown:**
- Mechanism: co-option of agendas
- Example: Avadi resolution 1955
- Result: Socialists lost their distinctiveness
- Also: Chandra and Kothari call Congress a "party of consensus" with left orientation (⚠️)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Avadi 1955 (✅ owner 33).
**Links:** MOD-5005, MOD-5026, MOD-5050, MOD-5038.
**Trap:** Co-option is not the same as ideological agreement; it is a strategy of containment (⚠️ owner 33 adv §4).
**Mains hook:** "How did Congress's strategy of co-optation shape party politics?" Use Avadi and Nagpur (MOD-5050) as two stages.
**Back:** *"Steal the plank, starve the rival: Avadi 1955."*

### Flashcard MOD-5037 — Electoral arithmetic: seat majorities on under 50% of votes
**Front:** How could Congress win large seat majorities while polling under half the vote?
**Definition (learn):** Congress's dominance was partly an electoral-system effect: large seat majorities on under 50% of the vote, because the non-Congress vote was divided. (✅ owner 33 §1, §8.4)
**Breakdown:**
- Congress: seat majorities on under half the popular vote
- Non-Congress: often polled more in aggregate but was divided
- Mechanism: first-past-the-post plus fragmentation
- Safe formula: "large seat majorities on under half the popular vote" (✅ owner 33 §8.8)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Under 50% of votes (✅ owner 33; no exact vote shares given; owner forbids unverified figures).
**Links:** MOD-5026, MOD-5038, MOD-5046.
**Trap:** Do not claim Congress won popular-vote majorities. Do not quote vote shares without verified figures.
**Mains hook:** Use the arithmetic thesis to explain why opposition could challenge but not defeat Congress before 1967.
**Back:** *"Seats for Congress, votes divided: under half, yet dominant."*

### Flashcard MOD-5038 — The opposition's structural weakness (ex-Congress, scattered)
**Front:** Why could the opposition challenge but not displace Congress before 1967?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's structural argument: fragmentation, ideological spread and shared origins in Congress meant the opposition could not aggregate its votes into seats. (✅ owner 33 adv §1; ⚠️ owner 33 §8.6)
**Breakdown:**
- Arithmetic: combined vote often exceeded Congress's, but seats did not follow
- Ideology: from Swatantra's free market to Jana Sangh's cultural nationalism to CPI's communism
- Shared origin: many leaders were ex-Congressmen sharing its idiom and personnel
- Aggregation, not support, was the opposition's problem (✅ owner 33 §8.6)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pre-1967 (✅ owner 33).
**Links:** MOD-5026, MOD-5031, MOD-5037, MOD-5042.
**Trap:** The opposition was not ineffective; it governed Kerala from 1957 and shaped Congress policy (✅ owner 33 §8.6 limit).
**Mains hook:** "Analyse the structural reasons for the opposition's failure to displace Congress before 1967."
**Back:** *"Votes enough, unity never: the opposition's aggregation problem."*

### Flashcard MOD-5039 — Party versus government: the Kripalani demand
**Front:** How was the party-versus-government question settled under Nehru?
**Definition (learn):** Kripalani's demand that the party organisation control the government was settled in favour of governmental responsibility to the legislature, concentrating authority around Nehru. (✅ owner 33 adv §1)
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: organisation controls government (Kripalani)
- Settlement: government responsible to the legislature
- Result: authority concentrated around Nehru
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Kripalani's resignation and debate date 1946–47 (✅ owner 33 adv mini-timeline).
**Links:** MOD-5028, MOD-5026, MOD-5042.
**Trap:** The dispute was not settled in favour of party control; it was settled in favour of legislative responsibility.
**Mains hook:** Use this to explain the concentration of power that later made succession fragile.
**Back:** *"Party or government? Government answers to the legislature."*

### Flashcard MOD-5040 — Dual membership: 1948 rule and 1979 Janata split
**Front:** How does the 1948 dual-membership rule compare with the 1979 Janata dispute?
**Definition (learn):** A comparison pair: the 1948 Congress rule that excluded rival-organisation members, and the 1979 Janata collapse over Jana Sangh members' RSS ties. (✅ owner 33 §8.10; ✅ owner 36 §5)
**Breakdown:**
- 1948: Congress barred members of rival organisations (the Socialists left)
- 1979: Janata fell over Jana Sangh members' RSS links (dual membership)
- Common thread: whether a constituent may keep a separate organisational loyalty
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1948; 1979 (✅ owner 33 & 36).
**Links:** MOD-5027, MOD-5105.
**Trap:** The 1948 rule was about party exclusion; the 1979 issue was about coalition loyalty. They are linked but not the same.
**Mains hook:** Use it as the continuity thesis that dual membership is the recurring fault line of coalition politics.
**Back:** *"1948 exclusion, 1979 collapse — the same question about loyalty."*

### Flashcard MOD-5041 — Left and Right alternatives: CPI, PSP, Jana Sangh and Swatantra compared
**Front:** How do the Left and Right opposition streams differ in origin, ideology and trajectory?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's Bank B stream table, to answer party-leader matching and trajectory questions. (✅ owner 33 §8.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Socialists/PSP: Congress-origin; democratic socialism; repeated splits
- Lohia socialists: militant anti-Congressism; backward-caste reservation; Hindi link language
- CPI: communist; insurrectionary 1948–51; won Kerala 1957; split 1964
- Jana Sangh: cultural nationalism; founded 1951; RSS-linked
- Swatantra: pro-free-enterprise, anti-planning; founded 1959
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 33 §8.5 table).
**Links:** MOD-5029, MOD-5033, MOD-5034, MOD-5035, MOD-5045.
**Trap:** Swatantra is right-wing; CPM was not founded in 1947; KMPP merged into PSP.
**Mains hook:** "Trace the Left and Right as national alternatives up to 1967."
**Back:** *"Left: PSP, Lohia, CPI. Right: Jana Sangh, Swatantra."*

### Flashcard MOD-5042 — The erosion chain: why the Congress system broke after 1967
**Front:** What chain of causes weakened the Congress system before 1967?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's causal chain: Nehru's authority held factions in balance; succession contests weakened arbitration; co-option narrowed under the mid-1960s crisis; defections became viable; Lohia-style anti-Congressism supplied a coalition formula; 1967 ended dominance in several states. (✅ owner 33 §8.7; ⚠️ causal framing)
**Breakdown:**
- Step 1: Nehru's authority holds factions together (to 1964)
- Step 2: succession contests weaken arbitration (1964–66)
- Step 3: economic crisis narrows policy space for co-option (basic 34)
- Step 4: state-level defections viable
- Step 5: Lohiaite coalition formula
- Step 6: 1967 breakdown in several states
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1964; mid-1960s crisis; 1967 (✅ owner 33 §8.7).
**Links:** MOD-5017, MOD-5016, MOD-5032, MOD-5060, MOD-5061.
**Trap:** The 1967 break was in states, not a loss of the Centre.
**Mains hook:** Use as the causal spine for "why did the Congress system end?"
**Back:** *"Nehru held the factions, succession broke the hold, 1967 showed it."*

### Flashcard MOD-5043 — Regional currents: Kerala, West Bengal, Tamil Nadu and the north
**Front:** Which regions developed non-Congress currents well before the national breakthrough of 1967?
**Definition (learn):** Regional variation in the owner's account of the Congress system: Kerala, West Bengal, Tamil Nadu and parts of the north. (✅ owner 33 adv §8.8)
**Breakdown:**
- Kerala: communist government 1957 (MOD-5009)
- West Bengal: early non-Congress currents
- Tamil Nadu: Dravidian currents (⚠️ owner, not detailed)
- North: parts of the Hindi belt
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Kerala 1957 (✅ owner 33).
**Links:** MOD-5009, MOD-5033, MOD-5044.
**Trap:** Do not generalise the Congress system uniformly across states.
**Mains hook:** Regional diversity qualifies the national-picture claim.
**Back:** *"Kerala, Bengal, Madras led; the north followed later."*

### Flashcard MOD-5044 — Historiography: Kothari's model and its critics
**Front:** What does Kothari's model explain and what do critics say it understates?
**Definition (learn):** Rajni Kothari's "Congress system" as the standard frame, which critics say understates coercion and patronage. (⚠️ owner 33 adv historiography note)
**Breakdown:**
- Model: dominance sustained by consensus, factional bargaining, co-option
- Critics: understates coercion and patronage
- Test: post-1967 breakdown shows how thin institutionalisation was
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (⚠️ owner 33 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5026, MOD-5042, MOD-5075.
**Trap:** Attribute Kothari's concept; do not quote it as Chandra's.
**Mains hook:** "The nature of the party system in the Nehruvian era" prompt: deploy Kothari, then test with 1967.
**Back:** *"Kothari explains dominance; critics say the coercion was hidden."*

### Flashcard MOD-5045 — PYQ 2024 Prelims: party-leader matching (Jana Sangh, Socialist Party, CFD, Swatantra)
**Front:** What did the 2024 Prelims GS-I (Q73) test, and what must the aspirant match?
**Definition (learn):** A party-leader matching demand for Jana Sangh, Socialist Party, CFD and Swatantra. (✅ owner 33 PYQ integration 2024–25)
**Breakdown:**
- Paper: Prelims GS-I, Q73
- Demand: match parties to founders/leaders
- Key: official Set-A key is local; answer not recorded in the owner
- CFD: Congress for Democracy (⚠️ standard; not expanded in the owner)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024, Q73 (✅ owner 33).
**Links:** MOD-5029, MOD-5030, MOD-5035, MOD-5041.
**Trap:** Do not infer the answer from the owner; match by verified founder associations only.
**Mains hook:** Matching is an entry point to the "Left and Right" comparison (MOD-5041).
**Back:** *"Jana Sangh 1951, PSP 1952, Swatantra 1959 — match the founders."*

### Flashcard MOD-5046 — Trap: one-party dominance is not one-party rule
**Front:** Why is "one-party dominance" not "one-party rule"?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's core trap: the Congress system contained a functioning opposition and internal dissent. (✅ owner 33 §5)
**Breakdown:**
- Dominance: Congress held most seats and power
- Rule: no one-party state; opposition parties governed states (Kerala 1957)
- Internal pluralism: dissent inside Congress
- Competitive in principle long before competitive in outcome (✅ owner 33 §8.2)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Kerala 1957 (✅ owner 33).
**Links:** MOD-5026, MOD-5009, MOD-5037.
**Trap:** The "Congress system" is not a one-party state.
**Mains hook:** "Dominance without rule" — the democratic-dominance thesis.
**Back:** *"Dominant, not dictatorial: Congress as several parties at once."*

### Flashcard MOD-5047 — Trap: party founders and dates (Swatantra, CPM and KMPP)
**Front:** Which party-origin statements are false and what is the correct version?
**Definition (learn):** Three false statements the owner lists as party-origin traps. (✅ owner 33 §5, §8.10)
**Breakdown:**
- Congress won majority of votes? Wrong; seat majorities on under 50%
- CPM founded in 1947? Wrong; created by a 1964 split
- Swatantra left-wing? Wrong; pro-free-market and anti-planning
- Jana Sangh had no parent? Wrong; RSS-linked
- Opposition united against Congress? Wrong; fragmented, ex-Congress
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 33 §5).
**Links:** MOD-5034, MOD-5035, MOD-5029, MOD-5030.
**Trap:** Use the correction, not the false version, when answering.
**Mains hook:** Use the corrections to frame a balanced verdict.
**Back:** *"Seats not votes; 1964 not 1947; right not left; RSS not orphan."*

### Flashcard MOD-5048 — Thesis: the democratic-dominance reading
**Front:** What is the democratic-dominance thesis and why does it matter for the answer?
**Definition (learn):** The thesis that one-party dominance coexisted with genuine competition: dissent was tolerated inside Congress, opposition governed states, and a communist government took office in Kerala. (✅ owner 33 adv §8.2 thesis options; ⚠️ thesis wording)
**Breakdown:**
- Claim: competition present in principle
- Evidence: Kerala 1957; opposition states; internal dissent
- Qualification: 1967 showed that competition could become outcome-changing
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1957 Kerala (✅ owner 33).
**Links:** MOD-5009, MOD-5046, MOD-5026.
**Trap:** Do not claim the Congress system was a democracy without limits; the owner records central intervention in states (⚠️ owner 33 §8.8).
**Mains hook:** Use as the balanced thesis in any "was dominance democratic?" question.
**Back:** *"Competitive in principle, dominant in outcome — until 1967."*

### Flashcard MOD-5049 — Chronology ladder: party politics (1948–1967)
**Front:** What is the chronological ladder of party politics between 1948 and 1967?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's mini-timeline for party-system milestones. (✅ owner 33 §10)
**Breakdown:**
- 1948: Congress bars rival-party members; Socialists leave
- 1950–51: Tandon–Nehru contest
- 1951: Jana Sangh; KMPP
- 1952: First election; PSP formed
- 1955: Avadi
- 1957: CPI wins Kerala
- 1959: Swatantra
- 1964: CPI split
- 1967: watershed (MOD-5060)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 33).
**Links:** MOD-5027, MOD-5028, MOD-5029, MOD-5030, MOD-5036, MOD-5009, MOD-5035, MOD-5034.
**Trap:** The 1967 watershed is outside this owner's core; it is covered in topic 34.
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to frame a chronological party-system answer.
**Back:** *"48 exit, 50 Tandon, 51 Sangh, 52 PSP, 55 Avadi, 57 Kerala, 59 Swatantra, 64 split."*

### Flashcard MOD-5050 — Nagpur 1959 cooperative resolution and the alienation of the Right
**Front:** How did the Nagpur 1959 resolution affect propertied and business interests and the Right?
**Definition (learn):** The Nagpur Congress resolution on cooperative farming, which alienated propertied interests and helped the Right grow. (✅ owner 33 adv §1, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Measure: cooperative resolution (Nagpur, 1959)
- Effect: alienated propertied and business interests
- Beneficiaries: Swatantra and Jana Sangh
- Limit: the Right remained regionally confined (✅ owner 33 adv §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Nagpur 1959; Avadi 1955 (✅ owner 33 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5005, MOD-5035, MOD-5036.
**Trap:** Nagpur (1959) is not Avadi (1955); both are socialist-pattern turns but different resolutions.
**Mains hook:** Use Avadi and Nagpur as two steps of the same drift to the Right.
**Back:** *"Nagpur 1959 scared the propertied, and they went Swatantra."*

### GROUP — 34 · From Shastri to Indira Gandhi, 1964–73

### Flashcard MOD-5051 — Concept: democratic resilience through successions and a war
**Front:** Why do the owners treat 1964–67 as a test that Indian democracy passed?
**Definition (learn):** Two unplanned successions within about twenty months, a war, a currency devaluation and the loss of eight states, all settled by election rather than extra-constitutional action. (✅ owner 34 §8.2 resilience thesis; ⚠️ thesis wording)
**Breakdown:**
- Succession 1: Nehru to Shastri, June 1964
- Succession 2: Shastri to Indira Gandhi, January 1966
- War: 1965 with Pakistan
- Economic: 1966 devaluation after harvest failures
- Electoral: 1967 loss of eight states
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** June 1964; January 1966; 1967 (✅ owner 34 §1, §8.2).
**Links:** MOD-5017, MOD-5052, MOD-5056, MOD-5060, MOD-5075.
**Trap:** Resilience is not the same as a smooth transition; the owner lists the costs (⚠️ owner 34 §8.6).
**Mains hook:** "How did the successions of 1964 and 1966 demonstrate the resilience of Indian democracy?" Thesis: institutionalised, not merely inaugurated, democracy.
**Back:** *"Two prime ministers, one war, one devaluation, eight states lost — and no coup."*

### Flashcard MOD-5052 — Lal Bahadur Shastri and "Jai Jawan Jai Kisan" (June 1964–January 1966)
**Front:** Who was Shastri, what was his slogan, and how long did he serve?
**Definition (learn):** Lal Bahadur Shastri, PM from June 1964 to January 1966, who led India through the 1965 war and coined "Jai Jawan Jai Kisan". (✅ owner 34 §1, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Office: PM June 1964 – January 1966 (✅ owner 34 §8.8)
- Slogan: "Jai Jawan Jai Kisan" (✅ owner 34 §4)
- Major act: Tashkent Declaration (January 1966)
- Death: Tashkent, January 1966
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Office from 9 June 1964 (✅ owner 34 §9 date control).
**Links:** MOD-5017, MOD-5053, MOD-5054, MOD-5055.
**Trap:** Shastri did not die in India; he died at Tashkent.
**Mains hook:** Use Shastri's short tenure as evidence that the PMO grew under succession pressure (owner 34 adv §1).
**Back:** *"Jai Jawan Jai Kisan: short term, long shadow."*

### Flashcard MOD-5053 — India–Pakistan war (August–September 1965)
**Front:** What triggered the 1965 India–Pakistan war and what was the outcome?
**Definition (learn):** The August–September 1965 war, begun after Pakistani infiltration into Kashmir failed, and ended by ceasefire and Soviet-brokered talks. (✅ owner 34 §1, §4; ✅ Wikipedia: Tashkent Declaration)
**Breakdown:**
- Trigger: Pakistani infiltration into Kashmir (owner 34 §8.5)
- Dates: August–September 1965 (✅ owner 34)
- Outcome: militarily inconclusive (owner 34 §8.5)
- Settlement: Tashkent Declaration, January 1966
- Peace achieved about 23 September 1965 by ceasefire (⚠️ Wikipedia text; date check advised)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Aug–Sep 1965 (✅ owner 34 mini-timeline).
**Links:** MOD-5054, MOD-5071, MOD-5052.
**Trap:** The 1965 war ended inconclusively; the 1971 war ended decisively. Do not mix the outcomes.
**Mains hook:** Use 1965 vs 1971 comparison (MOD-5071) for the foreign-policy question.
**Back:** *"1965: infiltration fails, war ends in a table, not a victory."*

### Flashcard MOD-5054 — Tashkent Declaration (10 January 1966)
**Front:** What did the Tashkent Declaration commit India and Pakistan to, and who mediated?
**Definition (learn):** The January 1966 declaration signed at Tashkent by Lal Bahadur Shastri and Ayub Khan, mediated by Soviet leader Alexei Kosygin, restoring pre-war positions. (✅ owner 34; ✅ Wikipedia: Tashkent Declaration)
**Breakdown:**
- Date signed: 10 January 1966 (✅ owner 34 §8.4)
- Signatories: Shastri (India), Ayub Khan (Pakistan) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Mediator: Alexei Kosygin (✅ Wikipedia)
- Terms: both militaries back to pre-war lines; no interference in internal affairs; restored economic and diplomatic ties; orderly POW exchange (✅ Wikipedia)
- Withdrawal target: 25 February 1966 (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 January 1966 (✅ owner and Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5053, MOD-5055, MOD-5071.
**Trap:** Tashkent is a peace declaration, not a settlement of Kashmir. The Simla Agreement (MOD-5069) is the later bilateral framework.
**Mains hook:** Use Tashkent as the diplomatic result of a war fought without decisive victory.
**Back:** *"10 Jan 1966, Shastri and Ayub, Kosygin in the chair: back to the lines."*

### Flashcard MOD-5055 — Shastri's death at Tashkent (January 1966)
**Front:** Where and when did Shastri die, and what is the trap?
**Definition (learn):** Shastri died at Tashkent in the early hours of 11 January 1966, the day after signing the declaration. (✅ owner 34 §8.8)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 11 January 1966 (✅ owner 34)
- Place: Tashkent (USSR)
- Wikipedia: died "under mysterious circumstances"; a report on the death has not been declassified (✅ Wikipedia)
- Owner states no conspiracy finding; the cause remains a matter of record (⚠️)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 11 January 1966 (✅ owner 34 §8.8).
**Links:** MOD-5052, MOD-5054, MOD-5056.
**Trap:** Shastri did not die in India, and the owner does not endorse any cause of death.
**Mains hook:** Use the succession as the test of democratic continuity.
**Back:** *"Tashkent, 11 January 1966: the death that followed the signing."*

### Flashcard MOD-5056 — Indira Gandhi elected PM (24 January 1966), beating Morarji Desai 355–169
**Front:** How did Indira Gandhi become PM in January 1966, and what was the margin?
**Definition (learn):** Indira Gandhi was elected PM by the party in January 1966, defeating Morarji Desai 355 to 169, with initial backing from the Congress bosses. (✅ owner 34 §1, §4, §9)
**Breakdown:**
- Office: 24 January 1966 (✅ owner 34 §9)
- Margin: 355 to 169 over Morarji Desai (✅ owner 34)
- Backing: Congress party bosses (the Syndicate)
- Premise: the Syndicate expected to control her; this premise broke in 1969 (MOD-5063)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 24 January 1966; 355–169 (✅ owner 34).
**Links:** MOD-5057, MOD-5063, MOD-5062.
**Trap:** The 355–169 figure is the party election, not a general election.
**Mains hook:** Use the Syndicate premise to explain why 1966 was the seed of the 1969 split.
**Back:** *"1966: Indira 355, Desai 169 — the Syndicate's miscalculation."*

### Flashcard MOD-5057 — The Syndicate (Congress bosses)
**Front:** Who were the Syndicate and what role did they play in 1966–69?
**Definition (learn):** The informal group of Congress bosses who backed Indira Gandhi's 1966 candidacy expecting to control her. (✅ owner 34 §1, adv §8.4)
**Breakdown:**
- Role: party bosses who expected to direct the PM
- 1969: Indira defeated the Syndicate by splitting the party (MOD-5063)
- Lesson: power moved from party bosses to direct leadership
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1966; 1969 (✅ owner 34).
**Links:** MOD-5056, MOD-5063, MOD-5073.
**Trap:** The Syndicate was not a formal body; do not describe it as a registered organisation.
**Mains hook:** Use the Syndicate to explain the populist turn of 1969–71.
**Back:** *"The bosses expected to steer her; she outmanoeuvred them."*

### Flashcard MOD-5058 — Rupee devaluation (June 1966)
**Front:** When was the rupee devalued, by how much did the owner say, and what is the verification caution?
**Definition (learn):** The June 1966 devaluation of the rupee, one of the mid-1960s economic shocks. (✅ owner 34 §4, §8.4)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: June 1966 (✅ owner 34)
- Size: about 35.5 per cent per the owner (✅ owner 34 §2)
- Context: war expenditure, harvest failures, dependence on food aid (⚠️ owner 34 adv)
- Political cost: aversion to externally pressed adjustment (owner 34 adv §8.4 B)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** June 1966; ~35.5% (✅ owner 34). ⚠️ Conflict: several standard sources state 36.5% for the 1966 devaluation (4.76 to 7.50 per dollar); verify before the exam.
**Links:** MOD-5059, MOD-5051, MOD-5170.
**Trap:** Do not state a trade or inflation figure from this owner beyond the devaluation percentage.
**Mains hook:** Devaluation as the economic shock that made 1967 a crisis of legitimacy.
**Back:** *"June 1966: rupee cut, ~35.5 per cent (verify: 36.5 in some sources)."*

### Flashcard MOD-5059 — The mid-1960s crisis: war, drought, food aid
**Front:** What combined to produce the mid-1960s economic crisis?
**Definition (learn):** Two harvest failures, the 1965 war's costs and dependence on food aid, which together weakened the Congress's economic credibility. (✅ owner 34 adv §8.4 B; ⚠️ causal framing)
**Breakdown:**
- War: 1965 (MOD-5053)
- Harvests: two failures (owner 34 §8.5 Thread; ✅ basic 38 §8.9)
- Food: dependence on food aid (⚠️)
- Result: devaluation and 1967 setback
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1965–67 (✅ owner 38 Thread 2 table).
**Links:** MOD-5058, MOD-5060, MOD-5061, MOD-5170.
**Trap:** Do not attach inflation or aid figures beyond the owner's data.
**Mains hook:** Economic distress produced regime change only when combined with a legitimacy crisis (see MOD-5168).
**Back:** *"War, drought, food aid — then the rupee fell."*

### Flashcard MOD-5060 — Fourth general election (1967): eight states lost
**Front:** What did the 1967 general election do to Congress?
**Definition (learn):** The fourth general election, which slashed Congress's Lok Sabha majority and cost it power in eight states amid defections and coalitions. (✅ owner 34 §1, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Lok Sabha: majority reduced (no figures in owner)
- States: power lost in eight states (✅ owner 34 §4)
- Coalitions: SVD governments (MOD-5061)
- Significance: end of easy dominance; not loss of the Centre (✅ owner 34 §8.8)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1967; eight states (✅ owner 34).
**Links:** MOD-5042, MOD-5061, MOD-5032, MOD-5075.
**Trap:** Congress was not removed from the Centre in 1967; the owner says do not claim it did.
**Mains hook:** "Assess the significance of the 1967 elections for the Indian party system."
**Back:** *"1967: eight states fall, the Centre holds — dominance cracks."*

### Flashcard MOD-5061 — Defections and SVD coalitions (1967–70)
**Front:** What did defections and the SVD coalitions show after 1967?
**Definition (learn):** The post-1967 pattern of coalition governments in states, mass floor-crossing and the Samyukta Vidhayak Dal (SVD) formula. (✅ owner 34 adv §1, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- SVD: Samyukta Vidhayak Dal (state coalitions)
- Defections: nearly 800 legislators crossed floors (1967–70)
- Phrase: "Aya Ram Gaya Ram" (✅ owner 34 adv mini-timeline)
- Legacy: anti-defection law later (MOD-5127)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Nearly 800 legislators, 1967–70 (✅ owner 34 adv §1).
**Links:** MOD-5060, MOD-5127, MOD-5042.
**Trap:** Anti-defection law is 1985, not 1967.
**Mains hook:** Use the defection pattern as an early form of coalition instability (see MOD-5147).
**Back:** *"Aya Ram, Gaya Ram: 800 floor-crossings, then the 1985 law."*

### Flashcard MOD-5062 — 1969 presidential election: V.V. Giri
**Front:** Who won the 1969 presidential election and why did it matter?
**Definition (learn):** V.V. Giri won the 1969 presidential election as Indira Gandhi's candidate, a test of her control within the party. (✅ owner 34 §1, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Winner: V.V. Giri
- Backing: Indira Gandhi
- Context: presidential contest at the time of the split
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1969 (✅ owner 34 §4).
**Links:** MOD-5063, MOD-5056, MOD-5057.
**Trap:** The 1969 election is not 1967; the split followed the contest.
**Mains hook:** Presidency as a site of intra-party contest, showing the 1969 rupture.
**Back:** *"1969: Giri wins; the Syndicate's candidate loses."*

### Flashcard MOD-5063 — 1969 Congress split: Congress-R and Congress-O
**Front:** What was the 1969 Congress split and what did it institutionalise?
**Definition (learn):** The 1969 split of the Congress into Congress-R (Indira) and Congress-O (Syndicate), with most MPs and the AICC siding with Indira. (✅ owner 34 §1, adv §1)
**Breakdown:**
- Congress-R: Indira's faction
