- Congress-O: the Syndicate's faction
- Cleavage: left-of-centre vs right-of-centre (✅ owner 34 adv §1)
- Result: Indira decisively broke the Syndicate's grip
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1969 (✅ owner 34).
**Links:** MOD-5057, MOD-5056, MOD-5062, MOD-5064, MOD-5073.
**Trap:** The split is 1969, not 1967 (✅ owner 34 §5 trap).
**Mains hook:** Use the split as the organisational rupture that made plebiscitary leadership possible.
**Back:** *"1969: R for Indira, O for the bosses — the party splits."*

### Flashcard MOD-5064 — Bank nationalisation (1969): 14 banks
**Front:** How many banks were nationalised in 1969, from when, and what was their deposit share?
**Definition (learn):** The 1969 nationalisation of the 14 largest commercial banks through an ordinance, later enacted by Parliament. (✅ owner 34 §1, §4; ✅ Wikipedia: Nationalisation of banks in India)
**Breakdown:**
- Count: 14 largest commercial banks (✅ Wikipedia)
- Ordinance: Banking Companies (Acquisition and Transfer of Undertakings) Ordinance, 1969
- Effective: midnight 19 July 1969 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Threshold: reserves above Rs 50 crore (✅ Wikipedia)
- Share: about 85 per cent of deposits (✅ Wikipedia)
- Assent: 9 August 1969 (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 19 July 1969 (✅ Wikipedia); 14 banks (✅ owner 34).
**Links:** MOD-5065, MOD-5066, MOD-5073, MOD-5072.
**Trap:** Bank nationalisation is 1969, not 1971. The 1980 round nationalised six more banks (⚠️ owner not detailed; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Mains hook:** Bank nationalisation as both policy and political instrument (MOD-5072).
**Back:** *"Fourteen banks, 19 July 1969: deposits to the state."*

### Flashcard MOD-5065 — Judicial setback to bank nationalisation and privy-purse derecognition (1970)
**Front:** What did the Supreme Court do in 1970 that forced legislative responses?
**Definition (learn):** The Supreme Court struck down the first bank-nationalisation order and the privy-purse derecognition in 1970, and the government responded by legislation and amendment. (✅ owner 34 adv §4; ⚠️ case names not verified)
**Breakdown:**
- First bank-nationalisation order: struck down, 1970 (✅ owner 34 adv)
- Privy-purse derecognition: struck down, 1970 (✅ owner 34 adv)
- Response: legislation and the 26th Amendment (1971)
- Standard case names (Rustom Cavasjee Cooper; Madhav Rao Scindia) are ⚠️ unverified here
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1970 (✅ owner 34 adv §4).
**Links:** MOD-5064, MOD-5066.
**Trap:** The SC struck down the orders; nationalisation was re-enacted, not abandoned.
**Mains hook:** Use it to show the courts-vs-executive contest of the period.
**Back:** *"1970: courts strike, Parliament re-enacts."*

### Flashcard MOD-5066 — Privy purses abolished (1971, 26th Amendment)
**Front:** When were privy purses abolished and by which amendment?
**Definition (learn):** The abolition of the privy purses paid to former princely rulers, carried out in 1971 by the 26th Amendment. (✅ owner 34 §4, adv §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 1971
- Amendment: 26th (✅ owner 34 adv §4)
- Context: after the 1970 judicial setback
- Political function: populist measure (MOD-5072)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1971 (✅ owner 34).
**Links:** MOD-5065, MOD-5067, MOD-5072.
**Trap:** Privy purses were not abolished in 1969; the owner records 1971 as the date (✅ owner 34 §5).
**Mains hook:** Separate the two populist measures: bank nationalisation and privy-purse abolition (MOD-5072).
**Back:** *"1971: privy purses gone, the 26th Amendment."*

### Flashcard MOD-5067 — Garibi Hatao and the 1971 landslide
**Front:** What was "Garibi Hatao" and what was the 1971 result?
**Definition (learn):** Indira Gandhi's 1971 campaign slogan for removing poverty, which won a landslide: Congress-R 352 of 518 (owner figures). (✅ owner 34 §1, adv §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Slogan: "Garibi Hatao"
- Result: Congress-R 352 of 518 (✅ owner 34)
- Effect: national mandate delinked from state politics (✅ owner 34 adv §1)
- Context: Bangladesh war in the same year (MOD-5068)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1971; 352 of 518 (✅ owner 34).
**Links:** MOD-5066, MOD-5068, MOD-5075.
**Trap:** The 1971 mandate was national, not a state-level verdict (✅ owner 34 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** "Analyse the significance of the 1971 verdict for federal politics."
**Back:** *"Garibi Hatao, 352 seats: a national mandate."*

### Flashcard MOD-5068 — The 1971 Bangladesh war
**Front:** What did the 1971 war achieve and what does the owner refuse to narrate?
**Definition (learn):** The 1971 India–Pakistan war that ended in a decisive Indian victory and the creation of Bangladesh. (✅ owner 34 §1, §8.4 E)
**Breakdown:**
- Outcome: decisive victory; Bangladesh created (✅ owner 34)
- Effect: Indira's popularity and authority (✅ owner 34)
- Lesson: strategic answer to 1962 (✅ owner 34 adv §8.4 E)
- Restraint: no operational details or casualty figures (✅ owner 34 §8.4 E)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1971; Bangladesh created (✅ owner 34).
**Links:** MOD-5067, MOD-5069, MOD-5071, MOD-5015.
**Trap:** Do not narrate casualty figures; the owner's restraint applies.
**Mains hook:** Use 1971 to argue that India's 1962 humiliation was answered within a decade.
**Back:** *"1971: decisive, Bangladesh born, no casualties in this card."*

### Flashcard MOD-5069 — Simla Agreement (2 July 1972)
**Front:** What did the Simla Agreement settle, who signed it, and when did it take effect?
**Definition (learn):** The bilateral India–Pakistan agreement signed at Simla in 1972 by Indira Gandhi and Zulfikar Ali Bhutto, making the ceasefire line in Jammu and Kashmir the Line of Control. (✅ owner 34 §9; ✅ Wikipedia: Simla Agreement)
**Breakdown:**
- Date signed: 2 July 1972 (documents dated; signed late night 3 July per Wikipedia) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Signatories: Indira Gandhi; Zulfikar Ali Bhutto (✅ Wikipedia)
- Ratification: Pakistan 15 July 1972; India 3 August 1972; in effect 4 August 1972 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Line of Control: ceasefire line of 17 December 1971 in J&K (✅ Wikipedia)
- Principle: settle differences bilaterally, no unilateral alteration of the LoC (✅ Wikipedia)
- Withdrawal to international borders (✅ Wikipedia)
- As of 2025: Pakistan suspended the agreement on 24 April 2025 (as-of Wikipedia page; ⚠️ time-varying)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2 July 1972 (✅ owner 34 §9; Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5053, MOD-5054, MOD-5068, MOD-5071.
**Trap:** Simla is bilateral; Tashkent was Soviet-mediated (MOD-5054). The LoC is the 1971 ceasefire line.
**Mains hook:** Use Simla to explain the bilateral resolution doctrine on Kashmir and India's rejection of third-party mediation.
**Back:** *"Simla 1972: bilateral only; the 1971 ceasefire line becomes the LoC."*

### Flashcard MOD-5070 — Pokhran-I (18 May 1974)
**Front:** When and how did India conduct its first nuclear test, and how was it described?
**Definition (learn):** India's first nuclear test, carried out at Pokhran on 18 May 1974 and officially described as a peaceful nuclear explosion. (✅ owner 34 §4, §9; ✅ owner 34 adv §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 18 May 1974 (✅ owner 34 §9 date control)
- Name: Pokhran-I
- Official description: peaceful nuclear explosion (✅ owner 34 §5)
- Role: immediate-aftermath enrichment, not part of the 1964–73 core (✅ owner 34 §9)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 18 May 1974.
**Links:** MOD-5068, MOD-5076.
**Trap:** Pokhran-I is 1974, not the 1960s; it was styled peaceful.
**Mains hook:** Use as strategic projection after 1971.
**Back:** *"May 1974: Pokhran-I, a 'peaceful' blast."*

### Flashcard MOD-5071 — Comparison: the 1965 and 1971 wars
**Front:** How do the 1965 and 1971 wars compare in origin, outcome and domestic effect?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's comparative engine: a war without decisive result settled by Tashkent, and a decisive Indian victory creating Bangladesh. (✅ owner 34 §8.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Origin: 1965 Pakistani infiltration into Kashmir; 1971 East Pakistan crisis and refugee influx
- Outcome: 1965 militarily inconclusive; 1971 decisive, Bangladesh created
- Diplomacy: Tashkent (Jan 1966); Simla (July 1972)
- Domestic: Shastri's stature rose, then death; Indira's authority unassailable
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1965, 1966, 1971, 1972 (✅ owner 34 §8.5).
**Links:** MOD-5053, MOD-5054, MOD-5068, MOD-5069.
**Trap:** Do not describe 1965 as a victory.
**Mains hook:** "1965 and 1971 compared" demand: objective, outcome, diplomatic settlement, domestic effect.
**Back:** *"65 stalemate to Tashkent; 71 victory to Bangladesh."*

### Flashcard MOD-5072 — Populist realignment: bank nationalisation versus privy-purse abolition
**Front:** Why are bank nationalisation and privy-purse abolition separate measures?
**Definition (learn):** Two distinct 1969 and 1971 measures that each had policy content and a political function against the Syndicate. (✅ owner 34 adv §8.4 D; ⚠️ interpretation)
**Breakdown:**
- Bank nationalisation: 1969; policy and symbol; isolated the Syndicate
- Privy-purse abolition: 1971; policy and symbol; rallied the poor
- Common thread: populist realignment, not ideology alone
- Caution: do not reduce either to only one function
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1969; 1971 (✅ owner 34).
**Links:** MOD-5064, MOD-5066, MOD-5067, MOD-5073.
**Trap:** Bank nationalisation was 1969; privy-purse abolition was 1971; they are not the same measure.
**Mains hook:** "Analyse how Indira Gandhi's 1969–71 measures combined ideology with power."
**Back:** *"Banks '69, purses '71: policy and power, not one or the other."*

### Flashcard MOD-5073 — Trap: the 1967, 1969, 1971 and bank dates
**Front:** Which date traps in 1964–73 does the owner warn against?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's list of paired traps. (✅ owner 34 §5)
**Breakdown:**
- Shastri died in India? No, at Tashkent (1966)
- Indira nationalised banks in 1971? No, 1969
- Privy purses abolished in 1969? No, 1971
- 1967 gave Congress a comfortable majority? No, slashed; eight states lost
- Congress system ended in 1969? No, cracked in 1967; 1969 completed the rupture
- Pokhran-I was a weapons test in the 1960s? No, 1974 "peaceful"
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 34 §5).
**Links:** MOD-5060, MOD-5063, MOD-5064, MOD-5066, MOD-5070.
**Trap:** Use the correction in an answer.
**Mains hook:** Use the corrected sequence to frame a consolidation answer.
**Back:** *"Tashkent, 69 banks, 71 purses, 67 cracks, 74 peaceful."*

### Flashcard MOD-5074 — PYQ 2019 Prelims: coal-sector nationalisation (evidence gap)
**Front:** What did the 2019 Prelims (Q48) demand, and why can the owner not answer it?
**Definition (learn):** Routed demand: the status of coal-sector nationalisation in independent India. (✅ owner 34 PYQ integration 2018–23)
**Breakdown:**
- Paper: Prelims GS-I, Q48 (2019)
- Demand: coal-sector nationalisation status
- Owner status: no modern-history source in the repository supports it; key unavailable
- Action: do not state statute names or dates from memory; route to Economy/Geography (✅ owner 34 adv §8.8)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019, Q48 (✅ owner 34).
**Links:** MOD-5064, MOD-5073.
**Trap:** Do not invent the nationalisation years or the statute sequence; verify from a reliable source.
**Mains hook:** No direct Mains route; use as a single-fact Prelims gap.
**Back:** *"Coal: an evidence gap, not a guess."*

### Flashcard MOD-5075 — Historiography: Chandra versus institutionalist readings of 1966–73
**Front:** How do the readings of Indira Gandhi's leftward turn differ?
**Definition (learn):** Chandra reads the turn sympathetically as a genuine but flawed attempt to give the poor a stake, while institutionalist accounts foreground erosion of party democracy. (✅ owner 34 adv historiography note; ⚠️ attribution)
**Breakdown:**
- Chandra: genuine, flawed, costs acknowledged
- Institutionalist: party democracy eroded; personalisation enabled 1975
- Synthesis: 1966–73 as a hinge from dominant-party to leader-centred democracy
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 34 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5044, MOD-5051, MOD-5060, MOD-5100.
**Trap:** Avoid moral characterisation; assess strategy, effect and institutional cost (✅ owner 34 adv §8.6).
**Mains hook:** "The seeds of the Emergency lay in the de-institutionalisation of 1969–73." Discuss.
**Back:** *"Chandra: a flawed gift to the poor; institutionalists: the hollowing of the party."*

### Flashcard MOD-5076 — Chronology ladder: 1964–1974 succession, split and consolidation
**Front:** What is the chronology ladder of 1964–74 as the owner gives it?
**Definition (learn):** Single ladder of the period's key events. (✅ owner 34 mini-timeline; ✅ owner 34 adv mini-timeline)
**Breakdown:**
- June 1964: Shastri PM
- Aug–Sep 1965: India–Pakistan war
- 10 Jan 1966: Tashkent; 11 Jan death
- 24 Jan 1966: Indira PM (355–169)
- June 1966: devaluation
- 1967: fourth general election; eight states lost
- 1969: bank nationalisation (19 July); Giri (presidency); Congress split
- 1971: Garibi Hatao; Bangladesh war; privy purses
- 2 July 1972: Simla
- 18 May 1974: Pokhran-I
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 34).
**Links:** MOD-5052 to MOD-5070.
**Trap:** Keep 1969 and 1971 distinct.
**Mains hook:** Use as the spine of a chronological answer on consolidation.
**Back:** *"64 Shastri, 65 war, 66 Tashkent-Indira-devalue, 67 states, 69 banks-split, 71 Bangladesh, 72 Simla, 74 Pokhran."*

### GROUP — 35 · The JP Movement and the Emergency

### Flashcard MOD-5077 — Concept: the Emergency as a test of democracy (convergence thesis)
**Front:** Why did the Emergency happen, and what do the owners conclude about the test it set?
**Definition (learn):** The Emergency (1975–77) was produced by the convergence of three crises: an economic crisis, a political crisis of mass agitation the government could not absorb, and a legal crisis created by the Allahabad judgment against the Prime Minister personally. (✅ owner 35 §8.2; ⚠️ thesis wording)
**Breakdown:**
- Economic: inflation, scarcity, unemployment, drought, oil shock
- Political: JP's movement and Congress decay (⚠️ owner 35 adv)
- Legal: Allahabad HC on 12 June 1975 (MOD-5082)
- Verdict: split-verdict thesis: institutions failed to prevent suspension, electorate reversed it (✅ owner 35 §8.2)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1972–77 window (✅ owner 35).
**Links:** MOD-5078, MOD-5082, MOD-5084, MOD-5088, MOD-5096, MOD-5099.
**Trap:** Do not argue that one cause alone sufficed; the owner's thesis needs all three.
**Mains hook:** "The Emergency was a test of Indian democracy that the people finally passed." Thesis: institutions largely failed; electorate decisively passed.
**Back:** *"Three crises fused — economy, mass protest, one court verdict."*

### Flashcard MOD-5078 — Economic crisis of 1972–73: monsoons, oil shock and inflation
**Front:** What economic shocks eroded Indira Gandhi's popularity before 1974?
**Definition (learn):** Two failed monsoons in 1972–73, drought, inflation, food scarcity and the 1973 oil shock, which together discredited the 1971 "Garibi Hatao" mandate. (✅ owner 35 §1, §8.4 A)
**Breakdown:**
- Monsoons: two failures, 1972–73 (✅ owner 35 §8.4 A)
- Oil shock: 1973 (✅ owner 35)
- Prices: about a 22 per cent price rise 1972–73 (✅ owner 35 adv)
- Political effect: mandate of 1971 converted into a legitimacy crisis
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1972–73; ~22% price rise (✅ owner 35 adv); no other figures claimed.
**Links:** MOD-5067, MOD-5079, MOD-5077.
**Trap:** Do not cite further inflation rates; owner forbids figures beyond verified support.
**Mains hook:** "Examine the economic and political causes of the 1973–75 crisis."
**Back:** *"Monsoons failed, oil spiked, prices rose — the mandate soured."*

### Flashcard MOD-5079 — Nav Nirman agitation, Gujarat (January 1974)
**Front:** What was the Nav Nirman movement and when did it begin?
**Definition (learn):** A student agitation in Gujarat that began in January 1974, a prelude to the wider mass protest. (✅ owner 35 §1, adv §8.4 B)
**Breakdown:**
- Place: Gujarat
- Date: January 1974
- Actors: students
- Link: fed the Bihar movement and JP's leadership
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** January 1974 (✅ owner 35).
**Links:** MOD-5080, MOD-5078, MOD-5077.
**Trap:** Nav Nirman was in Gujarat, not Bihar; Bihar came in March 1974.
**Mains hook:** The student-led start shows the movement's social base (middle class and students).
**Back:** *"Gujarat, January 1974: students start the storm."*

### Flashcard MOD-5080 — Bihar movement and JP's "Total Revolution" (March 1974)
**Front:** How did JP become the leader of the Bihar movement and what did he call for?
**Definition (learn):** The Bihar student movement erupted in March 1974; Jayaprakash Narayan (JP) took leadership and called for "Total Revolution (Sampoorna Kranti)". (✅ owner 35 §1, adv §8.4 B)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: March 1974
- Leader: JP (Jayaprakash Narayan)
- Slogan: "Total Revolution (Sampoorna Kranti)"
- JP's authority: from outside electoral politics (✅ owner 35 adv)
- Base: students, middle class, traders and intelligentsia; not the rural or urban poor (✅ owner 35 §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** March 1974.
**Links:** MOD-5079, MOD-5081, MOD-5082.
**Trap:** JP's movement was not primarily a movement of workers and peasants (✅ owner 35 §5).
**Mains hook:** "Assess the JP movement's character, strengths and limitations." Thesis: moral authority without social base beyond towns.
**Back:** *"Bihar, March 1974: JP calls for Total Revolution."*

### Flashcard MOD-5081 — All-India railway strike (May 1974, 22 days)
**Front:** What was the 1974 railway strike and why did it matter?
**Definition (learn):** An all-India railway strike of 22 days in May 1974, which challenged the state's economic lifeline. (✅ owner 35 §1, adv §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Duration: 22 days (✅ owner 35)
- Date: May 1974
- Significance: challenged the state's economic lifeline (owner 35 adv §8.5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** May 1974; 22 days (✅ owner 35).
**Links:** MOD-5080, MOD-5078, MOD-5085.
**Trap:** Do not confuse the railway strike with the movement's start in January 1974.
**Mains hook:** Use it in the causation chain (MOD-5099).
**Back:** *"May 1974: 22 days of rail stoppage, the lifeline challenged."*

### Flashcard MOD-5082 — Allahabad High Court judgment (12 June 1975)
**Front:** What did the Allahabad High Court decide on 12 June 1975 and who was the judge?
**Definition (learn):** The judgment by Justice Jagmohanlal Sinha on Raj Narain's petition that found Indira Gandhi guilty of misusing government machinery and set aside her election. (✅ owner 35 §4; ✅ Wikipedia: Emergency (India))
**Breakdown:**
- Court: Allahabad High Court
- Judge: Justice Jagmohanlal Sinha (✅ owner 35; ✅ Wikipedia)
- Petitioner: Raj Narain (✅ owner 35)
- Outcome: election voided; she was barred from contesting for six years (✅ Wikipedia)
- Significance: a personal trigger converted into an emergency (owner 35 adv §8.4 C)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12 June 1975 (✅ owner 35; Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5083, MOD-5084, MOD-5077.
**Trap:** The trigger was the Allahabad HC, not the Supreme Court (✅ owner 35 §5).
**Mains hook:** The judgment converted a threat to one seat into a national proclamation.
**Back:** *"12 June 1975: Allahabad unseats her; Jagmohanlal Sinha decides."*

### Flashcard MOD-5083 — Supreme Court conditional stay (24 June 1975)
**Front:** What did the Supreme Court do on 24 June 1975 and who wrote it?
**Definition (learn):** Justice V.R. Krishna Iyer granted a stay that let Indira Gandhi remain Prime Minister while the appeal was pending, while restricting her parliamentary rights. (✅ owner 35 adv §4; ✅ Wikipedia: Emergency (India))
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 24 June 1975 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Judge: V.R. Krishna Iyer (✅ owner 35 adv)
- Effect: upheld HC judgment but allowed her to stay as PM (✅ Wikipedia)
- Restrictions: MP privileges stopped; barred from voting (✅ Wikipedia)
- Owner calls it "conditional" (✅ owner 35 adv)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 24 June 1975 (✅).
**Links:** MOD-5082, MOD-5084.
**Trap:** It was the Allahabad HC that unseated her, not the SC (✅ owner 35 §5). The SC stay was only a partial remedy.
**Mains hook:** Use the stay to show the court's role within the crisis.
**Back:** *"24 June 1975: the Supreme Court stays, she stays on as PM."*

### Flashcard MOD-5084 — Emergency proclamation: dated 25 June, announced 26 June 1975
**Front:** Under which Article was the Emergency proclaimed and what is the chronology trap?
**Definition (learn):** The national Emergency under Article 352, proclaimed on the night of 25 June 1975 and publicly announced on 26 June. (✅ owner 35 §1, §4; ✅ Wikipedia: Emergency (India))
**Breakdown:**
- Article: 352 (national emergency), not 356 (MOD-5085)
- Proclamation date: 25 June 1975; Wikipedia says it was signed just before midnight
- Public announcement: 26 June 1975
- Cabinet ratification: the morning after (✅ Wikipedia)
- Opposition arrests begin the night of 25–26 June (✅ owner 35 adv)
- Period: 25 June 1975 – 21 March 1977 (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Proclamation 25 June; announced 26 June; revoked 21 March 1977.
**Links:** MOD-5085, MOD-5086, MOD-5097.
**Trap:** Do not collapse dating and announcement; the owner says keep both (✅ owner 35 adv §6).
**Mains hook:** Use the exact chronology in any answer on the Emergency.
**Back:** *"Dated 25, announced 26, lifted 21 March 1977."*

### Flashcard MOD-5085 — Trap: Article 352 versus Article 356
**Front:** Why must the answer distinguish the Emergency's Article 352 from President's Rule under Article 356?
**Definition (learn):** Article 352 is a national emergency; Article 356 is a state-level breakdown (President's Rule). The 1975 proclamation was under 352. (✅ owner 35 §5, adv §8.4 C)
**Breakdown:**
- Article 352: national emergency (proclaimed 25 June 1975)
- Article 356: President's rule in a state
- The owner treats 356 misuse as a federal issue in the 1980s (⚠️ owner 36 adv §8.6 historiography)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 352 (✅ owner 35); 356 (⚠️ standard).
**Links:** MOD-5084, MOD-5121.
**Trap:** Do not state the Emergency was declared under Article 356.
**Mains hook:** Use the Article distinction in any constitutional-historical answer.
**Back:** *"352 is national; 356 is the state. Don't mix them."*

### Flashcard MOD-5086 — MISA and detentions (110,806 arrests per Wikipedia)
**Front:** What detention instruments were used and how many arrests does Wikipedia record?
**Definition (learn):** Opposition leaders and others were detained under MISA (Maintenance of Internal Security Act) and DISIR during the Emergency. (✅ owner 35 §4; ✅ Wikipedia: Emergency (India))
**Breakdown:**
- MISA: Maintenance of Internal Security Act (✅ owner 35 §4)
- DISIR: the second preventive-detention instrument (✅ Wikipedia)
- Arrests: 110,806 in total (34,988 under MISA; 75,818 under DISIR) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Amnesty figure: about 140,000 arrested without trial (✅ Wikipedia; contested)
- Owner: "over 100,000" (✅ owner 35 adv §4)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 110,806 (✅ Wikipedia); ~100,000+ (✅ owner 35 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5084, MOD-5087, MOD-5096.
**Trap:** Figures vary by source; state the range and the source. Owner forbids unsourced numbers for sterilisations and demolitions, not detentions.
**Mains hook:** "Civil liberties suspended on a mass scale" — how legal emergency detention powers are used; qualify with the figure source.
**Back:** *"MISA and DISIR: 110,806 arrested; Amnesty says 140,000."*

### Flashcard MOD-5087 — Emergency instruments: censorship, suspended liberties and Article 359
**Front:** Which powers and instruments made the Emergency an authoritarian regime?
**Definition (learn):** The Emergency combined press censorship, suspended civil liberties, mass preventive detention and the 42nd Amendment's expansion of executive power. (✅ owner 35 §1, §4, adv §8.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Press: censored (✅ owner 35)
- Civil liberties: suspended (✅ owner 35)
- Detention: MISA and DISIR (MOD-5086)
- Amendment: 42nd Amendment expanded central power (MOD-5088)
- Article 359: suspension of enforcement of fundamental rights during an emergency (⚠️ standard; not detailed in owner)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1975–77 (✅ owner 35).
**Links:** MOD-5086, MOD-5088, MOD-5089.
**Trap:** The 44th Amendment (1978) later restricted what could be suspended (see MOD-5089).
**Mains hook:** Use the instruments list to show how legal emergency powers can be used against civil liberties.
**Back:** *"Press muzzled, liberties suspended, MISA as the key."*

### Flashcard MOD-5088 — 42nd Amendment (1976): contents and effect
**Front:** What did the 42nd Amendment change, and when was it enacted?
**Definition (learn):** The 1976 amendment passed during the Emergency that expanded parliamentary and executive power and curbed judicial review. (✅ owner 35 §1, §4; ✅ Wikipedia: 42nd Amendment)
**Breakdown:**
- Passed Lok Sabha: 2 November 1976 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Assent: 18 December 1976 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Most provisions effective: 3 January 1977 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Preamble: "sovereign democratic republic" changed to "sovereign, socialist, secular democratic republic"; "unity of the nation" to "unity and integrity of the nation" (✅ Wikipedia)
- Fundamental Duties: Part IVA and Article 51A inserted (✅ Wikipedia)
- Term: Lok Sabha and State Assemblies from five to six years (Arts 83(2), 172) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Article 368: amendments not questionable in any court; overrode Kesavananda Bharati in effect (✅ Wikipedia)
- Article 31C: Directive Principles over Fundamental Rights (✅ Wikipedia)
- Judicial limits on stays and writs (Art 226 substituted; 226A; 228A inserted) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Minerva Mills (1980): SC struck down ss 4 and 55 (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1976; 2 Nov (LS); 18 Dec (assent); 3 Jan 1977 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5087, MOD-5089, MOD-5091.
**Trap:** The 42nd Amendment is 1976; the 44th is 1978 and reversed many of its provisions. Do not call the 42nd Amendment protective of rights.
**Mains hook:** "Assess the constitutional damage of the Emergency" with the 44th as partial restoration.
**Back:** *"Forty-two expanded power: Preamble, duties, six years, Article 368."*

### Flashcard MOD-5089 — 44th Amendment (1978): restoring safeguards
**Front:** What did the 44th Amendment undo and what did it leave to the Polity owner?
**Definition (learn):** The 1978 amendment, passed by the Janata government after the 1977 verdict, which reversed many 42nd Amendment changes and restored safeguards. (✅ owner 35 §1, §8.4 E; ✅ Wikipedia: 44th Amendment)
**Breakdown:**
- Enacted: 1978; Lok Sabha passed on 7 December 1978; 17 state legislatures ratified (✅ Wikipedia)
- Assent: President Neelam Sanjiva Reddy; notified 30 April 1979 for some sections; others 1 August and 6 September 1979 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Restored five-year legislative terms (✅ Wikipedia, via 42nd entry)
- Article 352: "internal disturbance" replaced by "armed rebellion"; written Cabinet recommendation required (⚠️ standard; not fetched)
- Articles 20 and 21 cannot be suspended under Article 359 (⚠️ standard; not fetched)
- Article 19(1)(f) property right deleted; Article 31 omitted (✅ Wikipedia)
- Art 300A and other details are Polity-owned (owner 35 §3)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1978; 7 Dec 1978 (LS passage); 30 April 1979 (notification).
**Links:** MOD-5087, MOD-5088, MOD-5090.
**Trap:** The 44th is 1978, after the Emergency; do not describe it as passed during the Emergency (✅ owner 35 §5).
**Mains hook:** "Democracy emerged stronger." Use the 44th as the constitutional repair.
**Back:** *"Forty-four repaired: armed rebellion, not internal disturbance; 20-21 safe."*

### Flashcard MOD-5090 — Sanjay Gandhi and the extra-constitutional power centre
**Front:** Why did Sanjay Gandhi's position matter for the Emergency's excesses?
**Definition (learn):** Sanjay Gandhi held no office but exercised extra-constitutional power, which the owner treats as the key institutional point of the Emergency. (✅ owner 35 §1, adv §8.4 D)
**Breakdown:**
- Role: no office, no accountability (✅ owner 35 adv)
- Excesses: compulsory sterilisation and slum demolitions (MOD-5092)
- Lesson: unaccountable emergency power produces abuse
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1975–77 (✅ owner 35).
**Links:** MOD-5091, MOD-5092, MOD-5109.
**Trap:** Sanjay Gandhi had no legal existence in the state; do not attribute office to him.
**Mains hook:** "Personalisation of power in a parliamentary democracy" as a cause.
**Back:** *"No office, all power: Sanjay's shadow government."*

### Flashcard MOD-5091 — Twenty-Point Programme (1 July 1975) and the rival five-point programme
**Front:** What positive programme framed the Emergency, and what is the owner's conflict on Sanjay Gandhi's programme?
**Definition (learn):** The Twenty-Point Programme, announced on 1 July 1975, framed the "positive" agenda; Sanjay Gandhi's programme of 1976 followed. (✅ owner 35 adv §4, §8.4 D)
**Breakdown:**
- Twenty-Point Programme: 1 July 1975 (✅ owner 35 adv)
- Sanjay Gandhi's programme: owner says "four-point programme (1976)" (✅ owner 35 adv)
- ⚠️ Conflict: standard accounts describe Sanjay Gandhi's programme as five-point (family planning, tree planting, dowry, caste, literacy) — verify before use
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1 July 1975; 1976 (✅ owner 35 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5090, MOD-5092, MOD-5088.
**Trap:** The programmes were positive cover for coercive measures; do not present them as reforms alone.
**Mains hook:** Use the programme as evidence of the "development through discipline" argument.
**Back:** *"Twenty-point 1975, Sanjay's programme 1976 — count the points carefully."*

### Flashcard MOD-5092 — Sterilisation and slum demolitions: the era's worst excesses (no figures)
**Front:** Why were sterilisation and slum demolitions the Emergency's most decisive excesses, and what must the answer not quote?
**Definition (learn):** Compulsory sterilisation and slum clearance, run under Sanjay Gandhi's influence, were the most damaging excesses and a major cause of Congress's 1977 defeat. (✅ owner 35 §1, adv §5)
**Breakdown:**
- Sterilisation: compulsory (✅ owner 35)
- Demolitions: slum clearance (✅ owner 35)
- Electoral effect: decisive in Congress's 1977 defeat (✅ owner 35 adv §5 trap)
- Restraint: do not quantify (✅ owner 35 §8.4 D)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None quantified (✅ owner rule).
**Links:** MOD-5090, MOD-5091, MOD-5097.
**Trap:** Sterilisation was not a marginal issue (✅ owner 35 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** "Assess the electoral consequences of coercive population policy."
**Back:** *"Coercion, not numbers: sterilisation and demolition shaped 1977."*

### Flashcard MOD-5093 — Shah Commission (inquiry into Emergency excesses)
**Front:** What was the Shah Commission and what did it do?
**Definition (learn):** A commission of inquiry later set up to examine the Emergency's excesses. (✅ owner 35 adv §4; ⚠️ year 1977 not stated in owner or fetched page)
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: inquiry into excesses (✅ owner 35 adv)
- Year: ⚠️ standard knowledge (1977); not verified here
- Connection: Wikipedia mentions Jagmohan's role in Delhi demolitions in a quote about the Shah Commission (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Year: ⚠️.
**Links:** MOD-5092, MOD-5090.
**Trap:** Do not confuse the Shah Commission with the Shah Bano case (1985), a different matter.
**Mains hook:** Use the inquiry as evidence of the post-1977 accountability process.
**Back:** *"Shah inquires into the Emergency's excesses."*

### Flashcard MOD-5094 — JP's call for disobedience and the constitutional contest
**Front:** What did JP's extra-parliamentary call contribute to the crisis?
**Definition (learn):** JP's 25 June 1975 rally announced countrywide civil disobedience and called on police and administration to disobey unlawful orders, a claim the government used to justify its response. (✅ owner 35 §8.4 B; ⚠️ constitutional contest)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 25 June 1975 (✅ owner 35 §1)
- Call: civil disobedience; police disobedience of "illegal" orders (⚠️ owner adv)
- Use: government justification (✅ owner 35 adv)
- Chandra: opened the door to undemocratic possibilities (✅ owner 35 adv §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 25 June 1975.
**Links:** MOD-5080, MOD-5077, MOD-5095.
**Trap:** Do not present JP as purely peaceful (✅ owner 35 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** "The Emergency was avoidable; both sides abandoned democracy." Critically examine.
**Back:** *"JP's rally: civil disobedience, and the state's excuse."*

### Flashcard MOD-5095 — Chandra's both-sides verdict: the avoidable Emergency
**Front:** Why does Chandra say both protagonists abandoned democracy?
**Definition (learn):** Chandra's balanced thesis that JP's extra-parliamentary methods and Indira Gandhi's refusal of a constitutional exit (early elections) both spurned the democratic route. (✅ owner 35 adv §1, §5; ⚠️ attribution)
**Breakdown:**
- JP: extra-parliamentary defiance (MOD-5094)
- Indira: constitutional exit available (dissolving the Lok Sabha and holding early elections) but refused (⚠️ owner 35 adv)
- Result: avoidable crisis; test passed by the electorate, not by institutions
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 35 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5094, MOD-5077, MOD-5097.
**Trap:** Chandra does not put the whole blame on one side; do not make it one-sided.
**Mains hook:** "The Emergency was avoidable; both sides abandoned democracy." Discuss.
**Back:** *"Both spurned the ballot: the exit was there and refused."*

### Flashcard MOD-5096 — Trap: the Emergency's early acceptance and later disillusion
**Front:** Why was the Emergency initially welcomed by many, and why did that change?
**Definition (learn):** The Emergency was initially accepted for restoring order, ending strikes, disciplining smugglers and stabilising prices, under a "temporary" constitutional framing, before censorship, detention, sterilisation and Sanjay's power soured opinion. (✅ owner 35 adv §1, §5)
**Breakdown:**
- Early: order restored; strikes ended; prices stabilised (✅ owner 35 adv)
- Later: censorship, MISA detentions, 42nd Amendment, sterilisation (✅ owner 35 adv)
- Trap: the public did not oppose the Emergency from day one (✅ owner 35 adv §5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 35 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5086, MOD-5092, MOD-5097.
**Trap:** Initial acceptance is not the same as endorsement of authoritarianism.
**Mains hook:** Use the disillusion arc for "why did the 1977 result come?"
**Back:** *"Order first, liberties later: disillusion followed."*

### Flashcard MOD-5097 — Election call (January 1977), verdict (March 1977) and end of the Emergency
**Front:** When were the 1977 elections called and held, and when did the Emergency end?
**Definition (learn):** The election was called in January 1977, polls were held between 16 and 20 March 1977, and the Emergency ended on 21 March 1977. (✅ owner 35 §9; ✅ Wikipedia: 1977 Indian general election; ✅ Wikipedia: Emergency)
**Breakdown:**
- Election call: January 1977 (✅ owner 35 §9; owner adv mini-timeline)
- Polls: 16–20 March 1977 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Indira lost Rae Bareli; Sanjay lost Amethi (✅ Wikipedia)
- Emergency ended: 21 March 1977 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Janata government: Morarji Desai, 24 March 1977 (✅ Wikipedia; see MOD-5104)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** January 1977; 16–20 March 1977; 21 March 1977.
**Links:** MOD-5084, MOD-5098, MOD-5104.
**Trap:** The Emergency ended by electoral verdict, not by the courts (✅ owner 35 §5).
**Mains hook:** "The reversal was electoral, not institutional." Use the sequence to show who passed the test.
**Back:** *"January call, March vote, 21 March end: the people ended it."*

### Flashcard MOD-5098 — Fifty years of the Emergency (25 June 2025) commemoration
**Front:** What official commemoration marked fifty years of the Emergency and what does the owner say it fixes?
**Definition (learn):** The Union Cabinet's official resolution (25 June 2025) honouring resistance to the Emergency and reaffirming constitutional democracy. (✅ owner 35 §6, adv §6; PIB, PRID 2139543)
**Breakdown:**
- As-of date: 25 June 2025 (time-varying current affairs; owner-sourced)
- Resolution: honours resistance; reaffirms constitutional democracy
- Chronology lesson: dated 25 June 1975, announced 26 June
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 25 June 2025; PIB PRID 2139543 (✅ owner 35 §6).
**Links:** MOD-5084, MOD-5097.
**Trap:** This is a current-affairs item with an as-of date; do not extend it beyond the owner's sourcing.
**Mains hook:** Connect commemoration to the 1977 verdict as an example of institutional memory.
**Back:** *"Fifty years on, 2025: the resolution remembers the resistance."*

### Flashcard MOD-5099 — Causal chain: from 1971 landslide to the 1977 reversal
**Front:** What is the owner's causal chain for the Emergency?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's mechanism: a personalised 1971 mandate and weakened party organisation led to economic failure, student agitation, JP's leadership, the railway strike, the Allahabad judgment, the 25 June rally, the proclamation, the excesses and finally the 1977 electoral reversal. (✅ owner 35 §8.5; ⚠️ causal framing)
**Breakdown:**
- Step 1: 1971 landslide + personalised leadership (basic 34)
- Step 2: 1972–73 monsoon failure, inflation, oil shock
- Step 3: student agitations (Gujarat Jan 1974, Bihar Mar 1974)
- Step 4: railway strike May 1974
- Step 5: Allahabad HC judgment 12 June 1975
- Step 6: JP's 25 June rally
- Step 7: proclamation dated 25 June, announced 26 June
- Step 8: MISA, censorship, 42nd Amendment
- Step 9: March 1977 election
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 35 §8.5).
**Links:** MOD-5067, MOD-5078, MOD-5079, MOD-5080, MOD-5081, MOD-5082, MOD-5084, MOD-5097.
**Trap:** Do not skip the judicial trigger; the owner stresses it.
**Mains hook:** Use the chain as the spine of a causation answer.
**Back:** *"Mandate, monsoon, students, rail, court, rally, proclamation, vote."*

### Flashcard MOD-5100 — Chronology ladder: 1972–78 crisis and Emergency
**Front:** What is the chronology ladder for the crisis and the Emergency?
**Definition (learn):** Single ladder of anchor events. (✅ owner 35 mini-timeline; ✅ owner 35 adv mini-timeline; ✅ Wikipedia)
**Breakdown:**
- 1972–73: monsoon failure; 1973 oil shock
- Jan 1974: Nav Nirman (Gujarat)
- Mar 1974: Bihar; JP leads
- May 1974: railway strike (22 days)
- 12 June 1975: Allahabad HC
- 24 June 1975: SC conditional stay
- 25 June 1975: proclamation (announced 26 June)
- 1 July 1975: Twenty-Point Programme
- 1976: 42nd Amendment (assent 18 Dec 1976)
- 3 Jan 1977: most 42nd provisions effective
- Jan 1977: election call
- 16–20 Mar 1977: polls; 21 Mar: Emergency ends
- 1978: 44th Amendment; notified 1979
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ as cited).
**Links:** MOD-5078 to MOD-5099.
**Trap:** Keep 1976 (42nd) and 1978 (44th) separate.
**Mains hook:** Use as the spine of a chronology-sensitive answer.
**Back:** *"72 monsoon, 74 Gujarat-Bihar-rail, 75 court-stay-proclaim, 76 forty-two, 77 vote, 78 forty-four."*

### GROUP — 36 · Janata Interregnum, Indira's Return & Regional Crises

### Flashcard MOD-5101 — Concept: the Janata experiment and negative unity
**Front:** Why could anti-Congressism win in 1977 but fail to sustain a government?
**Definition (learn):** The Janata experiment's core lesson: an alliance united by opposition to Congress and the Emergency had no agreed programme, leader or organisational culture, so it won an election and could not govern. (✅ owner 36 §1, §8.2; ⚠️ thesis wording)
**Breakdown:**
- Win: 1977 election after the Emergency (MOD-5103)
- Cohesion: anti-Indira sentiment only (✅ owner 36 adv §1)
- Fault line: Jana Sangh–RSS dual membership (MOD-5105)
- Leadership rivalry: Desai, Charan Singh, Jagjivan Ram (✅ owner 36 adv §1)
- Result: collapse by 1979 (MOD-5106)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1977–79 (✅ owner 36).
**Links:** MOD-5040, MOD-5102, MOD-5105, MOD-5106, MOD-5108, MOD-5120.
**Trap:** Janata was an alliance and not a single unified party (✅ owner 36 §5).
**Mains hook:** "Janata could win an election but not run a government." Critically examine.
**Back:** *"Anti-Congress wins polls; without a programme, it cannot govern."*

### Flashcard MOD-5102 — Janata alliance formed (January 1977)
**Front:** Which parties came together in January 1977 to form the Janata alliance, and was it a merger?
**Definition (learn):** The opposition parties agreed in January 1977 to contest under one banner as the Janata alliance: INC(O), Bharatiya Jana Sangh, Bharatiya Lok Dal and the Praja Socialist Party. (✅ owner 36 §1, §4; ✅ Wikipedia: 1977 Indian general election)
**Breakdown:**
- Constituents: Congress (Organisation), Jana Sangh, Bharatiya Lok Dal, Socialists (PSP) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Owner: "merged into Janata Party" (✅ owner 36 §1) — ⚠️ Wikipedia calls it a "broad electoral alliance" whose members kept their own organisations
- Decision date: 20 January 1977 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Owner also names "Congress-O, Jana Sangh, BLD, Socialists" (✅ owner 36 adv)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** January 1977 (✅ owner 36; ✅ Wikipedia 20 Jan).
**Links:** MOD-5101, MOD-5103, MOD-5040.
**Trap:** "Merger" vs "alliance" is a wording conflict; prefer alliance with own organisations if the question is about structure. Flag it in the answer.
**Mains hook:** The structure of the alliance explains its later collapse.
**Back:** *"January 1977: four parties, one banner, their own organisations."*

### Flashcard MOD-5103 — 1977 election result: Janata's seat count (conflict flagged)
**Front:** What seats did the Janata alliance win in 1977 and what is the source conflict?
**Definition (learn):** The March 1977 general election that defeated Congress; the owner gives the Janata figure as 330 of 542. (✅ owner 36 §8.4 A)
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: Janata 330 of 542 (✅ owner 36)
- Wikipedia infobox: Janata alliance 298; Janata Party table: 295 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Congress: Wikipedia states INC(R) 154 seats; the owner does not give a Congress figure (✅ Wikipedia)
- ⚠️ Conflict: 330 appears to be the combined tally with allies; verify before quoting
- Polls: 16–20 March 1977 (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 16–20 March 1977; 330 (owner) vs 295/298 (Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5102, MOD-5104, MOD-5097.
**Trap:** Do not quote 330 as Janata Party alone; state the source and the allied count.
**Mains hook:** The 1977 verdict was a judgement on the Emergency rather than a durable realignment.
**Back:** *"Janata 330 (owner) or 295 party-alone (Wikipedia) — check which you quote."*

### Flashcard MOD-5104 — Morarji Desai: India's first non-Congress government at the Centre (24 March 1977)
**Front:** Who became PM after the 1977 election and what was the significance?
**Definition (learn):** Morarji Desai took office as Prime Minister on 24 March 1977, heading India's first non-Congress government at the Centre. (✅ owner 36 §1; ✅ Wikipedia: 1977 election)
**Breakdown:**
- PM: Morarji Desai (✅ owner 36)
- Date: 24 March 1977 (✅ Wikipedia)
- First non-Congress Centre (✅ owner 36)
- Caveat: Janata's internal fissures (MOD-5105)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 24 March 1977.
**Links:** MOD-5103, MOD-5105, MOD-5106.
**Trap:** Morarji Desai was not PM of a single unified party government.
**Mains hook:** Janata's first government as proof of alternation, followed by collapse.
**Back:** *"24 March 1977: Desai takes the first non-Congress Centre."*

### Flashcard MOD-5105 — Dual membership and the 1979 Janata collapse
**Front:** Why did the Janata Party fall apart in 1979, and what was the fault line?
**Definition (learn):** The Jana Sangh members' RSS ties, the "dual membership" issue, along with factional rivalry, split the Janata Party by 1979. (✅ owner 36 §1, §5, adv §1)
**Breakdown:**
- Fault line: dual membership (Jana Sangh–RSS) (✅ owner 36)
- Rivalry: Desai, Charan Singh, Jagjivan Ram (✅ owner 36 adv)
- Outcome: Desai resigned July 1979 (MOD-5106)
- Lesson: whether constituents can keep separate loyalties is coalition's central question (✅ owner 36 adv §8.4 A)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1979 (✅ owner 36).
**Links:** MOD-5040, MOD-5027, MOD-5101, MOD-5106.
**Trap:** The collapse was not only personality clashes; the dual-membership issue was structural (✅ owner 36 §5).
**Mains hook:** "Why did the Janata experiment fail? Analyse its internal contradictions."
**Back:** *"1979: dual membership, rivalry, collapse."*

### Flashcard MOD-5106 — Charan Singh's caretaker government (July 1979)
**Front:** Who headed the caretaker government in 1979 and what was its status?
**Definition (learn):** Charan Singh headed a short caretaker government after Morarji Desai resigned in July 1979. (✅ owner 36 §1, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Office: caretaker PM (✅ owner 36)
- Date: July 1979 (✅ owner 36 §8.4 A)
- Not the 1977 winner (✅ owner 36 §5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** July 1979.
**Links:** MOD-5105, MOD-5108.
**Trap:** Charan Singh did not lead Janata to victory in 1977.
**Mains hook:** Use to show the short-lived governments of the period.
**Back:** *"July 1979: Charan Singh caretaker, not winner."*

### Flashcard MOD-5107 — Congress split of 1978 and the formation of Congress(I)
**Front:** When did Indira Gandhi form Congress(I) and why is the date a verification point?
**Definition (learn):** Indira Gandhi's 1978 split, forming Congress(I), which later returned her to power in January 1980. (✅ owner 36 adv mini-timeline and §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 1978 (✅ owner 36 adv)
- Party: Congress(I) (✅ owner 36 adv §4)
- Month: ⚠️ not stated in owner; standard sources put it in January 1978 (not verified here)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1978 (✅ owner 36 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5108, MOD-5063.
**Trap:** Congress(I) is 1978; do not confuse with the 1969 Congress-R split.
**Mains hook:** The party's repeated splits show how personalised Congress had become.
**Back:** *"1978: Congress(I) born, Indira back in 1980."*

### Flashcard MOD-5108 — Indira Gandhi's return (January 1980): Congress-I 353 of 529
**Front:** How did Indira Gandhi return to power in 1980 and what did the result mean?
**Definition (learn):** The January 1980 election returned Congress(I) under Indira Gandhi with 353 of 529 seats (owner figures). (✅ owner 36 §2, §8.4 B)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: January 1980 (✅ owner 36)
- Result: Congress-I 353 of 529 (✅ owner 36)
- Meaning: the return rode on Janata's collapse, not a positive mandate (✅ owner 36 adv §1, §5)
- Caveat: do not read 1980 as endorsement of the Emergency (✅ owner 36 §8.4 B)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** January 1980; 353 of 529 (✅ owner 36).
**Links:** MOD-5107, MOD-5109, MOD-5105.
**Trap:** Indira's 1980 win was not a strong positive mandate (✅ owner 36 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** The electorate "punished authoritarianism and then punished incompetence."
**Back:** *"1980: Indira returns — on Janata's collapse, not a fresh mandate."*

### Flashcard MOD-5109 — Sanjay Gandhi's death (June 1980) and Rajiv's entry
**Front:** When and how did Sanjay Gandhi die, and what did it do to succession?
**Definition (learn):** Sanjay Gandhi died in an air crash in June 1980, which brought Rajiv Gandhi into politics. (✅ owner 36 §1, §4, §8.4 B)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: June 1980 (✅ owner 36)
- Manner: air crash (✅ owner 36 adv)
- Consequence: Rajiv Gandhi enters politics (✅ owner 36)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** June 1980.
**Links:** MOD-5090, MOD-5108, MOD-5123.
**Trap:** Sanjay died in 1980, not during the Emergency (✅ owner 36 §5 trap on Blue Star; general date control).
**Mains hook:** Succession after Sanjay shows dynastic reliance (⚠️ owner 36 adv §1).
**Back:** *"June 1980: Sanjay dies, Rajiv enters."*

### Flashcard MOD-5110 — Left Front in West Bengal (1977) under Jyoti Basu
**Front:** What did the Left Front achieve in West Bengal from 1977?
**Definition (learn):** The Left Front, led by Jyoti Basu of the CPI(M), took West Bengal in 1977 and governed the state as a durable agrarian-reformist regime. (✅ owner 36 §1, adv §8.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 1977 (✅ owner 36)
- Leader: Jyoti Basu (✅ owner 36)
- Ideology: agrarian-reformist (✅ owner 36 adv §1)
- Model: electoral coexistence; long-run stable government (✅ owner 36 adv §8.5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1977.
**Links:** MOD-5033, MOD-5034, MOD-5119.
**Trap:** The Left Front was not a single party; it is an alliance led by CPI(M).
**Mains hook:** Use West Bengal to show that regional left governments absorbed rather than threatened the system (⚠️ owner interpretation).
**Back:** *"1977: Left Front, Jyoti Basu, long rule in Bengal."*

### Flashcard MOD-5111 — Tamil Nadu: DMK and AIADMK (MGR) dominance
**Front:** How did Tamil Nadu's party system work in the 1970s–80s?
**Definition (learn):** Dravidian politics, with the DMK and AIADMK (under MGR) alternating dominance, displacing Congress. (✅ owner 36 §2, adv §1)
**Breakdown:**
- Parties: DMK and AIADMK (✅ owner 36)
- Leader: AIADMK under MGR (✅ owner 36 §2)
- Pattern: Dravidian and sub-national pride displaced Congress (✅ owner 36 adv §1)
- Allied to the 1977 result: AIADMK held Tamil Nadu (✅ owner 36 mini-timeline)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1977 AIADMK held (✅ owner 36).
**Links:** MOD-5112, MOD-5119.
**Trap:** Do not treat Tamil Nadu as a Congress state in this period.
**Mains hook:** Regionalism as an electoral resource (MOD-5119).
**Back:** *"Dravidian pride: DMK and AIADMK, MGR's party held Madras."*

### Flashcard MOD-5112 — Telugu Desam Party: founded 29 March 1982, 1983 landslide
**Front:** When and by whom was the Telugu Desam Party founded, and what was its 1983 result?
**Definition (learn):** The regional party founded by film star N.T. Rama Rao on 29 March 1982, which swept Andhra Pradesh in 1983 on a "Telugu self-respect" platform. (✅ owner 36 §4, adv §8.4 C; ✅ Wikipedia: Telugu Desam Party)
**Breakdown:**
- Founded: 29 March 1982 (✅ Wikipedia; owner 36 adv §9)
- Founder: N.T. Rama Rao (✅ Wikipedia)
- Ideology: Telugu self-respect (Telugu Vari Atma Gouravam) (✅ Wikipedia)
- 1983 result: 201 of 294 seats, 46.30% vote (✅ Wikipedia)
- Swearing-in: NTR as CM on 9 January 1983 (✅ Wikipedia)
- First regional party in united Andhra Pradesh (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 29 March 1982; 201 of 294; 46.30%; 9 January 1983.
**Links:** MOD-5111, MOD-5113, MOD-5119.
**Trap:** TDP was a new party, not a Congress offshoot (✅ owner 36 §5).
**Mains hook:** "Regionalism as an independent electoral resource" (MOD-5119).
**Back:** *"1982 founded, 1983 swept: 201 of 294 for NTR."*

### Flashcard MOD-5113 — The 1984 Andhra "constitutional crisis" and NTR's protest
**Front:** What happened to NTR's government in August–September 1984 and how does the Wikipedia page describe it?
**Definition (learn):** While NTR was abroad for treatment, TDP legislator Nadendla Bhaskara Rao took over as CM; NTR's party and a September 1984 protest in Delhi led to NTR regaining office. (✅ Wikipedia: Telugu Desam Party)
**Breakdown:**
- Absence: NTR in the US, August 1984 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Interim CM: Nadendla Bhaskara Rao, 16 Aug – 16 Sept 1984, backed by Governor Thakur Ram Lal (✅ Wikipedia)
- Response: legislators housed at Ramakrishna Cine Studios; Chandrababu Naidu rallied them (✅ Wikipedia)
- Protest: NTR's march in Delhi, 16 Sept 1984, against Indira Gandhi's Centre (✅ Wikipedia)
- Wikipedia calls it the "1984 constitutional crisis"; it does not use "Governor's coup" (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 16 Aug – 16 Sept 1984 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5112, MOD-5119, MOD-5121.
**Trap:** Do not write "Governor's coup" as a fact; use the page's language and note the Governor's role.
**Mains hook:** Use it as a Centre–state conflict example (see MOD-5121).
**Back:** *"Aug-Sept 1984: the Governor backs an interim CM; NTR returns by protest."*

### Flashcard MOD-5114 — Assam Movement (AASU, 1979–85) and the "foreigner" question
**Front:** What was the Assam Movement and what did it demand?
**Definition (learn):** A mass agitation led by AASU over illegal immigration and the "foreigner" question, which ran from 1979 for six years and led to the 1985 Assam Accord. (✅ owner 36 §1, adv §8.4 C; ✅ Wikipedia: Assam Accord)
**Breakdown:**
- Leader: AASU (All Assam Students' Union) (✅ owner 36)
- Start: 1979; six years (✅ Wikipedia)
- Demand: identification and deportation of illegal foreigners (✅ Wikipedia)
- Settlement: 1985 Assam Accord (MOD-5129)
- Restraint: do not use casualty figures (✅ owner 36 §8.6)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1979; 1985 (✅).
**Links:** MOD-5129, MOD-5119, MOD-5120.
**Trap:** The Assam movement was a mass movement over identity and migration, not a law-and-order episode (✅ owner 36 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** "Assess the impact of central interference on Assam and Kashmir." (⚠️ owner)
**Back:** *"1979 to 1985: AASU's foreigner movement ends in an accord."*

### Flashcard MOD-5115 — Punjab: the Anandpur Sahib Resolution and federal demands
**Front:** What federal demands did the Akali Dal frame in the Anandpur Sahib Resolution?
**Definition (learn):** The Akali Dal's resolution of 1973 framed demands for greater state autonomy, river waters and Chandigarh, among other items. (✅ owner 36 §1, §8.4 D; ⚠️ 1973 date standard, not stated in owner)
**Breakdown:**
- Demands: territorial (Chandigarh), river-water shares, federal autonomy (✅ owner 36 §8.4 D)
- Religious and linguistic elements: stated in owner as part of the claims (⚠️)
- Restraint: record only the territorial, river-water and autonomy heads (✅ owner 36 adv §10)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1973 (⚠️ standard).
**Links:** MOD-5116, MOD-5128.
**Trap:** Do not paraphrase the resolution beyond its three heads.
**Mains hook:** "Trace the causes and consequences of the Punjab crisis up to 1984."
**Back:** *"Anandpur: autonomy, waters, Chandigarh — negotiable, then not."*

### Flashcard MOD-5116 — Operation Blue Star (1–10 June 1984, Golden Temple)
**Front:** What was Operation Blue Star, when did it take place and who commanded it?
**Definition (learn):** The June 1984 army operation at the Golden Temple (Harmandir Sahib) complex in Amritsar to remove Jarnail Singh Bhindranwale and his followers. (✅ owner 36 §1, §4; ✅ Wikipedia: Operation Blue Star)
**Breakdown:**
- Dates: 1–10 June 1984 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Army stormed complex: night of 5 June; full control by morning 7 June (✅ Wikipedia)
- Subdivisions: Operation Metal (Golden Temple); Operation Shop (elsewhere in Punjab) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Commanders: Maj.-Gen. Kuldip Singh Brar (9th Infantry Division); Lt Gen. Krishnaswamy Sundarji planned (✅ Wikipedia)
- Restraint: no casualty figures (✅ owner 36 adv §8.6)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** June 1984 (✅ owner 36); 1–10 June (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5115, MOD-5117, MOD-5121.
**Trap:** Blue Star was June 1984, not 1983 (✅ owner 36 §5).
**Mains hook:** "Punjab: a negotiable federal demand escalated into a security operation."
**Back:** *"June 1984: the Golden Temple operation, five-to-seven June."*

### Flashcard MOD-5117 — Indira Gandhi's assassination (31 October 1984)
**Front:** When and by whom was Indira Gandhi assassinated, and what is the trap?
**Definition (learn):** Indira Gandhi was assassinated on 31 October 1984 by two of her Sikh bodyguards, five months after Blue Star. (✅ owner 36 §4, §5; ✅ Wikipedia: Operation Blue Star)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 31 October 1984 (✅ owner 36)
- Perpetrators: two Sikh bodyguards (✅ Wikipedia)
- Timing: five months after Blue Star (✅ Wikipedia)
- Anti-Sikh violence followed (✅ owner 36 adv; restraint applies, MOD-5121)
- Trap: not during the Emergency; assassination was years after (✅ owner 36 §5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 31 October 1984.
**Links:** MOD-5116, MOD-5123, MOD-5124.
**Trap:** Do not conflate the Emergency (1975–77) with the 1984 assassination.
**Mains hook:** Use the assassination as the succession that brought Rajiv to power.
**Back:** *"31 Oct 1984: two bodyguards, Rajiv takes over."*

### Flashcard MOD-5118 — Jammu & Kashmir: the Abdullah politics and central interference
**Front:** What was the Abdullah pattern in J&K and how does the owner describe central interference?
**Definition (learn):** The politics of the Abdullahs, Sheikh and then Farooq, played out against central interference with elected state governments. (✅ owner 36 §1, §8.4 C)
**Breakdown:**
- Sheikh Abdullah: dismissed 1953 and arrested; jailed for eleven years; charges dropped 8 April 1964 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Sheikh Abdullah: CM again from 25 February 1975 with Congress support; died 8 September 1982 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Congress later withdrew support; assembly dissolved; National Conference won a large majority (✅ Wikipedia)
- Owner: central interference corroded democratic legitimacy (✅ owner 36 adv §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1953; 25 Feb 1975; 8 Sept 1982 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5075, MOD-5119, MOD-5121.
**Trap:** The owner's "Abdullah dynasty" wording is a shorthand; do not assign motives. Keep 2020s J&K politics out of this historical owner (✅ owner 36 §8.6).
**Mains hook:** "Assess the impact of central interference on state politics in Kashmir." (⚠️ owner)
**Back:** *"1953 dismissed, 1975 back, 1982 dies: the Abdullah cycle."*

### Flashcard MOD-5119 — Comparative grid: regional crises of the 1980s
**Front:** How do Punjab, Assam, Andhra, West Bengal and J&K compare on grievance, actor, central response and settlement?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's comparative engine across five theatres. (✅ owner 36 §8.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Punjab: territorial, river-water, autonomy; Akali Dal; delayed negotiation then Blue Star (June 1984); accord 1985
- Assam: foreigner and identity; AASU; agitation then negotiation; accord 1985
- Andhra: regional self-respect; TDP (NTR, 1983); electoral; absorbed
- West Bengal: ideological and federal; Left Front (Jyoti Basu, 1977); electoral coexistence
- J&K: autonomy and centre–state; Abdullah leadership; interference; contested
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 36 §8.5).
**Links:** MOD-5110, MOD-5112, MOD-5114, MOD-5115, MOD-5118, MOD-5120.
**Trap:** Electoral pursuit tends to be absorbed; non-electoral pursuit after failed negotiation tends to escalate (⚠️ owner interpretation).
**Mains hook:** "The 1980s were the decade of regional assertion." Discuss with examples.
**Back:** *"Vote absorbs demands, failed talks escalate them."*

### Flashcard MOD-5120 — Thesis: delayed accommodation from Mizo to Punjab and Assam
**Front:** What is the delayed-accommodation thesis and which cases support it?
**Definition (learn):** The thesis that each 1980s regional crisis settled on terms resembling what could have been negotiated earlier, so the violence was the price of postponement. (✅ owner 36 adv §8.2; ⚠️ thesis wording)
**Breakdown:**
- Mizo: 1986 accord after insurgency since 1966 (MOD-5130)
- Assam: 1985 accord after 1979 agitation (MOD-5129)
- Punjab: 1985 accord after 1973 demands and 1984 operation (MOD-5128)
- Lesson: the settlement was available earlier
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1985; 1986 (✅ owner 36 adv; Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5119, MOD-5128, MOD-5129, MOD-5130.
**Trap:** Do not assert that the accords were the only possible outcome (⚠️ owner interpretation).
**Mains hook:** "Delayed settlement or security response? Analyse."
**Back:** *"Mizo, Assam, Punjab: the accord came later, at a cost."*

### Flashcard MOD-5121 — Historiography: political management versus national security versus federalism
**Front:** How do three readings explain the 1980s crises?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's three readings: Chandra's political-management thesis; a national-security reading on cross-border militancy support; and a federalist reading on the erosion of state autonomy and misuse of Article 356. (✅ owner 36 adv historiography note; ⚠️ attribution)
**Breakdown:**
- Political management: legitimate demands radicalised by central high-handedness (Chandra)
- National security: cross-border support for militancy
- Federalist: state autonomy eroded; Article 356 misuse
- Owner's preferred synthesis: political management privileged, with coercion without settlement as catastrophic
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 36 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5044, MOD-5085, MOD-5116, MOD-5113.
**Trap:** Do not present one reading as the consensus.
**Mains hook:** "Regionalism, federalism and national integration since the 1970s."
**Back:** *"Politics, security, federalism: integrate all three, privilege the first."*

### Flashcard MOD-5122 — Trap bundle: Janata, Charan Singh, TDP, Blue Star and assassination
**Front:** Which five traps about 1977–84 does the owner warn against?
**Definition (learn):** Five paired traps from owner 36 §5.
**Breakdown:**
- Janata was a single unified party? No, a merger of disparate parties that fragmented (owner's wording; see MOD-5102 on alliance structure)
- Charan Singh led Janata to victory? No, he headed a caretaker government in 1979
- Telugu Desam grew out of Congress? No, a new regional party founded 1982
- Blue Star was 1983? No, June 1984
- Indira assassinated during the Emergency? No, 31 October 1984
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 36 §5).
**Links:** MOD-5102, MOD-5106, MOD-5112, MOD-5116, MOD-5117.
**Trap:** Use the correction.
**Mains hook:** Use the corrections to frame a balanced answer.
**Back:** *"Not single, not Charan's win, not Congress-born, not 83, not during Emergency."*

### GROUP — 37 · The Rajiv Years & the Run-up to the Millennium

### Flashcard MOD-5123 — Concept: Rajiv Gandhi, modernisation versus the undone promise
**Front:** Why did Rajiv Gandhi's modernising promise fall apart, according to the owner?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's thesis that Rajiv's technology, telecom and panchayati agenda was real, but his identity judgements (Shah Bano, Ayodhya) and the Bofors scandal destroyed the political base that the agenda needed. (✅ owner 37 §1, §8.2; ⚠️ thesis wording)
**Breakdown:**
- Record mandate: December 1984 (MOD-5124)
- Agenda: technology, computerisation, telecom, panchayati raj, education (MOD-5126)
- Misjudgements: Shah Bano reversal (1986) and Ayodhya shrine opening (MOD-5132, 5133)
- Scandal: Bofors (1987) (MOD-5134)
- Outcome: V.P. Singh's National Front defeats Congress in 1989 (MOD-5135)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1984–89 (✅ owner 37).
**Links:** MOD-5124, MOD-5126, MOD-5132, MOD-5134, MOD-5135, MOD-5147.
**Trap:** Rajiv was not anti-reform; he began the modernising shift (⚠️ owner 37 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** "Rajiv Gandhi's modernising promise was undone by scandal and identity politics." Discuss.
**Back:** *"Modern agenda, identity misjudgements, Bofors: the promise undone."*

### Flashcard MOD-5124 — The December 1984 election: 404 of 514 elected seats
**Front:** What was Congress's 1984 result, and why is 543 the wrong denominator?
**Definition (learn):** The December 1984 general election (polls 24, 27 and 28 December 1984) gave Congress 404 of the 514 seats elected that round, with polling in Assam and Punjab deferred. (✅ owner 37 §2, §4; ✅ Wikipedia: 1984 Indian general election)
**Breakdown:**
- Polls: 24, 27 and 28 December 1984 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Elected in the main round: 404 of 514 (✅ owner 37; ✅ Wikipedia)
- Later delayed polls: 10 more, giving 414 in total (✅ Wikipedia)
- Deferred: Punjab (polled September 1985) and Assam (polled December 1985), 27 seats (✅ Wikipedia)
- Denominator: 543 is not the right base for the 404 figure (✅ owner 37 §8.8)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 404 of 514; 414 total (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5125, MOD-5123, MOD-5128, MOD-5129.
**Trap:** 543 is not the denominator; Lok Sabha seats were 541 at stake per Wikipedia.
**Mains hook:** "Record majority" as the starting point of the decline narrative.
**Back:** *"404 of 514 — Punjab and Assam voted later, so not 543."*

### Flashcard MOD-5125 — 1984 vote share and the BJP's two seats
**Front:** What vote share did Congress win in 1984 and how many seats did the BJP hold?
**Definition (learn):** Congress's 1984 vote share of 49.10 per cent and the BJP's two seats with 7.74 per cent. (✅ Wikipedia: 1984 Indian general election)
**Breakdown:**
- Congress vote share: 49.10% (✅ Wikipedia)
- BJP: 2 seats, 7.74% (✅ Wikipedia; owner 37 adv §4 "2 (1984)")
- Wikipedia's infobox vote total for Congress differs from its table total (unexplained on page)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 49.10%; 2 seats; 7.74%.
**Links:** MOD-5124, MOD-5136.
**Trap:** The 1984 BJP tally is 2 seats; the 1989 tally rose (MOD-5136).
**Mains hook:** The BJP's jump from 2 seats to a much larger tally in 1989 is the marker of the period's change.
**Back:** *"1984: Congress 49.10%, BJP 2 seats — the baseline."*

### Flashcard MOD-5126 — Rajiv's modernisation agenda: technology, telecom and panchayati raj
**Front:** What did Rajiv Gandhi's technocratic agenda include, and what is the owner's caution?
**Definition (learn):** The modernising programme of the 1980s: computerisation, telecom, science missions, panchayati raj initiatives and education. (✅ owner 37 §1, adv §1)
**Breakdown:**
- Technology: computerisation, telecom, science missions (✅ owner 37)
- Local government: panchayati raj initiative (✅ owner 37)
- Education: literacy emphasis (✅ owner 37 adv)
- Caution: panchayati raj was not yet constitutional until the early 1990s (⚠️ owner 37 §8.4 A; 73rd/74th Amendments 1992 standard, not verified)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1984–89 (✅ owner 37).
**Links:** MOD-5123, MOD-5124, MOD-5144.
**Trap:** Describe panchayati raj as an initiative of the period, not a completed reform.
**Mains hook:** The modernisation prefigured 1991 economically, though it was undone politically.
**Back:** *"Computers, telecom, panchayats: modernity begun, not completed."*

### Flashcard MOD-5127 — Anti-Defection Act (1985): 52nd Amendment and the Tenth Schedule
**Front:** Which amendment introduced the Anti-Defection Act, when, and under which Prime Minister?
**Definition (learn):** The 52nd Amendment (1985) inserted the Tenth Schedule, the anti-defection law, under Rajiv Gandhi. (✅ owner 37 §2, §5; ⚠️ Tenth Schedule and 1985 details are standard, not verified by fetch)
**Breakdown:**
- Amendment: 52nd (1985) (✅ owner 37)
- Schedule: Tenth Schedule (⚠️ standard)
- PM: Rajiv Gandhi (✅ owner 37 §5 trap)
- Later change: 91st Amendment (2003) altered the split provision (⚠️ standard, not verified)
- Defection history: nearly 800 floor-crossings 1967–70 (MOD-5061)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1985 (✅ owner 37).
**Links:** MOD-5061, MOD-5148, MOD-5123.
**Trap:** The 1985 Act was under Rajiv, not V.P. Singh (✅ owner 37 §5).
**Mains hook:** Use as the institutional response to the 1967–70 defection crisis.
**Back:** *"1985, 52nd, Tenth Schedule: Rajiv's answer to defection."*

### Flashcard MOD-5128 — The Rajiv–Longowal (Punjab) Accord (24 July 1985)
**Front:** Who signed the Punjab Accord, when, and what did it promise on Chandigarh, water and the Sarkaria question?
**Definition (learn):** The 24 July 1985 accord between Prime Minister Rajiv Gandhi and Akali leader Harchand Singh Longowal, settling Punjab's territorial, water and autonomy demands in part. (✅ owner 37 §8.4 A; ✅ Wikipedia: Punjab Accord)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 24 July 1985 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Signatories: Rajiv Gandhi; Harchand Singh Longowal (✅ Wikipedia)
- Chandigarh: to go to Punjab; transfer planned for 26 January 1986 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Boundary: Hindi-speaking villages to Haryana; boundary commission report by 31 December 1985 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Water: Ravi-Beas shares continue; tribunal to verify; Sutlej–Yamuna link canal by 15 August 1986 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Anandpur Sahib Resolution: only the Centre–State part referred to the Sarkaria Commission (✅ Wikipedia)
- Longowal killed by militants, August 1985 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Eradi Tribunal: 30 January 1987; Punjab 5.00 and Haryana 3.83 million acre-feet (✅ Wikipedia)
- Sarkaria Commission (October 1987) rejected the Anandpur approach (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 24 July 1985; signatories Rajiv Gandhi and Longowal.
**Links:** MOD-5115, MOD-5120, MOD-5131, MOD-5116.
**Trap:** Longowal was the signatory and was later assassinated; the accord was not implemented as written (✅ Wikipedia; implementation status partial).
**Mains hook:** "Delayed accommodation" thesis: the Punjab Accord reflected the 1973 demands that the 1984 operation had escalated.
**Back:** *"24 July 1985: Rajiv and Longowal sign; Chandigarh and waters in the text."*

### Flashcard MOD-5129 — The Assam Accord (15 August 1985)
**Front:** Who signed the Assam Accord, on what date, and what cut-off dates did it set?
**Definition (learn):** A Memorandum of Settlement signed on 15 August 1985 between the Government of India and the Assam Movement leaders, ending the 1979–85 agitation. (✅ owner 37 §8.4 A; ✅ Wikipedia: Assam Accord)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 15 August 1985, New Delhi (✅ Wikipedia)
- Movement side: Prafulla Kumar Mahanta (AASU President), Bhrigu Kumar Phukan (AASU General Secretary), Biraj Kumar Sarma (AAGSP General Secretary) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Government side: R.D. Pradhan (Home Secretary, GoI); P.P. Trivedi (Chief Secretary, Assam) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Rajiv Gandhi present as witness, not signatory (✅ Wikipedia)
- Cut-offs: 1 January 1966 (accept those who entered before); 25 March 1971 (identify and deport later arrivals) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Citizenship Act amended in 1986 (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15 Aug 1985; 1 Jan 1966; 25 Mar 1971 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5114, MOD-5124, MOD-5131.
**Trap:** Rajiv was a witness, not a signatory (✅ Wikipedia). Do not confuse 1966 and 1971 cut-offs.
**Mains hook:** Use the cut-off structure to show a negotiated settlement on identity.
**Back:** *"15 Aug 1985: 1966 accepted, 1971 deported, Rajiv witnesses."*

### Flashcard MOD-5130 — The Mizo Accord (30 June 1986) and Mizoram statehood (1987)
**Front:** Who signed the Mizo Accord and when did Mizoram become a state?
**Definition (learn):** A Memorandum of Settlement signed on 30 June 1986 between the MNF's Laldenga and the Government of India, ending the insurgency that began in 1966. (✅ owner 37 §8.4 A; ✅ Wikipedia: Mizoram Peace Accord)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 30 June 1986, New Delhi (✅ Wikipedia)
- Signatories: Laldenga (MNF); R.D. Pradhan (GoI); Lalkhama (Mizoram Chief Secretary) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Rajiv Gandhi did not sign; he arranged an emergency Cabinet meeting (✅ Wikipedia)
- MNF: abandoned armed struggle and independence goals (✅ Wikipedia)
- Statehood: 20 February 1987 as the 23rd state (✅ Wikipedia)
- Insurgency start: 1 March 1966 declaration of independence (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 30 June 1986; 20 Feb 1987 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5120, MOD-5131, MOD-5123.
**Trap:** Rajiv did not sign the Mizo Accord; the owner lists the Mizo accord among Rajiv's accords (✅ owner 37 basic §8.4 A). Mark the signatory difference in answers.
**Mains hook:** Use the Mizo accord as the third accord in the delayed-accommodation argument.
**Back:** *"30 June 1986: MNF gives up arms; Mizoram statehood 1987."*

### Flashcard MOD-5131 — Comparison: three Rajiv-era accords (Punjab, Assam, Mizoram)
**Front:** How do the Punjab, Assam and Mizo accords compare on date, signatories, mechanism and outcome?
**Definition (learn):** A comparison pair for the three 1985–86 accords. (✅ owner 37 §8.4 A; ✅ Wikipedia for all three)
**Breakdown:**
- Punjab: 24 July 1985; Rajiv and Longowal; territory, water and autonomy; partial implementation
- Assam: 15 August 1985; AASU/AAGSP leaders and GoI officials; citizenship cut-offs; Citizenship Act amended 1986
- Mizo: 30 June 1986; Laldenga and GoI officials; arms surrender; statehood 1987
- Common: negotiated settlement after 1979–86 crises
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ as cited).
**Links:** MOD-5128, MOD-5129, MOD-5130, MOD-5120.
**Trap:** Rajiv signed Punjab; he witnessed Assam; he did not sign Mizo.
**Mains hook:** "The accords are the most durable achievement of the period." Discuss with limits.
**Back:** *"Punjab signed, Assam witnessed, Mizo through officials."*

### Flashcard MOD-5132 — Shah Bano and the 1986 reversal
**Front:** What was the Shah Bano case and what was the legislative response?
**Definition (learn):** The 1985 Supreme Court ruling on maintenance for a divorced Muslim woman, followed in 1986 by legislation that reversed its effect for Muslim divorcees. (✅ owner 37 §8.4 B; ⚠️ 1985 judgment and 1986 Act name are standard, not fetched)
**Breakdown:**
- Case: Shah Bano (1985) (✅ owner 37)
- Response: legislative reversal (1986) (✅ owner 37)
- Owner's point: it alienated liberals and minorities (✅ owner 37)
- Restraint: record sequence and consequence only (✅ owner 37 §8.6)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1985; 1986 (✅ owner 37).
**Links:** MOD-5123, MOD-5133, MOD-5147.
**Trap:** Do not enter the legal merits; the owner forbids it.
**Mains hook:** "Identity balancing as a political strategy: two concessions, two losses." (✅ owner 37 §8.2)
**Back:** *"1985 ruling, 1986 reversal: balancing that alienated."*

### Flashcard MOD-5133 — Ayodhya shrine opening (1986): sequence and consequence only
**Front:** What did the owner record about the Ayodhya shrine opening and how is it to be treated?
**Definition (learn):** The 1986 opening of the Ayodhya shrine, one of Rajiv's twin identity concessions. (✅ owner 37 adv mini-timeline; ✅ owner 37 §8.4 B)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 1985–86 range in owner; the adv mini-timeline gives 1985–86 (✅ owner 37 adv)
- Pairing: Shah Bano reversal and the shrine opening (✅ owner 37 §1)
- Consequence: energised Muslim conservatism and Hindu mobilisation (✅ owner 37 adv §1)
- Restraint: political sequence and electoral consequence only (✅ owner 37 §8.6)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1985–86.
**Links:** MOD-5132, MOD-5141, MOD-5142.
**Trap:** Do not attribute religious or legal merits; do not conflate with 1992.
**Mains hook:** The "balancing" strategy that mobilised both communities.
**Back:** *"1986: the shrine opens; both sides mobilise."*

### Flashcard MOD-5134 — Bofors scandal (1987)
**Front:** When did the Bofors scandal break and what did it do to Rajiv Gandhi?
**Definition (learn):** The arms-deal scandal that broke in 1987, which wrecked Rajiv Gandhi's "Mr Clean" image. (✅ owner 37 §1, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 1987 (✅ owner 37)
- Effect: destroyed personal credibility (✅ owner 37)
- Political use: V.P. Singh turned the anti-corruption idiom (MOD-5135) (✅ owner 37 adv §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1987 (✅ owner 37).
**Links:** MOD-5123, MOD-5135, MOD-5136.
**Trap:** Owner records political effect only; do not narrate the arms-deal details.
**Mains hook:** Bofors as the decisive scandal in the Rajiv decline.
**Back:** *"1987: Bofors breaks, Mr Clean dims."*

### Flashcard MOD-5135 — V.P. Singh: Jan Morcha (1987) and the National Front / Janata Dal (1988)
**Front:** How did V.P. Singh build the opposition that defeated Congress in 1989?
**Definition (learn):** V.P. Singh broke away from Congress, formed the Jan Morcha in 1987, then the Janata Dal and National Front in 1988. (✅ owner 37 §8.4 B)
**Breakdown:**
- 1987: Jan Morcha (✅ owner 37)
- 1988: Janata Dal / National Front (✅ owner 37)
- 1989: defeated Congress (✅ owner 37)
- Outside support: BJP and the Left (✅ owner 37 adv §4)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1987; 1988; 1989.
**Links:** MOD-5134, MOD-5136, MOD-5138, MOD-5139.
**Trap:** V.P. Singh was a Congress leader who broke away; do not treat him as an outsider.
**Mains hook:** The anti-corruption platform's use against Congress.
**Back:** *"Jan Morcha 1987, National Front 1988, Congress out 1989."*

### Flashcard MOD-5136 — The 1989 election: Congress 197 and the National Front
**Front:** What did the 1989 election yield for Congress and the BJP?
**Definition (learn):** Congress fell to about 197 seats in 1989 and a National Front government formed with outside support from the BJP and the Left. (✅ owner 37 adv §4; ⚠️ BJP tally 86 (owner adv) vs 85 in some sources)
**Breakdown:**
- Congress: 197 (✅ owner 37 adv)
- National Front: outside support from BJP and Left (✅ owner 37 adv §4)
- BJP: from 2 (1984) to 86 (1989) per owner adv (⚠️ verify; some sources show 85)
- Government: V.P. Singh heads it (✅ owner 37 §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1989; 197 (✅ owner 37 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5125, MOD-5135, MOD-5139.
**Trap:** The National Front relied on both the BJP and the Left, not only the Left.
**Mains hook:** The 1989 coalition as the first sign of the multi-party era.
**Back:** *"1989: Congress 197, National Front with BJP and Left outside."*

### Flashcard MOD-5137 — The Indo-Sri Lanka Accord (29 July 1987)
**Front:** Who signed the Indo-Sri Lanka Accord and what did it commit India to?
**Definition (learn):** The accord signed in Colombo on 29 July 1987 by Rajiv Gandhi and J.R. Jayewardene, providing for devolution to provinces and sending the IPKF to Sri Lanka. (✅ owner 37 adv mini-timeline; ✅ Wikipedia: Indo-Sri Lanka Accord)
**Breakdown:**
- Date and place: 29 July 1987, Colombo (✅ Wikipedia)
- Signatories: Rajiv Gandhi; J.R. Jayewardene (✅ Wikipedia)
- Mechanism: provincial councils; Northern and Eastern merger subject to referendum; Tamil official language (✅ Wikipedia)
- 13th Amendment: intended by the accord; year not stated on the Wikipedia page (⚠️)
- IPKF induction followed; LTTE refused to disarm and rejected the accord (✅ Wikipedia)
- India ended support for Tamil separatism; no independent Tamil Eelam (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 29 July 1987.
**Links:** MOD-5136, MOD-5123.
**Trap:** The accord did not create an independent state; do not describe it as a separatist settlement.
**Mains hook:** Use as an example of an external military commitment that failed to bind the non-signatory armed group.
**Back:** *"29 July 1987: Rajiv and Jayewardene; IPKF in; LTTE out."*

### Flashcard MOD-5138 — The Mandal Commission: appointed 1979, report December 1980
**Front:** What was the Mandal Commission, who appointed it, and what did its 1980 report estimate?
**Definition (learn):** The Socially and Educationally Backward Classes Commission under B.P. Mandal, appointed in 1979 by the Janata government under Morarji Desai, reporting in December 1980. (✅ Wikipedia: Mandal Commission)
**Breakdown:**
- Appointed: 1979, Morarji Desai (✅ Wikipedia)
- Chairman: B.P. Mandal (✅ Wikipedia)
- Report: December 1980 (the 31 December date is not confirmed by the page) (✅ Wikipedia)
- Estimate: OBCs at 52 per cent of population; 3,743 castes (✅ Wikipedia)
- Data base: 1931 census (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1979; December 1980; 52%.
**Links:** MOD-5139, MOD-5140, MOD-5143.
**Trap:** The commission was set up in 1979, not in 1990; the 1990 event was implementation.
**Mains hook:** "Mandal and Mandir reshaped Indian politics after 1989." Mandal began as a 1979–80 report.
**Back:** *"1979 set up, 1980 report, 52 per cent OBC."*

### Flashcard MOD-5139 — Mandal implementation (7 August 1990): 27 per cent OBC quota
**Front:** When did V.P. Singh announce Mandal implementation, what was the quota, and what was the total reservation?
**Definition (learn):** On 7 August 1990, V.P. Singh's National Front announced 27 per cent reservation for OBCs in central government jobs, with the Government Order issued on 13 August. (✅ Wikipedia: Mandal Commission; ✅ owner 37)
**Breakdown:**
- Announcement: 7 August 1990 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Government Order: 13 August 1990 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Quota: 27 per cent for OBCs in central jobs and higher-education seats (✅ Wikipedia)
- Total: with SC/ST 22.5 per cent, about 49.5 per cent (✅ Wikipedia)
- Owner conflict: basic 37 says do not state ceilings; adv 37 states "~49.5%" (⚠️ conflict noted)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 7 Aug 1990; 13 Aug 1990; 27%; 49.5%.
**Links:** MOD-5138, MOD-5140, MOD-5135, MOD-5143.
**Trap:** The quota was 27 per cent, not 50 per cent. The 50 per cent figure is a ceiling, not the quota.
