**Mains hook:** "Mandal reshaped north Indian politics by organising backward castes as an electoral bloc."
**Back:** *"7 August 1990: 27 per cent for OBCs; with SC/ST, about 49.5."*

### Flashcard MOD-5140 — Indra Sawhney judgment (16 November 1992)
**Front:** What did the Indra Sawhney judgment hold on Mandal and when was it delivered?
**Definition (learn):** The nine-judge bench judgment of 16 November 1992 that upheld the 27 per cent OBC reservation, set a 50 per cent ceiling and excluded the creamy layer. (✅ Wikipedia: Mandal Commission)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 16 November 1992 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Bench: nine judges (✅ Wikipedia)
- Upheld: 27 per cent (✅ Wikipedia)
- Ceiling: 50 per cent (✅ Wikipedia)
- Creamy layer exclusion for OBCs (✅ Wikipedia)
- Earlier: a five-judge bench issued a stay (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 16 Nov 1992.
**Links:** MOD-5139, MOD-5143.
**Trap:** The judgment is 1992; the policy is 1990. Do not conflate.
**Mains hook:** Use the judgment as the constitutional and legal resolution of the policy (Polity owner for detail).
**Back:** *"16 Nov 1992: 27 upheld, 50 capped, creamy layer out."*

### Flashcard MOD-5141 — Advani's rath yatra (1990): Ayodhya mobilisation
**Front:** What did Advani's 1990 rath yatra do to the Ayodhya mobilisation?
**Definition (learn):** The 1990 rath yatra led by L.K. Advani intensified the Ayodhya mobilisation, one of the two parallel mobilisations of 1990. (✅ owner 37 §1, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 1990 (✅ owner 37)
- Leader: L.K. Advani (⚠️ name standard; owner says "Advani")
- Effect: intensified Ayodhya mobilisation (✅ owner 37)
- Parallel: Mandal (MOD-5139) (✅ owner 37)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1990.
**Links:** MOD-5142, MOD-5143, MOD-5139.
**Trap:** Mandal and Mandir were parallel but different mobilisations; do not merge.
**Mains hook:** "Mandal, Mandir and Market" as the three forces of 1989–92.
**Back:** *"1990: rath yatra, Mandir rises beside Mandal."*

### Flashcard MOD-5142 — Babri Masjid demolition (6 December 1992)
**Front:** When was the Babri Masjid demolished and how should the answer treat it?
**Definition (learn):** The Babri Masjid was demolished on 6 December 1992. (✅ owner 37 §4, §8.4 D)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 6 December 1992 (✅ owner 37)
- Not 1990 (✅ owner 37 §5 trap)
- Restraint: state the fact, date and political consequences only (✅ owner 37 §8.4 D)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 6 Dec 1992.
**Links:** MOD-5141, MOD-5143, MOD-5133.
**Trap:** Do not narrate the events of 1992 or assign communal responsibility (✅ owner 37 §8.6).
**Mains hook:** Use it as a dated marker of the Mandir turn.
**Back:** *"6 December 1992: the date, nothing more."*

### Flashcard MOD-5143 — Trap: Mandal and Mandir are not the same
**Front:** Why must Mandal (caste) and Mandir (Ayodhya) be kept apart in an answer?
**Definition (learn):** Two parallel 1990 mobilisations: Mandal divided the electorate by caste; Mandir sought to unite it by religion. (✅ owner 37 §5, §8.4 D)
**Breakdown:**
- Mandal: OBC reservation (27%), caste axis (MOD-5139)
- Mandir: rath yatra (1990), Babri (1992) (MOD-5141, MOD-5142)
- Effect: cross-cutting cleavages → no single national majority → coalition era
- Trap: do not telescope 1990 and 1992 into one event (✅ owner 37 §9)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1990; 1992.
**Links:** MOD-5138, MOD-5139, MOD-5140, MOD-5141, MOD-5142, MOD-5146.
**Trap:** Mandal is not the Babri demolition; the Indra Sawhney judgment is 1992, the Babri demolition is 1992, and Mandal is 1990.
**Mains hook:** "Mandal and Mandir reshaped politics after 1989." Discuss.
**Back:** *"Mandal divides by caste; Mandir unites by faith; both in 1990."*

### Flashcard MOD-5144 — The 1991 balance-of-payments crisis and liberalisation (Rao–Manmohan)
**Front:** Why did 1991 become the turning point for economic policy, and who carried it out?
**Definition (learn):** A balance-of-payments crisis in 1991 forced liberalisation under the Narasimha Rao government with Manmohan Singh as Finance Minister. (✅ owner 37 §1, §8.4 E; ✅ owner 38 Bank A)
**Breakdown:**
- Cause: balance-of-payments crisis (✅ owner 37)
- Government: Narasimha Rao (✅ owner 37)
- Finance Minister: Manmohan Singh (✅ owner 37)
- Significance: ended the Avadi-era model pursued for thirty-five years (✅ owner 37 adv)
- Political condition: a minority government could act without a mandate (✅ owner 37 §8.4 E)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1991.
**Links:** MOD-5145, MOD-5147, MOD-5170, MOD-5155.
**Trap:** Liberalisation was under Rao, not Rajiv (✅ owner 37 §5, §8.8).
**Mains hook:** "Assess 1991 as a turning point in India's political economy." Crisis-enabled reform thesis.
**Back:** *"1991: crisis forces the turn; Rao acts; Manmohan steers."*

### Flashcard MOD-5145 — Rajiv Gandhi's assassination (1991)
**Front:** When was Rajiv Gandhi assassinated and what is the owner's date control?
**Definition (learn):** Rajiv Gandhi was assassinated in 1991, the same year as the economic reforms. (✅ owner 37 §1, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 1991 (✅ owner 37)
- Context: the year liberalisation began
- Month: ⚠️ standard (May 1991) not stated in owner
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1991.
**Links:** MOD-5144, MOD-5123.
**Trap:** Rajiv's death was not the cause of reform; the reform was Rao's.
**Mains hook:** The coincidence of 1991 dates is a sequence to keep distinct.
**Back:** *"1991: the Rajiv death and the reform year coincide."*

### Flashcard MOD-5146 — Coalition era (1996–99): BJP, United Front and NDA
**Front:** What governments followed the 1989 National Front to 1999, and what did they normalise?
**Definition (learn):** The decade of coalitions: a short BJP government under Vajpayee in 1996, the United Front under Deve Gowda and then Gujral, and the NDA under Vajpayee (1998–99). (✅ owner 37 §1, §2; ⚠️ 13-day length standard)
**Breakdown:**
- 1996: short BJP government under Vajpayee; 13 days (⚠️ standard)
- United Front: Deve Gowda, then I.K. Gujral (✅ owner 37)
- NDA: Vajpayee, 1998–99 (✅ owner 37)
- NDA tally in 1999: about 296 with allies (⚠️ owner adv, not verified)
- Normalised: multi-party coalition governance (✅ owner 37 adv §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1996; 1998–99.
**Links:** MOD-5135, MOD-5136, MOD-5143, MOD-5147.
**Trap:** Vajpayee's first government was 1996, not 1998; the NDA was 1998–99.
**Mains hook:** "Analyse the rise of coalition governance and the decline of one-party dominance (1989–99)."
**Back:** *"1996 BJP brief, UF two PMs, NDA 1998–99: coalitions normal."*

### Flashcard MOD-5147 — System-change engine: 1984 to 1999 causal chain
**Front:** What is the owner's causal chain from the 1984 majority to coalition government?
**Definition (learn):** A chain from one-party majority (1984), through identity misjudgements, Bofors and fragmentation, to Mandal and Mandir, the 1991 BoP crisis and coalitions as the normal form. (✅ owner 37 §8.5; ⚠️ causal framing)
**Breakdown:**
- 1984: single-party record majority
- 1985–86: identity misjudgements (Shah Bano; shrine opening)
- 1987: Bofors destroys the coalition of support
- 1989: National Front minority government
- 1990: Mandal (caste) and Mandir (religion) mobilise the electorate differently
- No national majority possible
- 1991: BoP crisis and minority government; liberalisation without a mandate
- 1996–99: coalition government is normal
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 37 §8.5).
**Links:** MOD-5123, MOD-5132, MOD-5134, MOD-5136, MOD-5143, MOD-5144, MOD-5146.
**Trap:** Keep the 1990 and 1992 events separate even within the chain.
**Mains hook:** Use the chain to answer "Trace the transformation of Indian politics 1984–2000."
**Back:** *"Majority, misjudge, Bofors, Mandal and Mandir, crisis, coalition."*

### Flashcard MOD-5148 — Trap: anti-defection under Rajiv and liberalisation under Rao
**Front:** Which two attributions does the owner correct?
**Definition (learn):** Two traps: the Anti-Defection Act was under Rajiv (1985), and liberalisation began under Rao in 1991. (✅ owner 37 §5)
**Breakdown:**
- Anti-Defection: 52nd Amendment (1985) under Rajiv Gandhi, not V.P. Singh (MOD-5127)
- Liberalisation: 1991 under Narasimha Rao, not Rajiv (MOD-5144)
- Also: Rajiv began a modernising shift (⚠️ owner adv §5)
- Also: Vajpayee's first government was 1996 (MOD-5146)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1985; 1991; 1996.
**Links:** MOD-5127, MOD-5144, MOD-5146.
**Trap:** Use the corrected attributions.
**Mains hook:** Attribution errors cost marks; check the PM at each date.
**Back:** *"Defection law: Rajiv 1985. Reform: Rao 1991."*

### Flashcard MOD-5149 — Historiography: decline versus democratic deepening (1989–99)
**Front:** How do the two readings of 1989–99 differ?
**Definition (learn):** Chandra reads the decade as the unravelling of the Nehruvian consensus under Mandal, Mandir and Market; critics argue it deepened democracy by including subordinated castes, regions and markets. (✅ owner 37 adv historiography note; ⚠️ attribution)
**Breakdown:**
- Chandra: three simultaneous pressures
- Critics: democratic deepening; elite consensus replaced by competition
- Synthesis: a crisis of the old order and a deepening of democracy at once
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 37 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5147, MOD-5044, MOD-5075.
**Trap:** Do not present the decline narrative as the only reading.
**Mains hook:** "Changing nature of Indian politics since the late 1980s."
**Back:** *"Chandra: unravelling. Critics: deepening. Both are true in part."*

### Flashcard MOD-5150 — Chronology ladder: 1984–2000 (Rajiv to the millennium)
**Front:** What is the chronology ladder from the 1984 election to the 1999 NDA?
**Definition (learn):** A ladder of the owner's anchor events in this period. (✅ owner 37 mini-timeline; ✅ Wikipedia where stated)
**Breakdown:**
- Dec 1984: election (404 of 514)
- 1985: Anti-Defection Act; Punjab (24 Jul); Assam (15 Aug)
- 1986: Shah Bano reversal; Mizo (30 Jun); Ayodhya shrine
- 1987: Bofors; Jan Morcha; Indo-SL (29 Jul)
- 1988: Janata Dal / National Front
- 1989: Congress defeated (197); V.P. Singh
- 1990: Mandal (7 Aug); rath yatra
- 1991: BoP crisis; reforms; Rajiv assassinated
- 16 Nov 1992: Indra Sawhney
- 6 Dec 1992: Babri demolition
- 1996: BJP short government; UF
- 1998–99: NDA under Vajpayee
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ as cited).
**Links:** MOD-5124 to MOD-5147.
**Trap:** Separate 1990 (Mandal) and 1992 (Babri and Indra Sawhney).
**Mains hook:** Use as the spine of the 1984–2000 answer.
**Back:** *"84 mandate, 85 accords, 86 Mizo, 87 Bofors, 89 defeat, 90 Mandal, 91 reform, 92 two events, 98 NDA."*

### GROUP — 38 · Economy, Land, Society & State: A Post-Independence Synthesis

### Flashcard MOD-5151 — Concept: the developmental state, growth with democracy and an equity deficit
**Front:** What is the owner's synthesis of the post-independence developmental state?
**Definition (learn):** The thesis that the Nehruvian developmental state delivered growth and democracy but under-delivered on equity, with the licence-permit raj as its structural drag and 1991 as a correction, not a surrender. (✅ owner 38 adv §1; ⚠️ thesis wording)
**Breakdown:**
- Model: planning, import substitution, public-sector core (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Drag: licence-permit-quota raj (⚠️ owner 38 adv)
- Correction: 1991 changed instruments, not goals (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Democracy: free press and popular sovereignty helped avert coercive extraction and famine (⚠️ owner 38 adv)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1948–1991 span (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5152, MOD-5153, MOD-5155, MOD-5176, MOD-5181.
**Trap:** The model was not a failure from the start; it delivered growth and capacity before the control raj became its drag.
**Mains hook:** "The developmental state delivered growth and democracy but under-delivered on equity." Critically examine.
**Back:** *"Growth and democracy yes, equity short — 1991 corrected the tools."*

### Flashcard MOD-5152 — Industrial Policy Resolutions (1948 and 1956): the commanding heights
**Front:** What did the IPRs of 1948 and 1956 establish for the public sector?
**Definition (learn):** The two Industrial Policy Resolutions that defined the mixed economy and the public sector's role, especially in heavy industry. (✅ owner 38 §1, §8.4 A)
**Breakdown:**
- IPR 1948: mixed economy defined (✅ owner 38)
- IPR 1956: public-sector "commanding heights" (✅ owner 38)
- Linked: Mahalanobis Second Plan (1956) (MOD-5008)
- Self-reliance as capital-goods capacity (✅ owner 38 Thread 1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1948; 1956 (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5008, MOD-5151, MOD-5170.
**Trap:** The 1956 IPR is not the 1991 reform; the 1991 change dismantled the 1956 frame later.
**Mains hook:** Use the two IPRs as the institutional base of the planned economy.
**Back:** *"1948 and 1956: the state takes the commanding heights."*

### Flashcard MOD-5153 — The licence-permit raj: IDRA 1951, MRTP and small-scale reservation
**Front:** What controls formed the licence-permit raj and why did they throttle the economy?
**Definition (learn):** The web of licences, permits and controls that protected inefficiency and limited scale and competition, which the owner dates to the 1951 framework. (✅ owner 38 adv §1)
**Breakdown:**
- IDRA 1951 (Industries Development and Regulation Act) (✅ owner 38 adv §1)
- MRTP (Monopolies and Restrictive Trade Practices) controls (✅ owner 38 adv §1)
- Small-scale reservation (✅ owner 38 adv §1)
- Effect: efficiency and productivity eroded by the 1980s (✅ owner 38 adv §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1951 (IDRA); 1980s productivity erosion (✅ owner 38 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5151, MOD-5155, MOD-5144.
**Trap:** Do not attribute the whole stagnation to the control regime alone; the owner calls it a structural flaw.
**Mains hook:** "Why did India's growth lag until 1991?" Use the control raj with the 1980s productivity point.
**Back:** *"IDRA 1951: licences, MRTP, small-scale reserved, efficiency lost."*

### Flashcard MOD-5154 — Growth of about 4 per cent to the mid-1960s and the date trap
**Front:** What growth rate does the owner attribute to India, and what is its time limit?
**Definition (learn):** About 4 per cent a year to the mid-1960s, which far exceeded the colonial rate. (✅ owner 38 adv §8.4 A, §8.12)
**Breakdown:**
- Rate: about 4 per cent per year (✅ owner 38 §1)
- Period: to the mid-1960s only (✅ owner 38 adv §8.4 A)
- Comparison: exceeded the colonial rate (✅ owner 38)
- Caution: do not extend to later decades or substitute rates (✅ owner 38 adv §8.4 A)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ~4% to mid-1960s (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5018, MOD-5008, MOD-5151.
**Trap:** The 4 per cent is not a 1991-era or post-1980 growth figure.
**Mains hook:** Use the period-limited figure to argue for the state's early success.
**Back:** *"Four per cent, until the mid-sixties, no further."*

### Flashcard MOD-5155 — 1991 reforms as correction, not repudiation (Chandra)
**Front:** How does Chandra present the 1991 reforms and why does the framing matter?
**Definition (learn):** The 1991 reforms kept the goals of growth, self-reliance and poverty removal but changed the instruments, which the owner calls a correction of excessive dirigisme. (✅ owner 38 adv §1, §5)
**Breakdown:**
- Goals retained: growth, self-reliance, poverty removal (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Instruments changed: markets, deregulation (⚠️ owner interpretation)
- Crisis: balance-of-payments crisis, 1991 (✅ owner 38)
- Framing: a correction of a success, not repudiation of a failure (⚠️ owner verdict)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1991 (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5144, MOD-5151, MOD-5153, MOD-5181.
**Trap:** Chandra's framing is not the market-liberal framing; it is one of three schools (MOD-5020).
**Mains hook:** "Assess 1991 as a turning point." Use the correction-not-repudiation reading with the market-liberal counter-reading.
**Back:** *"1991: same goals, new tools."*

### Flashcard MOD-5156 — Zamindari abolition: about 20 million superior tenants became owners
**Front:** How far did zamindari abolition go and what did it achieve?
**Definition (learn):** The abolition of intermediary tenures (zamindari), largely completed by about 1956, which made about 20 million superior tenants owners. (✅ owner 38 §1, §4; ✅ owner 38 adv §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Target: intermediaries (zamindars), politically weak
- Result: about 20 million superior tenants became owners (✅ owner 38)
- Timing: largely by 1956 (✅ owner 38 §9)
- Backing: First (1951) and Fourth (1955) Amendments (MOD-5157)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ~20 million; ~1956.
**Links:** MOD-5157, MOD-5158, MOD-5161, MOD-5162.
**Trap:** Zamindari abolition succeeded; tenancy and ceiling reform did not (see MOD-5162).
**Mains hook:** "Zamindari abolition succeeded where tenancy and ceiling reforms failed." Analyse the causes.
**Back:** *"Zamindari gone by 1956; twenty million tenants own the land."*

### Flashcard MOD-5157 — First (1951) and Fourth (1955) Amendments: protecting land-reform laws
**Front:** Which amendments protected land-reform laws, and from what?
**Definition (learn):** The First Amendment (1951) and Fourth Amendment (1955) protected land-reform laws from challenge in court. (✅ owner 38 adv §4)
**Breakdown:**
- First Amendment: 1951 (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Fourth Amendment: 1955 (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Function: shielded land laws from litigation (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Polity link: detailed constitutional doctrine is Polity-owned (⚠️ owner 38 adv §3)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1951; 1955 (✅ owner 38 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5156, MOD-5158, MOD-5088.
**Trap:** The amendments protected land laws; they did not themselves redistribute land.
**Mains hook:** Use to show how the state protected reform while implementation lagged.
**Back:** *"1951 and 1955: land laws shielded from the courts."*

### Flashcard MOD-5158 — Tenancy and ceiling reform failed: evasion, benami and collusion
**Front:** Why did tenancy and ceiling reform largely fail?
**Definition (learn):** Tenancy and ceiling reforms were defeated by "personal cultivation" loopholes, benami transfers, poor records, delay, evasion and official collusion. (✅ owner 38 §1, §8.5; ✅ owner 38 adv §1)
**Breakdown:**
- Tenancy: oral and concealed tenancies poorly recorded; evictions (✅ owner 38)
- Ceilings: evasion through benami and family partition (✅ owner 38 §8.5)
- Loophole: personal cultivation (✅ owner 38 adv §1)
- Political logic: the rural classes targeted were the state's own political base (✅ owner 38 §8.5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5156, MOD-5159, MOD-5161, MOD-5162.
**Trap:** Do not lump all land reform together; zamindari succeeded and tenancy and ceiling failed.
**Mains hook:** Explanatory variable: the political weight of the class being expropriated.
**Back:** *"Tenancy and ceilings: evaded, recorded badly, protected by the powerful."*

### Flashcard MOD-5159 — Tenancy-area estimate: 42 per cent falling to 20–25 per cent
**Front:** What rough tenancy figures does the advanced owner give and why are they unreliable?
**Definition (learn):** A rough estimate that tenancy area fell from about 42 per cent (1950–51) to about 20–25 per cent by the early 1960s, with the owner warning the data are unreliable. (✅ owner 38 adv §1, §4)
**Breakdown:**
- 1950–51: about 42 per cent (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Early 1960s: about 20–25 per cent (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Caveat: oral tenancy undercounted; eviction contributed to the decline (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Do not cite as precise (✅ owner 38 §8.5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 42%; 20–25%; caveats (✅ owner 38 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5158, MOD-5156, MOD-5162.
**Trap:** The fall is not proof of success: some of it came through eviction.
**Mains hook:** Use the caveat to show why tenancy reform cannot be judged by a single statistic.
**Back:** *"Forty-two falling to twenty-five, but evictions made it look better."*

### Flashcard MOD-5160 — Bhoodan and Gramdan (Vinoba Bhave, Pochampalli 1951)
**Front:** What was the Bhoodan movement, where did it start, and why could it not substitute for reform?
**Definition (learn):** Vinoba Bhave's voluntary land-gift movement, which began at Pochampalli in 1951, and its Gramdan extension. (✅ owner 38 §1, §4, §8.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Leader: Vinoba Bhave (✅ owner 38)
- Start: Pochampalli, 1951 (✅ owner 38)
- Limit: voluntary donation cannot reallocate land at scale (✅ owner 38 §8.5)
- Outcome: much donated land was never effectively distributed (✅ owner 38 §8.5)
- Gramdan: village-level land donation (✅ owner 38 adv §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1951 (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5158, MOD-5161, MOD-5162.
**Trap:** Bhoodan donations were not redistribution; the owner says much was never distributed.
**Mains hook:** Use as an example of voluntary means failing structural reform.
**Back:** *"Pochampalli 1951: a gift is not a redistribution."*

### Flashcard MOD-5161 — Land-reform disaggregation table: zamindari, tenancy, ceilings and Bhoodan
**Front:** How does the owner's component table explain land-reform outcomes?
**Definition (learn):** A four-row table separating zamindari abolition, tenancy reform, ceiling legislation and Bhoodan, with their outcomes and reasons. (✅ owner 38 §8.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Zamindari abolition: largely succeeded; ~20 million owners; clear target, political will, weak intermediaries
- Tenancy reform: largely failed; unreliable records, evasion, eviction
- Ceiling legislation: largely failed; benami and family partition
- Bhoodan: could not substitute for structural reform; land not effectively distributed
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 38 §8.5).
**Links:** MOD-5156, MOD-5158, MOD-5160, MOD-5162.
**Trap:** The explanatory variable is the political weight of the expropriated class.
**Mains hook:** The table is the answer to "zamindari succeeded where tenancy failed."
**Back:** *"Zamindari yes; tenancy and ceilings no; Bhoodan a gift."*

### Flashcard MOD-5162 — Trap: zamindari versus tenancy and Bhoodan redistribution
**Front:** Which land-reform misreadings does the owner correct?
**Definition (learn):** Three paired traps from owner 38 §5.
**Breakdown:**
- All land reforms succeeded equally? No: zamindari worked; tenancy and ceiling mostly failed
- Bhoodan redistributed most donated land? No: much was never effectively distributed
- Tenancy-area fall proves success? No: some came through eviction (MOD-5159)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 38 §5).
**Links:** MOD-5156, MOD-5158, MOD-5160, MOD-5161.
**Trap:** Keep the component distinction in every land answer.
**Mains hook:** Use the corrections to frame the land-reform verdict.
**Back:** *"Zamindari yes, tenancy no, Bhoodan not redistribution."*

### Flashcard MOD-5163 — The Green Revolution: HYV package and the start-date conflict
**Front:** What was the Green Revolution's package and when did it start, given the owner and Wikipedia?
**Definition (learn):** The high-yielding-variety (HYV) package for wheat, first concentrated in Punjab, Haryana and western UP, which raised foodgrain output and made India food-self-sufficient. (✅ owner 38 §2, §8.6; ✅ Wikipedia: Green Revolution in India)
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: HYV package from the mid-1960s, initially Punjab, Haryana and western UP (✅ owner 38)
- Wikipedia: first introduced in Punjab in late 1966–67; says the Indian programme "commenced in 1968" (✅ Wikipedia)
- Wikipedia: by 1970 Punjab produced 70 per cent of the country's foodgrains (✅ Wikipedia)
- Drought background: 1964–65 and 1965–66 (✅ Wikipedia)
- Later diffusion to rice regions and other states (✅ owner 38 §8.6)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** mid-1960s (owner); late 1966–67 Punjab (Wikipedia); 70% of foodgrains by 1970 (Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5164, MOD-5165, MOD-5159, MOD-5168.
**Trap:** Conflict: owner "mid-1960s" vs Wikipedia's 1966–68 start: state "mid-1960s" as the owner's period and flag the Wikipedia date.
**Mains hook:** The output achievement is the first point; distribution is the contested second point.
**Back:** *"Mid-sixties HYV, Punjab 1966–67, 70 per cent by 1970."*

### Flashcard MOD-5164 — The architects of the Green Revolution: Borlaug, Swaminathan and Subramaniam
**Front:** Who are the key figures behind India's Green Revolution?
**Definition (learn):** The people associated with the programme: Norman Borlaug (global framework), M.S. Swaminathan (chief architect in India) and C. Subramaniam (the political minister). (✅ Wikipedia: Green Revolution in India)
**Breakdown:**
- Norman Borlaug: global agricultural research framework (✅ Wikipedia)
- M.S. Swaminathan: "Father of the Green Revolution in India"; ICAR director-general (✅ Wikipedia)
- C. Subramaniam: food and agriculture minister; "Political Father" (✅ Wikipedia)
- Dilbagh Singh Athwal: "Father of the Wheat Revolution" (✅ Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5163, MOD-5165.
**Trap:** Do not credit Borlaug as an Indian official; the programme was an Indian state effort with international research.
**Mains hook:** "The Green Revolution as state policy and international science."
**Back:** *"Borlaug the frame, Swaminathan the architect, Subramaniam the minister."*

### Flashcard MOD-5165 — The Green Revolution debate: Chandra versus the critics
**Front:** How do Chandra and the critical literature read the Green Revolution's distribution and ecology?
**Definition (learn):** Chandra argues the technology was broadly scale-neutral, spread beyond the north-west and did not produce general de-peasantisation; critics stress unequal access, regional lag, labour displacement and ecological cost. (✅ owner 38 §1, §8.6, adv §4; ✅ Wikipedia criticisms)
**Breakdown:**
- Chandra: food self-sufficiency achieved; diffusion; scale-neutral (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Critics: irrigation and credit access unequal; regional lags (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Ecology: fertiliser and pesticide pollution; soil degradation; groundwater falls (✅ Wikipedia)
- Debt: high-cost seed and inputs pushed small farmers into debt (✅ Wikipedia)
- Trap: do not attribute "widened inequality" to Chandra (✅ owner 38 adv historiography)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 38; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** MOD-5163, MOD-5164, MOD-5020.
**Trap:** Present both positions; asserting one alone is a factual-discipline failure.
**Mains hook:** "Assess the social contradictions unleashed by the Green Revolution."
**Back:** *"Chandra: diffusion and neutrality. Critics: access, regions, ecology."*

### Flashcard MOD-5166 — Naxalbari (1967), Srikakulam and CPI(ML) (1969)
**Front:** What was the Naxalbari uprising, where was it, and when was CPI(ML) formed?
**Definition (learn):** The 1967 uprising in Naxalbari, Darjeeling district, which launched the Naxalite movement; CPI(ML) was formed in 1969 and the Srikakulam struggle followed. (✅ owner 38 §2, §4, adv §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Naxalbari: 1967; Darjeeling district (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Srikakulam: struggle after 1967 (✅ owner 38 adv mini-timeline)
- CPI(ML): formed 1969 (✅ owner 38)
- Class content: landless and tenant struggles (✅ owner 38 §8.6)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1967; 1969.
**Links:** MOD-5167, MOD-5168, MOD-5169, MOD-5033.
**Trap:** Naxalbari was 1967, not the 1970s; do not merge it with the 1980s farmers' movements (✅ owner 38 §5, §8.6).
**Mains hook:** Use as the rural-class counter-example to the Green Revolution.
**Back:** *"1967 Naxalbari, 1969 CPI(ML), Srikakulam after."*

### Flashcard MOD-5167 — The 1980s farmers' movements: Nasik (1980) and Sisauli (1986)
**Front:** What were the two rich-farmer movements of the 1980s and who led them?
**Definition (learn):** Market-oriented farmers' movements: Sharad Joshi's Shetkari Sanghatana at Nasik (1980) and Mahendra Singh Tikait's BKU at Sisauli (1986). (✅ owner 38 §8.6, adv §4)
**Breakdown:**
- Nasik, 1980: Sharad Joshi (Shetkari Sanghatana) (✅ owner 38)
- Sisauli, 1986: Mahendra Singh Tikait (BKU) (✅ owner 38)
- Demands: prices, inputs, credit (✅ owner 38 §8.6)
- Class content: surplus producers, not landless (✅ owner 38 §8.6)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1980; 1986 (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5166, MOD-5168.
**Trap:** Do not merge with Naxalbari; they represent opposed rural interests (✅ owner 38 §8.6).
**Mains hook:** The shift from landless struggle to market demand is evidence of the Green Revolution's class effects.
**Back:** *"1980 Nasik and 1986 Sisauli: market farmers, not landless."*

### Flashcard MOD-5168 — Agrarian distress and regime change: the 1857–1977 thread
**Front:** Why did agrarian distress produce regime change only sometimes?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's thread: distress rarely produced regime change on its own; it did so when it coincided with a legitimacy crisis (1857, 1967, 1975–77), and otherwise produced remedial legislation (1879) or localised revolt. (✅ owner 38 §8.9 Thread 2; ⚠️ analytic pattern)
**Breakdown:**
- 1857: taluqdar dispossession; revolt (✅ owner 38)
- 1859–1900: Indigo, Pabna, Deccan riots → Deccan Agriculturists' Relief Act, 1879 (✅ owner 38)
- 1917–22: Champaran, Kheda, Awadh (✅ owner 38)
- 1928–34: Bardoli; no-tax campaigns (✅ owner 38)
- 1946–47: Tebhaga and Telangana (✅ owner 38)
- 1965–67: food aid, devaluation; 1967 election (✅ owner 38)
- 1972–77: monsoons, inflation; JP and Emergency (✅ owner 38)
- Mirror pattern: relief and technology, not redistribution (✅ owner 38 §8.9)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 38 §8.9).
**Links:** MOD-5059, MOD-5077, MOD-5163, MOD-5169, MOD-5181.
**Trap:** Distress alone rarely changes regimes; the legitimacy crisis is the variable.
**Mains hook:** "Agrarian distress and state response across colonial, Nehruvian and post-1967 regimes."
**Back:** *"Distress plus legitimacy crisis equals regime change; distress alone, a 1879 Act."*

### Flashcard MOD-5169 — Tebhaga and Telangana (1946–47): agrarian radicalism at the transfer of power
**Front:** What did Tebhaga and Telangana demand at the moment of transfer of power?
**Definition (learn):** Peasant movements in 1946–47 over sharecropping shares, forced labour and landlord power, which shaped the new state's land agenda. (✅ owner 38 §8.9 Thread 2; ✅ owner 23 basic cross-ref)
**Breakdown:**
- Tebhaga: sharecropping shares (✅ owner 38)
- Telangana: forced labour; landlord power (✅ owner 38)
- Timing: 1946–47 (✅ owner 38)
- Effect: agrarian radicalism shaping land agenda (✅ owner 38)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1946–47 (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5168, MOD-5166.
**Trap:** Tebhaga and Telangana are different regional movements; do not merge them.
**Mains hook:** Use as the pre-independence agrarian pressure on the land agenda.
**Back:** *"1946-47: sharecroppers and Telangana press the new state's land agenda."*

### Flashcard MOD-5170 — Thread: "self-reliance" from 1905 to 1991
**Front:** How did the content of "self-reliance" change between 1905 and 1991?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's thread: the word stayed the same but its content changed from village production to fiscal autonomy, capital-goods capacity, insulation and finally competitiveness, each after a failure of the last. (✅ owner 38 §8.9 Thread 1; ⚠️ thesis)
**Breakdown:**
- 1905 Swadeshi: boycott, indigenous enterprise, National Council of Education (1906) (✅ owner 38)
- 1920–47 Gandhian: khadi and charkha; village production (✅ owner 38)
- 1928–39 Nationalist economics: protective tariffs; lower rupee-sterling ratio; coastal shipping reserved (✅ owner 38)
- 1948–66 Planning: capital-goods capacity; IPR and Second Plan (✅ owner 38)
- 1966–80 deepened autarky: controls; licensing; bank nationalisation (✅ owner 38)
- Mid-1960s onward: food self-reliance; Green Revolution (✅ owner 38)
- 1991 onward: competitiveness and external solvency (✅ owner 38)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 38 §8.9).
**Links:** MOD-5152, MOD-5163, MOD-5155, MOD-5064, MOD-5181.
**Trap:** Do not treat self-reliance as one unchanging idea; the owner says the error is what the question tests.
**Mains hook:** "Self-reliance as one idea or many?" The continuity of the word with changing content.
**Back:** *"Swadeshi, Khadi, capital goods, insulation, competitiveness: one word, five meanings."*

### Flashcard MOD-5171 — Caste: untouchability abolished, reservations, and the political trajectory
**Front:** What did the state do on caste and how did caste become political?
**Definition (learn):** Untouchability was abolished in law and reservations extended; the political trajectory ran from Ambedkar through the Dalit Panthers and the BSP to Mandal (1990). (✅ owner 38 §1, §8.7; ⚠️ Article 17 reference is standard, not in owner)
**Breakdown:**
- Legal: untouchability abolished (✅ owner 38; ⚠️ Article 17)
- Reservations: extended (✅ owner 38)
- Political: Ambedkar → Dalit Panthers → BSP → Mandal 1990 (✅ owner 38 §8.7)
- Dalit Panther and BSP dates are not stated in the owner (⚠️ do not state)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mandal 1990 (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5139, MOD-5174, MOD-5175, MOD-5181.
**Trap:** Formal abolition did not end social hierarchy; the owner's "law versus society" gap.
**Mains hook:** "Trace the transformation of caste and gender relations since 1947."
**Back:** *"Abolish untouchability in law; caste goes political from Ambedkar to Mandal."*

### Flashcard MOD-5172 — Hindu Code Bill: four separate Acts (1955–56)
**Front:** Which Acts did the Hindu Code become, and what did they change?
**Definition (learn):** The Hindu Code Bill was enacted as four separate Acts: the Hindu Marriage Act (1955) and the Hindu Succession, Hindu Minority and Guardianship, and Hindu Adoptions and Maintenance Acts (1956). (✅ owner 38 §8.7, §9)
**Breakdown:**
- Hindu Marriage Act, 1955 (✅ owner 38)
- Hindu Succession Act, 1956 (✅ owner 38)
- Hindu Minority and Guardianship Act, 1956 (✅ owner 38)
- Hindu Adoptions and Maintenance Act, 1956 (✅ owner 38)
- Rights expanded: marriage, succession, guardianship, adoption, maintenance (✅ owner 38 §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1955; 1956 (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5173, MOD-5171, MOD-5174.
**Trap:** The Hindu Code was not a single enacted code; do not describe it as one (✅ owner 38 §5, §8.12).
**Mains hook:** Formal rights advanced; complete equality did not follow.
**Back:** *"Four Acts, 1955-56: marriage, succession, guardianship, adoption."*

### Flashcard MOD-5173 — The women's movement (1970s–1990s): dowry, rape and domestic violence
**Front:** What did the new women's movement take up after the legal reforms?
**Definition (learn):** The women's movement of the 1970s–90s moved from legal reform to dowry, rape and domestic violence. (✅ owner 38 §1, §8.7)
**Breakdown:**
- Period: 1970s to 1990s (✅ owner 38)
- Issues: dowry, rape, domestic violence (✅ owner 38)
- Shift: from legal rights to social violence (✅ owner 38 adv §1)
- Implementation gaps and elite capture remained (✅ owner 38 adv §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1970s–90s (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5172, MOD-5171, MOD-5175.
**Trap:** Do not import contemporary statistics; the owner forbids them (✅ owner 38 §8.7).
**Mains hook:** "The law-versus-society gap produced movements rather than merely legislation."
**Back:** *"1970s-90s: dowry, rape, domestic violence put on the agenda."*

### Flashcard MOD-5174 — Communalism: revival from the 1960s and endemic after the late 1970s
**Front:** How did communalism develop after 1947, and what did Nehru say about it?
**Definition (learn):** Communalism was contained in the early years, revived from the 1960s and became entrenched after the late 1970s; the owner treats it as an ideology and not only riots. (✅ owner 38 §8.7, adv §1)
**Breakdown:**
- Early years: contained (✅ owner 38 adv)
- 1960s: revival (✅ owner 38)
- Late 1970s: endemic (✅ owner 38)
- Nehru's phrase "the Indian version of fascism" (attributed; ✅ owner 38 adv)
- National Integration Council under Nehru (✅ owner 38 adv §4)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1960s; late 1970s.
**Links:** MOD-5163, MOD-5172, MOD-5175, MOD-5141.
**Trap:** Communalism is not only riots; it is an ideology (✅ owner 38 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** "Communalism is an ideology, not merely a law-and-order problem."
**Back:** *"1960s revival, late-70s endemic: an ideology, not just riots."*

### Flashcard MOD-5175 — Institutional decay: Parliament, Cabinet, PMO, defection and the judiciary
**Front:** What institutional decline does Chandra document in the post-1960s state?
**Definition (learn):** Chandra's "crisis of governability": decline of Parliament, erosion of the Cabinet by the PMO from 1969, defection curbed only in 1985, a conservative judiciary and an unreformed bureaucracy and police. (✅ owner 38 adv §1; ⚠️ attribution)
**Breakdown:**
- Parliament: disruption from the late 1960s (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Cabinet: eroded by a dominant PMO from 1969 (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Defection: curbed only in 1985 (✅ owner 38 adv; MOD-5127)
- Judiciary: conservative and delay-ridden (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Bureaucracy and police: unreformed, coercive (✅ owner 38 adv)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** late 1960s; 1969; 1985.
**Links:** MOD-5127, MOD-5034, MOD-5176, MOD-5177.
**Trap:** Institutional decay did not end democracy; the owner says democracy survived and deepened (✅ owner 38 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** "Institutions decayed even as democracy endured."
**Back:** *"Parliament disrupted, PMO rising, defection curbed 1985, courts slow."*

### Flashcard MOD-5176 — Democracy shaped development: free press, famine and coercion
**Front:** What is the owner's argument that democracy shaped development?
**Definition (learn):** The thesis that a free press helped avert famines and popular sovereignty prevented the coercive extraction of surplus from peasants and workers seen in authoritarian industrialisers. (✅ owner 38 adv §1; ⚠️ owner interpretation)
**Breakdown:**
- Free press: helped avert famines (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Popular sovereignty: prevented coercive extraction (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Comparison: authoritarian industrialisers (⚠️ owner)
- Limit: equity still under-delivered (✅ owner 38 adv)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 38 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5151, MOD-5175, MOD-5177.
**Trap:** Do not state the famine argument as a settled fact; it is the owner's interpretation.
**Mains hook:** "Democracy and development: complements or trade-off?" Use the owner's thesis with the equity qualification.
**Back:** *"A free press averted famine; sovereignty stopped coercive surplus extraction."*

### Flashcard MOD-5177 — The millennium balance sheet: grand success and major failure
**Front:** What is the owner's verdict on the post-independence record by 2000?
**Definition (learn):** The mixed balance sheet: the grand success is the survival and deepening of democracy and a capable state; the major failure is persistent poverty, competitive populism, unsustainable subsidies and weak governance. (✅ owner 38 adv §1; ⚠️ verdict)
**Breakdown:**
- Success: democracy's survival and deepening (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Success: capable state (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Failure: persistent poverty (✅ owner 38 adv)
- Failure: competitive populism; unsustainable subsidies; weak governance (✅ owner 38 adv)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 38 adv).
**Links:** MOD-5019, MOD-5175, MOD-5176, MOD-5181.
**Trap:** Do not present only one column; the owner's verdict requires both.
**Mains hook:** "Assess India's post-independence development record." Use the two columns and a graded verdict.
**Back:** *"Democracy and capable state; poverty, populism, weak governance."*

### Flashcard MOD-5178 — PYQ 2019 Prelims: land-reform legislation (direct demand)
**Front:** What did the 2019 Prelims GS-I (Q2) test, and is the owner able to answer it?
**Definition (learn):** The routed demand on land-reform legislation in post-independence India, which the owner's land bank covers. (✅ owner 38 PYQ integration 2018–23)
**Breakdown:**
- Paper: Prelims GS-I, Q2 (2019) (✅ owner 38)
- Demand: land-reform legislation (✅ owner 38)
- Coverage: zamindari, tenancy, ceiling, Bhoodan (MOD-5161)
- Key: official key unavailable locally; answer not inferred (✅ owner 38)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019, Q2 (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5161, MOD-5156, MOD-5158.
**Trap:** Do not state the answer beyond the owner's verified land-reform content.
**Mains hook:** The land bank answers the 2019 demand's content.
**Back:** *"Q2, 2019: land reform, the four-part table."*

### Flashcard MOD-5179 — PYQ 2018 Prelims: founders of the Hind Mazdoor Sabha (gap)
**Front:** What did the 2018 Prelims (Q70) ask about the Hind Mazdoor Sabha, and why is it an explicit gap?
**Definition (learn):** A routed demand on the founders of the Hind Mazdoor Sabha (1948), which the owner's source books do not attest. (✅ owner 38 PYQ integration; ✅ owner 38 §8.12)
**Breakdown:**
- Paper: Prelims GS-I, Q70 (2018) (✅ owner 38)
- Demand: founders of the 1948 labour organisation (✅ owner 38)
- Gap: unattested in the repository's source books (✅ owner 38 §8.12)
- Owner action: no guess; split-and-federation history covered analytically in basic 23 (✅ owner 38 §8.12)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1948 (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5169, MOD-5166.
**Trap:** Do not supply founders from memory; mark as unverified.
**Mains hook:** No direct Mains route; use as a Prelims verification gap.
**Back:** *"Hind Mazdoor Sabha 1948: unattested here; verify before answering."*

### Flashcard MOD-5180 — Seven current-affairs routing artefacts (not history demands)
**Front:** Which seven routed Prelims demands in owner 38 should the aspirant not answer from history?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's classification of seven routed demands as current-affairs artefacts with no modern-history content. (✅ owner 38 §8.12, PYQ blocks)
**Breakdown:**
- Exercise Mitra Shakti-2023 (2024, Q82)
- Gandhi Peace Prize jury (2025, Q93)
- First Kho Kho World Cup (2025, Q99)
- 45th Chess Olympiad and records (2025, Q100)
- Laureus World Sports Awards (2021, Q98)
- Tokyo 2020 motto and new sports (2021, Q99)
- ICC World Test Championship finalist criteria (2021, Q100)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 38 §8.12).
**Links:** MOD-5178, MOD-5179.
**Trap:** Routing artefacts are not gaps in the history owner; they need a current-affairs source.
**Mains hook:** None; this is a classification card.

### Flashcard MOD-5181 — Synthesis engine: state capacity, from colonial inheritance to the market turn
**Front:** What is the owner's synthesis chain linking state capacity to the 1991 turn?
**Definition (learn):** The 20-mark synthesis chain: state capacity inherited from the colonial order was deployed for planning and heavy industry, not against rural property; technology substituted for reform; output was secured but rural inequality and ecological cost followed; caste and gender rights lagged; excluded groups organised; the model's inefficiency and the BoP crisis forced the market turn. (✅ owner 38 §8.8; ⚠️ causal framing)
**Breakdown:**
- Inherited state capacity (basic 29)
- Deployed for planning and heavy industry (achieved)
- Not deployed against rural property (tenancy and ceilings failed)
- Technology substituted for structural reform (Green Revolution)
- Excluded groups organise (Dalit Panthers, BSP, women's movement, Mandal)
- BoP crisis and market turn (1991)
- Verdict: transformed economy and politics far more than social structure
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 38 §8.8).
**Links:** MOD-5151, MOD-5158, MOD-5163, MOD-5171, MOD-5144, MOD-5168.
**Trap:** Do not present the chain as deterministic; it is an analytical synthesis.
**Mains hook:** "Assess the post-independence development record" with the synthesis verdict.
**Back:** *"Capacity deployed to factories not fields; excluded groups organise; crisis forces the market."*

### Flashcard MOD-5182 — Chronology ladder: economy, land, society (1948–1991)
**Front:** What is the chronology ladder for the economy, land and social strands?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's mini-timeline for the thematic synthesis, from the IPRs to liberalisation. (✅ owner 38 §10; ✅ owner 38 adv mini-timeline)
**Breakdown:**
- 1948: IPR; 1956: IPR (updated)
- 1950–51: Planning Commission; First Plan; Bhoodan (Pochampalli 1951)
- 1951: First Amendment; 1955: Fourth Amendment
- 1955–56: Avadi; Hindu Code Acts
- Mid-1960s: Green Revolution (HYV)
- 1956–66: Mahalanobis strategy; 1966 devaluation
- 1967: Naxalbari
- 1969: CPI(ML)
- 1980: Nasik farmers' movement; 1986: Sisauli
- 1990: Mandal
- 1991: liberalisation
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 38).
**Links:** MOD-5152, MOD-5156, MOD-5160, MOD-5163, MOD-5166, MOD-5167, MOD-5172, MOD-5138, MOD-5144.
**Trap:** Keep the 1966 devaluation (economic) and the 1966 Green Revolution start separate in your answer.
**Mains hook:** Use as the spine of a thematic synthesis.
**Back:** *"48 IPR, 51 Bhoodan, 55 Fourth, 56 Hindu Code, 67 Naxal, 80 Nasik, 86 Sisauli, 90 Mandal, 91 reform."*
